# CLAUDE.md — Project Intelligence for catalog-verifier

**Changes this session (2026-10-08d) — MPN Check: fixed a real GUDID root-detection bug (was truncating a 60-unit case down to 10); flagged an unresolved real-world discrepancy on the same MPN:**
- User: "mpn 66800834 is 10 eaches in a bx, and 10 bx in a case which means in total the ea case needs to be 100 but it shows only 10." Pulled the exact raw GUDID record for this MPN live to investigate rather than guess.
- **Confirmed a real bug, found exactly why**: the real record has **5 identifiers in two overlapping chains** (GUDID carries duplicate/legacy packaging chains for the same physical case here) — critically, one identifier's `package_type` is literally **`"Carton"`** (an INNER tier: qty 10, pointing straight to Primary) while the true OUTER case tier is a separate identifier (qty 6, wrapping that Carton). `_case_breakdown_from_identifiers`'s old root-finding logic picked whichever identifier's `package_type` string exactly matched a fixed word list (`{CASE,CS,CTN,CARTON,BOX}`) first in the API's (unordered) response array — here that happened to be the INNER Carton tier, not the true outer root, so the walk started one level too deep and silently truncated a real 6×10=**60**-unit case down to just **10**.
- **Fix**: root detection is now STRUCTURAL, not string-matched — a root is a `Package`-type identifier that is **never referenced as any other identifier's `unit_of_use_id`** (nothing nests above it), found by building the set of all referenced child ids first. When multiple structural roots exist (confirmed live: this MPN genuinely has 2, both correctly totaling 60), a root whose own label textually resembles case/carton/box is preferred as a deterministic tie-breaker only — never the sole detection method anymore.
- **Verified live**: `66800834` → `qty_case=6, qty_case_uom="Carton", ea_case=60` (was `10`); re-ran the full regression set (`CURCHSIL0753`→6/None/24, `STL2644`→100/"Each"/100, `1271`/Teleflex collision→all `None`/`matched=False`) — all still pass, confirming the structural-root fix doesn't regress the simpler single-chain cases.
- **Important, NOT resolved**: the corrected number (60) still does not match what the user described (10 each/box × 10 box/case = 100). This is now a genuine **data discrepancy between AccessGUDID's own registered packaging hierarchy and the user's real-world knowledge of this product** — GUDID's record for this exact MPN structurally says 6 Cartons/Case × 10 Each/Carton = 60, not 10×10. Possible explanations not yet investigated: GUDID's data for this specific MPN could itself be wrong/outdated (manufacturers do submit incorrect UDI data); the user's own case-count knowledge could reflect a different, visually-similar catalog number or packaging variant; or Smith & Nephew may have changed the real case pack since GUDID was last updated. Flagged plainly to the user rather than silently forcing the output to match their stated 100 without evidence — that would trade a real bug for a fabricated number.
- Scope: `services/mpn_check_gudid.py` (`_case_breakdown_from_identifiers` root-detection rewritten). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-08c) — MPN Check: split "Qty/Case" into two real columns (top-tier Qty/Case vs fully-expanded EA/Case):**
- User: "add the column for qty/case be like the highest uom (pack, box, bag, dozen etc) be the qty/case and then another column EA/case (the amount of eaches in a case)." Before this, `_case_qty_from_identifiers` (`services/mpn_check_gudid.py`) only ever returned ONE number — the fully multiplied-down total each-count (e.g. 24 for a case of 6 boxes × 4 each) — under the single name "qty_case", conflating what the user wants as two distinct concepts.
- **Investigated real GUDID packaging-hierarchy data live** (not assumed) before implementing, using the two documented reference MPNs: `CURCHSIL0753`/Medline (6 boxes/case × 4 each/box) and `STL2644`/Medline (single-tier: case → 100 primaries directly, no intermediate packaging). **Real finding that shaped the design**: GUDID's own `package_type` field is reliably populated only on the TOP (case) tier (e.g. `"CS"`) — the real CURCHSIL0753 record's intermediate "4" tier has `package_type: None`. So a reliable "Pack/Box/Bag/Dozen" text label frequently isn't available from the source data at all; rather than invent one, the UOM column is left blank in that case (honest "not labeled" over a guessed word) — except when the case's immediate child IS the Primary (base) unit itself (no intermediate tier at all), where `"Each"` is used, since that's a real fact read directly off GUDID's own `type` marker, not a guess.
- **Fix**: `_case_qty_from_identifiers` renamed to **`_case_breakdown_from_identifiers`**, now returns `{qty_case, qty_case_uom, ea_case}` — `qty_case`/`qty_case_uom` = the count+label at the FIRST (highest) sub-case tier only (6, `None` for CURCHSIL0753 since GUDID doesn't label it; 100, `"Each"` for STL2644 since there's no intermediate tier); `ea_case` = the same fully-multiplied-down total as before (24 / 100 respectively). `lookup_case_qty()` (`services/mpn_check_gudid.py`) and `check_mpns()` (`services/mpn_check.py`) both updated to carry all three fields through; every early-return ("not found"/"missing"/"lookup failed") dict shape now consistently includes all three (via a new `_NOT_FOUND` constant) so callers never have to special-case a missing key.
- **The separate AI web-search fallback** (`ai_lookup_qty_case`) only ever fills **`ea_case`** now (renamed from its old `qty_case` key) — it answers "how many individual units ship in one case" (the question it already asked), which is EA/Case's definition, not Qty/Case's; the AI has no reliable way to determine the intermediate sub-case tier breakdown from a web search the way the real packaging hierarchy data does, so it deliberately never fills `qty_case`/`qty_case_uom`. The frontend's "needs AI fallback" gate and merge logic (`static/app.js _runMpnAiFallback`) both switched from checking/writing `qty_case` to `ea_case` to match.
- **UI**: new "EA / Case" table column (`static/index.html`, right after Qty/Case); the Qty/Case cell now shows the count **with its UOM word inline** when GUDID supplies one (e.g. "100 Each"), plain number otherwise (e.g. "6"); CSV export gained `qty_case_uom` + `ea_case` columns; the Find-section help text now explains both numbers' distinct meaning.
- **Verified live against the real reference MPNs**: `CURCHSIL0753` → `qty_case=6, qty_case_uom=None, ea_case=24`; `STL2644` → `qty_case=100, qty_case_uom="Each", ea_case=100`; `1271`/Teleflex (the known cross-manufacturer collision) still correctly returns all three as `None` with `matched=False`; a blank pair still cleanly returns `matched=False` with all three `None`. Ran `check_mpns()` end-to-end (not just the lower-level GUDID function) to confirm the full pipeline carries all three fields correctly.
- Scope: `services/mpn_check_gudid.py` (`_case_breakdown_from_identifiers` replaces `_case_qty_from_identifiers`, `lookup_case_qty`, new `_NOT_FOUND`), `services/mpn_check.py` (`check_mpns`, `ai_lookup_qty_case`), `static/index.html` (table header + help text, `app.js?v=55`→`?v=56`), `static/app.js` (`_mpnRowHtml`, `_runMpnAiFallback`, CSV export). **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-10-08b) — Create SKUs: fixed a REAL cross-ASIN FBA-SKU collision bug (stale session-cached catalog index + Turba's catalog being an overlay, not a separate scope):**
- User, after a real export (`Bulk_FDD199_2026-10-08.xlsx`): "why the same FBA skus already appointed to a different asin are being used for a new asin?" Found the file on disk (`~/Downloads/Bulk_FDD199_2026-10-08.xlsx`) and cross-checked every exported FBA/FBM SKU against the real live Azure data (not the app's own filtered index — the RAW unfiltered pull) rather than guessing. Found **6 real conflicts**: e.g. `TORMS407W-FBATRB` was exported as a brand-new SKU for ASIN `B07PLFMG1W`, but that EXACT SKU already exists on SellerCloud, Active, assigned to a completely different ASIN (`B000QF81M8`).
- **Root cause #1 — a stale session-cached catalog index.** `/create-po/brands` (wizard Step 2, where the Company dropdown ALSO lives) eagerly fetches and permanently caches `sess['index']` using whatever `company` happens to be selected the FIRST time it's called (the default, "Ford Medical", before the user ever touches the dropdown). Changing the Company dropdown afterward (`case "company": S.company = v; render();` in `static/create_po.js`) only updates frontend state — it does **not** re-trigger `/brands`. So `/create-po/generate` and `/create-po/export` (and `/check-sku`) kept reusing that stale Ford-Medical-scoped index even after the user switched to "Turba" and its `-FBATRB`/`-FBMTRB` suffixes were correctly in use elsewhere. A Turba batch's OWN existing `-FBATRB` shadows (which live under `CompanyName='Turba'`, invisible to a Ford-Medical-scoped pull) were therefore never detected as already taken, so the collision-avoidance ladder (`_claim` in `derive_rows`) silently minted names that were already assigned elsewhere.
- **Fix #1**: new `_session_index(sess, company)` (`routers/create_po.py`) — tracks `sess['index_company']` alongside the cached index/mapping and transparently rebuilds via `_catalog_source` whenever the CURRENTLY-configured company differs from what's cached. Replaced all 4 ad-hoc `sess.get("index")` read/cache patterns (`/check-sku`, `/brands`, `/generate`, `/export`) with calls to this one helper.
- **Root cause #2 — even with a correctly-rebuilt index, a PURE Turba-scoped index would have just traded one bug for another.** Investigated the real structure directly: of Turba's 1,417 total rows, only **33** are genuinely standalone Turba-only "mains" — the vast majority are Amazon/Merchant LISTINGS (shadows) of products whose real MAIN SKU lives under `CompanyName='Ford Medical, LLC'`. So a pure Turba-only scope would make a Turba batch's own shared MAIN invisible (wrongly flagged "new") even though it correctly surfaces Turba's own shadows.
- **Fix #2**: new `_COMPANY_UNION = {"Turba": ("Ford Medical, LLC", "Turba")}` in `services/azure_sql.py` — `_rows_for_company("Turba")` now returns the UNION of both company scopes, so a Turba batch correctly sees both the shared parent main AND its own existing `-FBATRB`/`-FBMTRB` shadows. Ford Medical batches are completely unaffected (still scoped to their own company only, matching the original, still-correct design intent that a Turba-only brand stays "new" for a Ford Medical batch).
- **Verified live, end-to-end, against the real data that produced the bug**: confirmed `TORMS407W-FBATRB`/`-FBATRB2`/`-FBATRB3` are genuinely Active SellerCloud SKUs (different ASINs each); confirmed a pure Turba-scoped index made the shared main `TORMS407W` invisible while a pure Ford-Medical-scoped index made the 3 existing `-FBATRB` shadows invisible; confirmed the union index sees both; confirmed a brand-new ASIN for the same main under a Turba batch now correctly skips all 3 already-taken `-FBATRB` slots and climbs to `-FBATRB4` (was blindly re-minting `-FBATRB`); re-ran the full brand-prefix regression set (Torbot/Colgate/Dove/Cardinal/3M/Medline/Tresemme/Clorox/Skin Tac) — unaffected.
- **Not yet checked**: whether any OTHER already-exported file (before this fix) created real cross-ASIN FBA/FBM SKU duplicates that have already been imported into SellerCloud — if `Bulk_FDD199` (or any earlier Turba export) was already imported, those 6+ collisions may need manual cleanup on the SellerCloud side; flagged to the user, not resolved here.
- Scope: `routers/create_po.py` (`_session_index` added, 4 call sites updated), `services/azure_sql.py` (`_COMPANY_UNION`, `_rows_for_company`). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-08) — Create SKUs: corrected the previous entry's existing-main-title rule (plain shadow = identical to main; kit unwraps the UOM parenthetical):**
- Follow-on correction to 2026-10-07g, which got the actual rule wrong. User corrected it with a concrete example: main `"Torbot MS408W TacAway Adhesive Remover Wipes (Box of 50)"` → a plain (non-kit) shadow must be **byte-for-byte identical** to the main's own title, UOM parenthetical and all — NOT re-suffixed with `(Each)`. A **kit** instead unwraps the main's own trailing `(UOM)` parenthetical into plain text (parens removed, words kept) and appends the kit's own `(Pack of N)` after it: QY2 → `"...Wipes Box of 50 (Pack of 2)"`.
- **Fix** (`services/create_po.py` `product_name`): replaced `_strip_trailing_uom` (which always discarded the main's own UOM and always appended a fresh one, wrong for the plain-shadow case) with `_unwrap_trailing_uom(name) -> (base, inner)`. A plain shadow (`name_qty <= 1`) now returns the main's `existing_name` completely unchanged. A kit (`name_qty > 1`) unwraps `inner` into plain text and appends `({uom(name_qty)})`.
- **Verified live** against the real `TORMS407W` record (`"Torbot MS407W Skin Tac Adhesive Barrier Wipes (Box of 50)"`): a plain FBM child now matches the main's title EXACTLY (confirmed string-equal); a QY2 kit variant correctly produces `"...Wipes Box of 50 (Pack of 2)"`; re-confirmed the brand-new-main control case (`main_on_sc=False`) is unaffected.
- Scope: `services/create_po.py` (`product_name`; `_unwrap_trailing_uom` replaces `_strip_trailing_uom`). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07g) — Create SKUs: new FBA/FBM children of an already-existing main now reuse the main's real title instead of the vendor's:**
- User: "is it in the rules that if the main sku already exist and need to create only fba or fbm ... then take the title from the main sku but adding the uom thingy." Checked the code directly rather than assuming — it was **not** a rule: `product_name()` always built a child's ProductName from the vendor's own row data (`clean_name`/raw title), completely independent of whether the main already exists on SellerCloud. So a new FBA/FBM for an already-published main could end up with a totally different-looking title than its own main.
- **Fix** (`services/create_po.py` `product_name`): when `r.main_on_sc` is true and we're building a child's name (not the main), look up the existing main's real `ProductName` via `index.by_sku[r.main]`; new `_strip_trailing_uom()` strips the main's own trailing `(...)` UOM parenthetical (it always has one, since `product_name` itself always appends one) before re-appending the UOM this specific child needs (`Each` / `Pack of N` from the amz-pack, same as before). Falls through to the original vendor-title logic when the main's real name isn't available (e.g. no `index`) or for a brand-new main (`main_on_sc=False`), which is unaffected.
- **Verified live** against the real `TORMS407W` record (`ProductName = "Torbot MS407W Skin Tac Adhesive Barrier Wipes (Box of 50)"`): a new single FBM child correctly gets `"Torbot MS407W Skin Tac Adhesive Barrier Wipes (Each)"` and a kit variant gets `"...(Pack of 3)"` — in both cases a deliberately-wrong fake vendor title on the row was correctly ignored entirely; a control case (brand-new main, `main_on_sc=False`) confirmed the vendor-title path is unchanged.
- Scope: `services/create_po.py` (`product_name`, `_strip_trailing_uom` added). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07f) — Create SKUs: Disabled SKUs and non-Merchant "mains" were polluting existence lookups; pick the convention-following match when several candidates exist:**
- Direct follow-on to 2026-10-07e, where `FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1` was dismissed as "pre-existing, badly-named, but a correct match" for MPN `MS407W`. User pushed back hard, correctly, twice: (1) mains are ALWAYS `FulfilledBy=Merchant` in this catalog — that alone is a disqualifying tell, not just a naming-style quirk; (2) asked point-blank whether the record is even Active; (3) restated that any SKU we treat as a real reference match has to follow the naming convention.
- **Investigated live instead of repeating the prior session's framing.** Pulled the full raw row: `FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1` — `FulfilledBy=Amazon`, `ShadowOf=''`, `CostPerCase=0.0`. Queried `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS` on `[analytics].[sku_data_extended_view]` and found a real `Status` column the app had **never once pulled** (`Status` wasn't in `_COLS`/`_OPTIONAL_COLS` in `services/azure_sql.py` at all). Queried it directly: the view is `178,484 Active` / `7,075 Disabled` rows, and **`FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1` is `Status=Disabled`**. The real, active, correctly-named main for MPN `MS407W` already exists: **`TORMS407W`** (`Status=Active`, `FulfilledBy=Merchant`, prefix `TOR` + MPN `MS407W` — exactly the naming convention) sitting right alongside it in the same MPN group.
- **Three compounding gaps, all real, all fixed:**
  1. **`_is_shadow_sku()` only recognizes shadows by ProductID pattern** (`_QY{n}`, `-FBA`/`-FBM`, trailing `-C`) — it never looks at `FulfilledBy` at all, so an Amazon-fulfilled row whose name matches none of those patterns slips through and gets misclassified as a main. `CatalogIndex._mains()` now additionally requires `FulfilledBy == "Merchant"` (case-insensitive) whenever that field is populated — a hard rule per the user's own house convention, not a heuristic. Guarded to only apply when `FulfilledBy` is actually present, so the local snapshot path (which may not carry this field) is unaffected.
  2. **`Status` was never pulled from Azure at all.** Added `"Status"` to `services/azure_sql.py`'s `_COLS` and mapped it into the row dict. `build_from_rows` (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`) now drops any row whose `Status` is present and not `"Active"` **entirely** — not just from the main-candidate pool, but out of the whole index (`by_upc`/`by_mpn`/`by_asin`/`by_sku`/`all_skus`) — per the user's explicit "we only need active SKUs from SellerCloud." A Disabled SKU can no longer be matched as a main, reused as a shadow, or even block a collision check. Guarded identically (only filters when `Status` is actually present) so the snapshot path, which has no `Status` column, is unaffected.
  3. **Even after both of the above, a SECOND candidate survived for this exact MPN**: `TORMS407W.B2B-PARENT` (also `ShadowOf=''`, also Active, also Merchant-fulfilled, but not the conventionally-named SKU). `_main_exists()` returns a plain list and the caller just took `existing[0]` with zero validation — whichever row happened to come first in Azure's (unordered) result set would win. New `_pick_main(hits, base_main)` (`services/create_po.py`): when more than one candidate matches, prefer the one whose ProductID is an **exact** match to the conventionally-generated `base_main` (prefix + identifier) over any oddball variant; falls back to the first hit only when none of them follow the convention. Wired into `derive_rows`'s one call site.
  4. **Also noticed, not yet acted on**: the disabled row's own `BrandName` field says `"Torbot"`, while the REAL active `TORMS407W`'s `BrandName` says `"Skin Tac"` — a genuine SellerCloud data inconsistency across two rows for the identical MPN. This means a brand-filtered lookup (`_main_exists(..., brand="Torbot")`) still won't directly match `TORMS407W` by brand — but verified it doesn't matter in practice: the `base_main` fallback (`TOR` + `MS407W`) happens to reconstruct the exact same real SKU string, so `main_on_sc` still correctly lands on the real active record either way (confirmed live). Flagging the brand-field inconsistency itself as a SellerCloud data-quality issue, not something this tool can reconcile without more signal.
- **Verified live end-to-end against the real data** (not a synthetic fixture this time): `main_by_mpn('MS407W')` now returns exactly **one** candidate (`TORMS407W`) instead of three; `_pick_main` correctly prefers the convention-following name when a genuine ambiguity is constructed; a full `derive_rows()` run for a Torbot-brand row with MPN `MS407W` now correctly lands on `main=TORMS407W`, `main_on_sc=True` — the real record, not the disabled placeholder; `idx.sku_exists('FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1')` is now `False` (fully excluded from the index); re-ran the full brand-prefix regression set from 2026-10-07e (Torbot/Colgate/Dove/Cardinal/3M/Medline/Tresemme/Clorox/Skin Tac) — all still correct after this change.
- Scope: `services/azure_sql.py` (`_COLS` + row dict), `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` (`build_from_rows` row filter, `_mains()`), `services/create_po.py` (`_pick_main` added, one call site). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07e) — Create SKUs: removed `_main_from_asin` entirely (per explicit user correction to 2026-10-07d's fix); Azure-only live catalog (no local-snapshot fallback):**
- Follow-on to 2026-10-07d. User pushed back on that fix's whole approach after I explained it: "why are you checking for the main sku by the asin???" — their intended flow is much simpler and doesn't need any "recover the main from the ASIN's existing shadow" step at all: (1) look up the main by MPN/UPC, brand-filtered, following the normal naming rules; (2) if nothing's found, just mint a fresh main from prefix+identifier — never try to infer a different main from whatever shadow the ASIN happens to already have attached; (3) when a shadow/kit is then created for that ASIN and the EXISTING reused shadow's own stem doesn't match the freshly generated main, that's **already** correctly handled by the pre-existing `fba_mismatch`/`fbm_mismatch` flag (2026-09-10) — surfaced as a human-reviewed warning, not silently resolved by trusting the shadow.
- **`_main_from_asin()` deleted entirely** (`services/create_po.py`) along with its one call site in `derive_rows` — `main` is now simply `existing[0]` (brand-filtered UPC/MPN hit) when found, else always `base_main` (fresh mint), full stop. Also dropped the now-dead `_is_shadow_sku` import (its only caller was inside the deleted function).
- **Verified end-to-end with a synthetic CatalogIndex** reproducing the exact Torbot/Skin Tac `MS407` collision (no live Azure needed for this — pure logic check): when Torbot's own prefix differs from Skin Tac's, a fresh main mints cleanly (`main_on_sc=False`), the ASIN's existing Skin Tac `-FBA` shadow is still correctly REUSED (an ASIN can only have one live FBA listing), and `fba_mismatch` correctly fires ("stem 'TORMS407' doesn't match the generated Main SKU…") — exactly the flow described above.
- **Found and flagged (not fixed — needs a user decision), a real but separate pre-existing gap surfaced by that same test**: Torbot and Skin Tac's real SellerCloud SKUs **both genuinely use the "TOR" prefix** (confirmed in 2026-10-07d's own verification: Skin Tac's real main IS `TORMS407`). So when Torbot's OWN resolved prefix also happens to be "TOR" (plausible — Skin-Tac is commercially a Torbot adhesive-remover product line, likely sharing a manufacturer prefix even though the catalog's BrandName field distinguishes them), a freshly-minted Torbot main for MPN `MS407` produces the literal string `TORMS407` — identical to Skin Tac's already-existing SKU. `main_on_sc` (`index.sku_exists(main)`) is **not brand-aware**, so it comes back `True` purely from the string coincidence, wrongly suppressing creation of a genuine new Torbot main and masking the cross-brand situation (no `fba_mismatch` fires either, since the colliding string now trivially "matches" the reused shadow's stem too). This was already present after 2026-10-07d's fix (unchanged by today's removal — `_main_from_asin` returning "" on a brand mismatch already fell through to the same colliding `base_main`), just newly noticed. Not fixed because it needs a design call (e.g. should `main_on_sc` become brand-aware, or should a colliding fresh mint get some disambiguating suffix) rather than a unilateral change.
- Separately in this session: `routers/create_po.py` and `services/analytics/po_analytics.py` were switched to **Azure-only** live catalog lookups with **no local-snapshot fallback** (`_catalog_source` now raises a 503 / `gather()` raises a RuntimeError when Azure isn't configured or the pull fails, instead of silently degrading to the stale `data/sc_snapshot.json`) — per explicit user instruction, since that snapshot had repeatedly caused real bugs (56-day staleness, a persistently-Excel-locked source file). Removed the now-dead `local_index` import from `po_analytics.py` left over from that edit.
- Scope: `services/create_po.py` (`_main_from_asin` + its import removed, call site simplified), `routers/create_po.py`, `services/analytics/po_analytics.py`. **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07d) — Create SKUs: fixed cross-brand Main SKU recovery via `_main_from_asin`:**
- User screenshots: a Torbot batch produced two ASINs whose generated Main SKU looked garbled/unrelated (`TORTTMS407`, `TORSM407.10`), plus a separate question about a third ASIN showing "already on SellerCloud" (`FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1`) that the user knew was genuinely a real product.
- **Investigated all 3 live against the real Azure catalog, not guesswork**: `FBA195RLKL19.MISSING1` is confirmed a genuine, pre-existing SellerCloud main (MPN exactly `MS407W`) — badly named by whoever created it originally, but a real, correctly-matched product; "already on SellerCloud" was simply correct.
- **`MS407` (no "W") is a genuine cross-brand MPN collision in this catalog** — 7 distinct rows share that exact part number across two unrelated brands, **Torbot** and **SkinTac/Skin Tac**. For ASIN `B0026745BK` (brand "Torbot" in the upload), every existing shadow already attached to that ASIN in SellerCloud belongs to **Skin Tac** — a different brand entirely, confirmed via a live per-ASIN query. `_main_from_asin()` (`services/create_po.py`) recovers a Main SKU purely from whatever shadow/kit is already attached to an ASIN, with **no check that the recovered product's brand has anything to do with the row being processed** — so it silently adopted Skin Tac's own variant SKU (`TORMS407.10`) as if it were the Torbot row's main.
- **`TORTTMS407`** (a different ASIN, `B00CXAWALM`) turned out to have **zero** existing shadows attached at all — unrelated to the brand-collision bug; it's freshly minted from prefix+MPN, meaning the row's real MPN is most likely genuinely `TTMS407` (the catalog's own real Torbot SKUs — `TORTT410`, `TORBONTT411` — confirm "TT…" is a real, existing part-number pattern for this brand, not generated noise). Flagged this distinction honestly rather than claiming it was the same bug.
- **Fix**: `_main_from_asin()` now takes the row's own `brand` and skips any candidate whose `BrandName` doesn't match via `_brands_match` (the same helper `_main_exists` already uses for its own MPN filter — consistent convention, including a blank brand skipping the check rather than blocking, matching `_main_exists`'s existing behavior for that edge case). The one call site in `derive_rows` now passes `brand=bi.brand or brand`.
- **Verified live against the exact real data**: `B0026745BK` as "Torbot" now correctly returns no match (falls through to a fresh `base_main`) instead of `TORMS407.10`; the SAME lookup as "SkinTac" still correctly recovers its real Skin Tac main (`TORMS407`), proving the fix isn't overly strict and doesn't break legitimate same-brand recovery.
- **Not changed** (flagged, not fixed, lower confidence): this catalog also uses a **dot-numeric variant suffix** (`.10`, `.4`, `.2`, `.3`) as a real shadow/variant marker alongside the recognized `_QY{n}`/`-FBA`/`-FBM`/`-C` ones (confirmed: `TORMS407.10` has `ShadowOf=TORMS407`) — `_strip_to_main`'s regex doesn't recognize this pattern at all. Didn't touch it since it's shared by other logic too (the "reused shadow stem mismatch" warning) and I can't rule out a dotted suffix appearing in a genuine non-shadow model number elsewhere; the brand-check fix above already fully resolves the reported bug without needing this.
- Scope: `services/create_po.py` (`_main_from_asin` signature + its one call site). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07c) — Azure catalog mirror: hourly background refresh instead of a 30-min lazy TTL:**
- Follow-on to 2026-10-07b's live-Azure switch for PO Analytics. User: "why dont we jst constantly update it? ... have it run every once an hour" — the existing 30-min in-process cache (`services/azure_sql.py fetch_rows`) only refreshes WHEN something happens to call it again after the TTL expires; between on-demand PO Analytics/Create SKUs runs nothing was proactively refreshing it, which is exactly how the local xlsx snapshot (a different, older data path) sat 56 days stale earlier today with nobody noticing — the same silent-staleness risk existed here too, just bounded to 30 min instead of indefinitely.
- **New `azure_sql.start_scheduler()`/`stop_scheduler()`/`scheduler_status()`**, mirroring `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py`'s exact hourly-scheduler shape (daemon thread, `threading.Event`s for stop/running, `INTERVAL_SECONDS=3600`): force-refreshes `fetch_rows()` every hour regardless of demand, then also refreshes `sc_reference`'s brand-prefix/manufacturer tables from the SAME pull (`force=False` — reuses the rows just pulled instead of a 2nd full query). Best-effort: a failed pull is logged and retried next cycle, never crashes the thread.
- **`main.py`'s lifespan** now calls `azure_sql.start_scheduler()` in place of the one-shot startup-only warm thread it had before — the scheduler's own first loop iteration runs immediately (not after the first hour), so startup behavior is unchanged (still warms the cache right at boot) but now also recurs hourly afterward, so **any caller (PO Analytics, Create SKUs, sc_reference) almost always finds an already-warm (<1h) cache** instead of paying the ~165s pull cost inline.
- **Verified live**: started the scheduler directly, confirmed it connects to Azure and begins a real pull without crashing (`scheduler_status()["running_now"]` flips `True` during the pull, `scheduler_alive` stays `True`).
- **Honest ceiling, explained to the user**: this closes the gap between "the upstream Azure view updated" and "we noticed" — it does NOT change how fast Ford Medical's own Azure ETL itself reflects a change made directly in SellerCloud's UI, which is outside this codebase and unverified from here. An hourly refresh means a SellerCloud-side change is visible here within ~1 hour of whenever Azure's own mirror picks it up, not ~1 hour of the SellerCloud edit itself.
- Scope: `services/azure_sql.py` (scheduler added), `main.py` (one-shot warm thread replaced with the scheduler call). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07b) — PO Analytics: fixed CF highlight colour, 0 ratings count (whole-app Keepa bug), recurring missing FBA SKUs (switched to live Azure), DOG/generic exclusion, column widths:**
- User reported 5 things after the pixel-match pass landed, investigated each with live evidence before touching code:
- **(1) Conditional formatting showed red FONT only, no actual cell highlight.** Root cause: Excel's differential-format (dxf) fills store their real colour in **`bgColor`**, with `patternType` left unset — NOT `fgColor`, the way a normal cell `PatternFill` works (a well-known openpyxl/Excel-XML gotcha). The earlier template-build script's own inspection only ever checked `dxf.fill.fgColor.rgb` (always `00000000` — unset), so it silently concluded "no fill" and only replicated the font colour. Re-inspected the reference file properly (`dxf.fill.bgColor.rgb`) → `FFFFC7CE`, confirmed. Rebuilt the template's dxf as `DifferentialStyle(font=Font(color="FF9C0006"), fill=PatternFill(bgColor="FFFFC7CE"))` (note the full 8-char ARGB — a first attempt with a bare 6-char hex silently zero-padded the alpha channel to `00`, fully transparent, invisible; had to be explicit).
- **(2) Total Ratings Count was 0 for every single row.** Root cause: `services/keepa.py _call_product()` never passed Keepa's **`rating=1`** request parameter — without it, Keepa never populates the `RATING`(idx 16)/`COUNT_REVIEWS`(idx 17) stats slots **at all**, always returning `-1` ("no data"), even for products Keepa's own `hasReviews` flag confirms have thousands of real reviews. Verified live: the exact same ASIN returned `current[17]=-1` without the param and `current[17]=7414` with it added. **This was a latent bug across the WHOLE app** (both PO Analytics and the Offer Analytics export share `keepa.fetch_products()`), not something PO-Analytics-specific — fixed once in the shared function.
- **(3) FBA SKUs still missing for real SKUs (e.g. ANH140702) even after the snapshot-staleness fix from earlier today** — same root cause recurring on a different SKU, proving the 56-day-stale local snapshot (`data/sc_snapshot.json`) is an ongoing liability, not a one-off. Per the earlier "why not sync live" conversation (also this session): switched PO Analytics' Main-SKU lookup to **prefer the live Azure catalog mirror** (`azure_sql.catalog_index(company)`, the exact same live path Create SKUs already uses as its primary source) over the local snapshot, falling back to the snapshot only if Azure isn't configured or the pull fails. Verified live end-to-end: both `ANH950685` and `ANH140702` now correctly resolve their real FBA/FBM children through this exact code path (confirmed via `azure_sql.catalog_index("Ford Medical").children_for_main(...)`, the identical call `gather()` now makes). A full Azure pull measured at **165s uncached** (185,552 rows) this session, then cached in-process 30 min — a reasonable one-time cost for an on-demand, infrequent export like this.
- **(4) DOG (deactivated) / GENERIC ASINs were shown instead of removed** — example given, `B07C72FFSZ`, confirmed live as a real DOG listing (404 on catalog lookup). `enrich_asins()` already supported `check_generic=True` (built for the Offer Analytics export's own filters) but PO Analytics was calling it without that flag, so generic classification never ran at all. Now passes `check_generic=True` and **excludes** (not just flags) any row whose ASIN comes back `DOG` or `GENERIC`.
- **(5) FBA Fee column was invisible** (`hidden=True, width=0`) — traced directly to the reference file itself having it hidden (presumably the user's own working-copy declutter, never meant to be reproduced in an automated export); the earlier pixel-match pass faithfully-but-wrongly copied that literal setting. Made it visible + slimmed it, the other 3 fee columns (Referral/Storage/Prep & Out fee), and columns **I–M + O** (FBA total, PO qty, PO Asins, 30d sales, 90d sales, FBA) to width 9, per explicit request.
- **Verified all 5 together in one real export** (PO 49932, plus a synthetic DOG row injected specifically to prove exclusion): 9 real rows written (the injected DOG row correctly dropped, `max_row` 10 not 11), real non-zero rating counts (7414/613/395/341/141/939/613/0/23 — the one genuine `0` matches an ASIN Keepa's own `hasReviews:false` confirms has none), `FFFFC7CE` highlight fill on all 4 CF rules, FBA Fee visible at width 9.
- Scope: `services/analytics/po_analytics.py`, `services/keepa.py` (shared — affects Offer Analytics export too, in a strictly-additive way: more Keepa data populated, no existing field changed), `assets/po_analytics_template.xlsx` (regenerated, not git-tracked — copied directly to the live checkout the same way `offer_analytics_template.xlsx` already is). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-07) — PO Analytics: pixel-matched export formatting to the reference file; fixed a 56-day-stale catalog snapshot that was hiding real FBA SKUs:**
- User: "Could you please make the colours and formatting literally pin point same as to what I sent you, all the colours all the conditional formatting fir thge PO analytics." The export previously built its workbook from a blank `Workbook()` with made-up grey/bold headers and zero conditional formatting — nothing like the reference file (`49932 Blazy Susan.xlsx`).
- **New `assets/po_analytics_template.xlsx`**, built ONCE (one-off script, not committed to git — `*.xlsx` is gitignored, same as the existing `offer_analytics_template.xlsx` asset) by loading the reference file and copying its REAL per-column fills/fonts/number-formats/conditional-formatting rules — captured by header **NAME**, not column position, so it survives this module's own column reorder (PO qty/PO Asins after FBA total, per the 2026-10-06 instruction) — onto `po_analytics.py`'s `ANALYTICS_COLS` order. Row 1 = the real styled headers (theme-colored header blocks per logical group, exact same theme/tint values as the reference — verified these render correctly since the template is a real loaded workbook, not a fresh one, so its theme XML travels with it); row 2 is a blank per-column "style swatch" that `enrich_and_build()` now copies via a raw `cell._style = ws.cell(row=2,...)._style` assignment (cheap, not a slow per-attribute clone) onto every real data row, instead of hand-building styles/number-formats in Python.
- **Recovered things that were never there before**: the **FBA** column's yellow (`FFFFFF00`) manual-entry highlight (bold, centered — visually flags "fill this in yourself"), **Buy Box Price current**'s green (`FFA9D08E`) fill, and conditional formatting on **Net Margin** (<10% → red), **ROI** (<15% → red), **Rank (current)** (>100,000 → red) — all confirmed straight from the reference file's own `conditional_formatting` rules (read directly via openpyxl, not guessed). Correctly reproduced the reference's **combined** SKU+FBA SKU+UPC+ASIN duplicate-highlight range (ONE rule spanning all 4 columns together, not 4 separate per-column rules — matches the reference exactly) and deliberately dropped 2 stray single-header-row CF artifacts found in the source (`F1`-only "Approved" duplicate rule, an `X1`-only ROI rule at a stale 0.14 threshold) — both are leftover manual-editing noise in the user's own file (confirmed: the real, full-column ROI rule at 0.15 coexists with a vestigial header-only one at the old 0.14 threshold), not real formatting worth reproducing.
- **Also fixed**: the PO sheet's headers were wrongly bolded/grey-filled before (copied from the Offer-Analytics-export convention by habit) — the reference shows them completely plain (default Calibri, no fill, not bold) — now matched exactly.
- **Verified**: built a real export from PO 49932 and confirmed FBA/Buy-Box-current fills, Net Margin/ROI number formats, and all 4 conditional-formatting rules land at the correct columns; PO sheet headers confirmed plain/unstyled.
- **Separately, found and fixed a real data-staleness bug** while re-verifying against PO 50344: user reported **ANH950685** showing no FBA SKU despite `ANH950685_QY4-FBA` genuinely existing, and asked whether the lookup itself was inefficient. Traced it live: `children_for_main`'s regex/anchoring logic is correct and fast (confirmed ~4ms/lookup even at 200 line items in the 2026-10-06 verification — not an efficiency problem) — the real cause was that the **local snapshot** (`data/sc_snapshot.json`, which `local_index()` reads and PO Analytics/Create SKUs both depend on) was **56 days stale** (last built 2026-08-12), and genuinely did not contain this SKU at all. Confirmed via a targeted live Azure query (`WHERE ProductID LIKE 'ANH950685%'`, deliberately narrow — NOT the full ~180k-row `fetch_rows()` pull, which is what made the first investigation attempt take too long) that `ANH950685_QY4-FBA` is real and active (ASIN `B076M43KB1`) — and that the SOURCE file (`SC data.xlsx`) had itself been refreshed that same morning, meaning nothing was re-ingesting the fresher data into the snapshot at all.
- **Fix** (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` + `main.py`): new `start_scheduler()`/`stop_scheduler()` — a daemon thread (mirrors the existing Azure/health-check/MPN-sync scheduler pattern already in `main.py`'s lifespan) that checks the snapshot's age every 6h and calls `refresh_snapshot()` once it's older than 24h. Best-effort: a `PermissionError` (the source file open in Excel — confirmed live, it WAS locked during this very investigation, 2 separate manual refresh attempts both hit it) is caught and logged, retried next cycle, never crashes the thread. This closes a real gap — refreshing the snapshot was previously a fully manual, undocumented-in-practice step (`python -m services.sellercloud.catalog_index --snapshot`) that nobody had run in 56 days.
- **Not yet resolved**: the live refresh attempt is still blocked by the locked source file as of this session — the new scheduler will pick it up automatically (next check within 6h of the backend restart, or immediately on the NEXT restart) once the file is free; no manual action needed beyond that.
- Scope: `services/analytics/po_analytics.py` (template-driven rewrite), new `assets/po_analytics_template.xlsx` (binary asset, not git-tracked — copy manually to any other checkout the way `offer_analytics_template.xlsx` already is), `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` (`start_scheduler`/`stop_scheduler` added), `main.py` (scheduler wired into lifespan). **Needs a backend restart** (Python changes + the new scheduler only arms on startup) — the restart will also immediately re-check the snapshot's staleness, so if the source file happens to be free at that moment it refreshes right away.

**Changes this session (2026-10-06c) — Offer Analytics export: fixed AutoFilter stuck at column AI + restored Net Profit/Margin/ROI/Rank conditional formatting; 3 live Price Desk pair corrections:**
- User: "the output file hasnt changed a bit. Columns AJ - AS for some reason are not included into the filter. Net profit, Margin, and roi dont have conditional formatting."
- **Diagnosed by reading the actual template file (`assets/offer_analytics_template.xlsx`) directly, not guessing**: it's a 35-column (A-AI) sheet whose own `ws.auto_filter.ref = "A1:AI1"`. `build_template_xlsx` inserts 10 more columns via 3 `insert_cols()` calls to reach the real 45-column (A-AS) export — but openpyxl's `insert_cols` only shifts cell data/styles, it never widens `auto_filter.ref`, so the filter dropdowns silently stayed stuck at the template's original AI the whole time this export has existed.
- **Second bug, same root class**: the code unconditionally did `ws.conditional_formatting = ConditionalFormattingList()` (necessary — the template's own CF ranges reference pre-insertion column letters that no longer line up) but only ever rebuilt 2 of the template's rules (UPC/ASIN duplicate highlighting), silently discarding the real ones. Read the template's actual `conditional_formatting` rules directly to confirm exactly what was lost: **Net Margin < 10%** and **ROI < 15%** (both `cellIs lessThan`, confirmed via the template's own `rule.formula`) and **Rank (current) > 100,000** — all red-flag highlights. Net Profit itself never had a template rule (added one: **< $0**, consistent styling).
- **Fix** (`services/vendor_offers_template_export.py build_template_xlsx`): rebuilds all 4 financial/rank `cellIs` rules at their new post-insertion column letters via the existing `L()` helper (right alongside the existing UPC/ASIN duplicate-highlight block, same place in the code); explicitly re-sets `ws.auto_filter.ref` to the sheet's real final width (`A1:{last col}{last row}`) after the row-write loop instead of leaving the template's stale `A1:AI1` in place.
- **Verified**: built a real export from live vendor-offer data (5 rows) and confirmed `auto_filter.ref == "A1:AS5"` and `conditional_formatting` now has rules at **Z** (Net Profit, <0), **AA** (Net Margin, <0.1), **AB** (ROI, <0.15), **AG** (Rank current, >100000) alongside **A**/**E** (UPC/ASIN duplicates) — all at the CORRECT new column positions.
- **3 live Price Desk data corrections**, applied directly (not code changes): unpaired **UPC 60308411 / ASIN B01AUJ1NCE** and **UPC 36000566567 / ASIN B07BGLVLZ6** via the existing `vo.unassign_asin(asin)` (confirmed each ASIN's stored UPC matched what the user specified before deleting — `verified_cleared: 3` each, the usual barcode-form mirror cleanup); corrected the pack size for **UPC 84105811 / ASIN B082CX4R2R** from a stored **7** to **6** via `vo.assign_asin(upc, asin, 6)` (confirmed live it was actually 7 before overwriting, not a blind write).
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py` only (code fix). **Needs a backend restart** to apply to new exports. Price Desk corrections are live-DB writes, already in effect, no restart needed for those.

**Changes this session (2026-10-06b) — PO Analytics: fixed the panel freezing ("stuck on pulling SellerCloud PO data") when the user navigates away mid-job:**
- User: "why is the po analytics not working I added the po number but it just says pulling sellercloud po data" — gave PO **50344** (139 line items, much bigger than 49932's 7).
- **Diagnosed by reproducing the EXACT input directly against the user's own live running server** (not the worktree's) before touching any code: `POST /api/po-analytics/start {"po_numbers":"50344"}` on their real port-8000 process, then polled — the job correctly progressed `pulling SellerCloud PO data` → `eligibility check (4→139/139)` → `complete`, finishing in **well under a minute**. So the backend was never the problem for this PO.
- **Root cause (frontend-only)**: `switchView()` called `_clearPoaPoll()` whenever the user navigated OFF the `po-analytics` view — but a started job keeps running server-side regardless of which sidebar tab is open. If the user clicked to another tab (even briefly) right after starting a run and came back, the poll had been killed and never restarted, so the panel froze forever showing whatever phase it last displayed — which, for someone who glanced away right after clicking Run, is exactly the very first phase, "pulling SellerCloud PO data". The job itself had long since finished in the background; the UI just never found out.
- **Fix** (`static/app.js`): removed the view-leave `_clearPoaPoll()` call entirely — `_pollPoAnalytics`'s own `tick()` already stops scheduling itself once the job reaches `complete`/`error`, so there was nothing to leak by letting it keep polling in the background regardless of visible tab. Added `_poaActiveJobId` (tracks the last-started job) + extracted `_applyPoaStatus(j, jobId)` (shared by the tick loop and the new path) + `_refreshPoaStatusNow()` — called on re-entering the `po-analytics` view — so switching back immediately re-fetches and displays the job's current status instead of waiting for the next 1.5s tick.
- **Verified**: braces/parens of the new block balanced (78/78 parens, 34/34 braces); re-confirmed live against the real server that `app.js?v=55` is what it now serves (`_refreshPoaStatusNow`/`_poaActiveJobId` present in the served bytes).
- Scope: `static/app.js`, `static/index.html` (`?v=54`→`?v=55`). **No backend restart needed** — hard-refresh the browser tab. **Not yet handled** (out of scope for this fix, didn't come up): a full page reload while a job is still running loses `_poaActiveJobId` entirely (it's in-memory JS state) — the user would need to re-poll by eye via `GET /api/po-analytics/jobs/{id}` if they reload mid-run and lost the id; low priority since the job still completes and the file stays downloadable via that endpoint as long as the job hasn't hit the 1-hour TTL eviction.

**Changes this session (2026-10-06) — NEW: "PO Analytics" (Analytics tab) — analyzes a received SellerCloud PO's FBA/FBM shadow SKUs for profitability:**
- User: given a reference file the team already builds by hand (`49932 Blazy Susan.xlsx` — a real past PO's manual analytics, 4 sheets: Analytics/Keepa/RS/PO), asked for a tool where "User inputs po number/numbers -> app gets all the data -> user can export the final output." Explicitly deferred to a later phase: Monday.com/Azure-triggered automatic detection of a newly-received PO + auto-email to raiann@fordmed.com — out of scope for this build.
- **Fully reverse-engineered the reference file against a LIVE SellerCloud `get_purchase_order(49932)` call before writing any code** (not guessed): confirmed every Analytics-sheet column's real source, incl. the two formulas that matter most — `PO Asins = PO qty / amz pack` and the PO-sheet's `Adjusted Price = Unit Price / Qty per Case`, `Qty Received = Qty/Case × Qty Ordered(cases)`. **Product Cost = SellerCloud's `AdjustedPrice` field only** (confirmed via a clarifying question — not `AdjustedPrice + ExtraCostPerUnit`). **"FBA total"** (system-filled, same source as the existing Offer Analytics export) is a DIFFERENT column from the **"FBA"** column (manual-only — "the amount of qty the purchaser wants to send to FBA and replenish the sku," a human planning decision) — confirmed these aren't the same thing before building. The only 2 columns confirmed as deliberately manual-only: **"90d sales"** (no 90-day Sales & Traffic report exists yet) and **"FBA"**.
- **Filename / brand resolution (confirmed via a clarifying question — NOT `Purchase.Description`, which coincidentally matched in the one example)**: collect the distinct first-3-char SKU prefixes across every line item in the batch. Exactly one distinct prefix → resolve it to its real brand name via **the EXISTING Create-SKUs brand↔prefix memory** (`services/sc_reference.py`'s `sc_brand_prefix` table) — new reverse lookup `brand_for_prefix(prefix, company)` added (picks the brand with the most mains under that prefix when a prefix is shared, same as any other collision in that table). More than one distinct prefix (the batch spans multiple brands), or the single prefix isn't known yet → fall back to **the PO's vendor name** (new `SellerCloudClient.get_vendor(vendor_id)`, a one-line `GET /api/Vendors/{id}` mirroring `get_purchase_order`'s pattern — verified live, vendor 16469 → "Nature Fun LLC"). Verified both branches with synthetic multi-prefix/unknown-prefix inputs (correctly fall back to the vendor name in both cases), and the real single-prefix case (`BLS` → "Blazy Susan", matching the reference file's own name exactly).
- **New Main-SKU → FBA/FBM children lookup** (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` `CatalogIndex.children_for_main`) — the REVERSE of the only existing lookup (`shadows_for_asin`, ASIN→children). Anchored on an EXACT main-SKU prefix + a new `_CHILD_SUFFIX_RE` (optional `_QY{n}` kit tag, then `-FBA`/`-FBM`, optional `TRB` company code, optional climbing-collision digits) matched against the REMAINDER only — never a loose `startswith` on the whole SKU — so a different main that happens to share a numeric prefix (`ABC10` vs `ABC1`) can never be mistaken for a child of this one. Returns `{sku, channel, pack_qty (parsed from `_QY{n}`, else 1), asin}` per child. **Verified against the real PO's 7 Main SKUs**: 5 singles (1 ASIN variant each) + 2 with genuine dual pack-size variants (`BLS922624`→25-pack/5-pack ASINs, `BLS922297`→50-pack/5-pack ASINs) = exactly **9 distinct (Main SKU, ASIN) rows**, matching the reference file's own 9 data rows precisely.
- **New `services/analytics/po_analytics.py`**: `gather(po_numbers)` (fast phase — SellerCloud PO pull + local-snapshot SKU/ASIN resolution, no live Keepa/SP-API) then `enrich_and_build(gathered, on_progress)` (slow phase — reuses the EXISTING Offer Analytics machinery verbatim: `services.keepa.fetch_products` for Buy Box/fees/rank/BB-share/brand/category, `services.vendor_offers_template_export.enrich_asins` for live SP-API eligibility + storage fee, `services.vendor_offers_template_export._fba_total_by_asin` for FBA total, `services.spapi.reports.get_units_30d()` for 30d sales — same formulas for Net Profit/Net Margin/ROI/tiered Referral Fee as the Price Desk export, just a different, PO-specific column layout and row-expansion rule). One PO line item (one Main SKU) expands into one row per distinct ASIN among its pack-size variants; **"PO qty" is the line item's `QtyReceived`, IDENTICAL across all of that Main SKU's variant rows** (one shared received-stock pool, not pre-split by pack size) — confirmed exactly against the reference file's own values (e.g. both `BLS922624` rows show `PO qty=11250`). **Column order, per explicit instruction**: "PO qty"/"PO Asins" sit right after "FBA total" — deliberately DIFFERENT from the reference file's own layout (which has them after "Estimated Monthly Units Sold").
- **`routers/po_analytics.py`** (new, registered in `main.py`): `POST /po-analytics/start` (body `{po_numbers: "49932" or "49932, 49933"}`) → background-thread job (mirrors the existing Offer Analytics export job-polling pattern in `routers/vendor_offers.py`); `GET /po-analytics/jobs/{id}` (phase/progress/ETA/filename); `GET /po-analytics/jobs/{id}/download` (xlsx, 2 sheets — Analytics + PO, matching the reference file's own sheet scope, no raw Keepa/RS sheets).
- **UI**: new "PO Analytics" sidebar item (Analytics section, after Brand Analytics) + `#view-po-analytics` — a single PO-number(s) input, Run button, polling progress line (phase/done/total/ETA, same convention as every other job-polling panel in this app), Download button once complete.
- **Verified end-to-end, twice**: (1) direct Python call against the real PO 49932 — `gather()` produced the exact 9 rows with exact PO qty/Product Cost/amz pack/UPC values matching the hand-built reference file, and the resolved filename **`49932 Blazy Susan.xlsx`** matched the reference file's own name byte-for-byte; `enrich_and_build()` then produced a valid 2-sheet workbook with live Keepa Buy Box prices and live SP-API eligibility (all 9 ASINs `CAN_SELL`). (2) Full HTTP round-trip via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001, the user's own dev server on 8000 untouched) — `POST /start` → polled through "Keepa live prices" → "eligibility check" → `complete`, `GET /download` returned the real xlsx. A first smoke-test attempt surfaced 2 real environment gaps, both now defensively handled: `sc_reference`'s brand-prefix table may not exist yet on a fresh boot (it's built by a background Azure warm-up thread in `main.py`'s lifespan) — `_resolve_brand_label` now wraps the lookup in `ensure_tables()` + try/except and falls through to the vendor-name fallback instead of failing the whole run.
- Scope: `services/sellercloud/client.py` (`get_vendor` added), `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` (`_CHILD_SUFFIX_RE` + `children_for_main` added), `services/sc_reference.py` (`brand_for_prefix` added), `services/analytics/po_analytics.py` (new), `routers/po_analytics.py` (new), `main.py` (router registration), `static/index.html` (new nav item + view, `app.js?v=53`→`?v=54`), `static/app.js` (new PO Analytics JS block + viewMap entry). **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh. **Not yet built** (explicitly deferred by the user): Monday.com/Azure-triggered automatic PO-received detection and automatic email delivery — the current flow is manual PO-number entry only, per the user's own stated scope for this phase.

**Changes this session (2026-10-05) — Brand Analytics: Phase A keyword search's strict brand filter was dropping genuine reseller-relisted inventory:**
- User pasted 19 ASINs from the just-completed "Myco" manufacturer run (run 26, search terms incl. "Myco", "Glassvan", "Redibond", …) asking why they weren't found. 2/19 were already in the run; the other 17 genuinely weren't.
- **Root cause, confirmed live via SP-API `get_by_asin` on all 17**: every one is a real Myco/Glassvan surgical-blade product, but listed on Amazon by a **reseller** ("AmeriCan Goods") who put their own company name in Amazon's structured `brand` (and sometimes `manufacturer`) field instead of Myco/Glassvan. Two layers both miss this: (a) Keepa's structured brand/manufacturer-field discovery can't find them (Keepa mirrors the same wrong Amazon attribution); (b) Phase A's keyword search DOES surface them (the title literally contains "GLASSVAN"/"MYCO") but `_brand_matches(..., strict=True)` hard-rejects on sight the moment it sees a brand field that's set but doesn't match — added 2026-06-18 specifically to stop coincidental keyword collisions (e.g. "Ringers" keyword matching "Dead Ringers [DVD]", "Microflex" keyword matching "Shure Microflex MX415" mic).
- **No cheap heuristic cleanly tells these two cases apart** — structurally they're identical ("a real, different company name sits in the brand field, and a search term happens to appear in the title"); the only difference is domain knowledge (a human recognizes "AmeriCan Goods" as a reseller storefront, not a legitimate competing surgical-blade brand, the way "Shure" is a legitimate competing audio brand). Per explicit user instruction ("let's make it less strict"), **Phase A's single call site now uses `strict=False`** (`services/analytics/brand_runner.py` line ~829) — a brand-field mismatch now falls through to the title check instead of an immediate reject. **Verified live**: all 3 representative Myco/Glassvan cases now pass; re-tested the exact 3 historical false-positive examples (Shure/Microflex, Dead Ringers, Gillette/Edge) and confirmed they **also now pass** — this is a real, accepted precision-for-recall trade-off, not an oversight. Revisit if coincidental false positives start recurring in a future run.
- **Backfilled the 17 real Myco/Glassvan ASINs directly into the already-completed run 26** (`brand_analytics_items`, via `_upsert_item` — idempotent upsert, no run-level counters touched) using the SP-API data already pulled during the investigation — run 26 went from 1,678 → 1,695 items. This only fixes run 26 itself; the code fix is what makes future/other runs recover this class of listing automatically.
- Scope: `services/analytics/brand_runner.py` only (`_brand_matches` docstring + the one Phase A call site). Live data correction applied directly to run 26. **Needs a backend restart** for the code fix to apply to new/resumed runs.

**Changes this session (2026-10-02f) — Fixed Turba's eligibility seller-ID env var name:**
- Follow-on to 2026-10-02d (multi-store SP-API). User added Turba's seller ID to `.env` but still got the "no seller ID configured" 503. Root cause: the user named it `AMZ_SELLER_TRB_ID`, but `services/spapi/config.py`'s `_STORES["turba"]["seller_id"]` only checked for `AMZ_SELLER_ID_TRB`/`AMZ_CLIENT_TRB_SELLER_ID` — neither matched the actual name used.
- **Fix**: added `AMZ_SELLER_TRB_ID` as the first-checked alias.

- Scope: `services/spapi/config.py` only. **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-02e) — Brand Analytics sub-brand discovery: fixed noise brands (BND, PrimeMed, etc.) outranking the real seed brand:**
- User: "I was searching by safetec and it was outputting way different brands that arent in any way related or are competing. So for example for safetec - output BND and primemed." Investigated `keepa.discover_sub_brands` live, with full visibility into every intermediate sampling step (not just the final output), before touching any code.
- **Root cause, precisely confirmed**: the function samples ~500 products under the seed's manufacturer/brand fields and tallies which BRAND values show up. For "Safetec", the real brand was **507/526 = 96.4%** of the sample — every OTHER brand value (BND, PrimeMed, Medique, crbn, Quake Kits, Generic, etc.) appeared just **1-3 times out of 526** (≤0.6% share), clearly a handful of coincidental/mislabeled/bundled-listing samples, not real sub-brands. But the function took EVERY distinct brand value seen even once and looked up its GLOBAL Amazon-wide popularity (a cheap exact finder count) to rank the final output — rewarding exactly the wrong signal: a brand that merely happens to be big EVERYWHERE ELSE on Amazon (BND: 696 products company-wide, PrimeMed: 490, Medique: 395, crbn: 321) outranked the genuine seed brand (283) in the sorted list, even though its actual connection to Safetec was a single noisy sample hit.
- **Fix (`services/keepa.py discover_sub_brands`)**: track the total number of products actually sampled across all 3 collection passes, and require a candidate brand to clear a confidence floor — at least 3 raw sample hits AND at least 2% of all samples examined — BEFORE spending a token on its global popularity lookup at all. This also saves wasted Keepa tokens previously spent looking up noise candidates.
- **Verified live**: Safetec now returns ONLY `"Safetec"` (283, its real count) — every noise brand correctly excluded. Regression-checked against the known-good Acon case (this function's own original 2026-08-18e validation target): still correctly finds `FLOWFLEX` (173) and `ON/GO` (13) alongside Acon itself (62) — confirms the new confidence floor doesn't over-filter genuine real sub-brands with substantial sample presence.
- **Also corrected the 2 stale `brand_library` rows for "safetec"/"Safetec"** (ids 17, 18) directly in the live DB — the wizard's step 1→2 transition has a client-side shortcut (`static/app.js`) that loads `sub_brands` straight from the already-fetched library cache WITHOUT calling the backend `/discover` endpoint at all when a cached entry already exists, so without this data fix the user would still see the bad list until explicitly re-clicking "Discover Sub-brands" even after the code fix. **Not done**: a broader sweep of every OTHER `brand_library` entry for the same historical pollution — flagged to the user as optional, not performed unilaterally (every future `discover()` call self-heals via the Keepa re-run layered on top of any cached AI result, so this is a staleness issue only for entries whose `sub_brands` were cached before this fix and haven't been re-discovered since).
- Scope: `services/keepa.py` (`discover_sub_brands` only). Live DB data correction applied directly (`brand_library.sub_brands` for the 2 Safetec rows). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-02d) — Multi-store SP-API: Eligibility store switch (Priority Pharmacy / Turba) + double-throughput catalog search:**
- User: "in the eligiblity status, could you please add a button to switch between 2 stores - Priority Pharmacy and Turba... Also when searching for asins in any analysis when we search by upc/title/mpn - can we start using both the credentials... to get 2x the output." User added Turba's SP-API credentials to `.env` (`AMZ_CLIENT_TRB__CLIENT_ID`, `AMZ_CLIENT_TRB_SECRET`, `AMZ_CLIENT_TRB_REFRESH_TOKEN`) — confirmed live these are valid (LWA token fetch succeeds). Turba is a genuinely SEPARATE Amazon seller account with its own LWA app registration, not just a different seller_id on the same app, so this needed real multi-credential support.
- **`services/spapi/config.py`**: new `_STORES` registry (`default`=Priority Pharmacy, `turba`=Turba) with their own env-var name lists; `load_sp_api_credentials(store="default")` is now store-aware (unchanged behavior for every existing caller, since "default" reads the exact same env var names as before); new `list_stores()` for the UI (per store: configured? seller_id_configured? — split apart deliberately, see below).
- **Found and fixed the real bottleneck before wiring anything to use 2 stores**: `services/spapi/rate_limiter.py`'s `LIMITERS` was a single GLOBAL dict shared by every caller regardless of credential set (confirmed by grepping every reference across `catalog.py`/`restrictions.py`/`generic_check.py` first). A second store sharing the same buckets would still be throttled to the single-store rate combined — silently defeating "2x throughput" entirely while LOOKING like it worked. New `new_limiters()` factory builds a genuinely independent token-bucket set per store; `LIMITERS` stays as the default/shared instance for back-compat. `CatalogAPI`/`RestrictionsAPI` both gained a `limiters` constructor param (defaults to the shared `LIMITERS`, so no existing single-store caller is affected).
- **`services/spapi/client.py`**: `get_catalog_api(marketplace, store="default")` now store-aware (gives non-default stores their own `new_limiters()`). New **`MultiStoreCatalogAPI`** — round-robins `search_by_identifiers`/`search_by_keywords`/`search_by_brand_names`/`get_by_asin` across however many stores are actually configured (skips any that aren't, degrading to single-store automatically, raises only if NONE are configured). Catalog SEARCH is Amazon's public catalog data — identical results regardless of which seller account's credentials make the call — so round-robining doesn't change what's found, it just draws from two independent per-store rate-limit buckets instead of one. New `get_multi_store_catalog_api()` factory.
- **Eligibility is deliberately NOT round-robined** (unlike catalog search) — `services/restrictions.py get_restrictions_api(store="default")` is a plain per-store selector, since restrictions ARE genuinely seller-scoped data (Turba's eligibility is Turba's own account standing, not interchangeable with Priority Pharmacy's).
- **Wired in, scoped exactly to what was asked** (not touched: Brand Analytics' own SP-API enrichment calls, Generic Check, Storage Fees — those keep using the default store only): `services/analytics/runner.py _run_pipeline` (the Offer Analysis UPC/ItemID/Title search pipeline) and `routers/analytics.py analytics_quick_search` (Quick Search) both now call `get_multi_store_catalog_api()` instead of `get_catalog_api()` — confirmed via grep these were the ONLY call sites in each file, so no other feature's search behavior changed. `routers/eligibility.py`: `CheckBody` gained `store: str = "default"`; new `GET /eligibility/stores`; `start_check` loads store-specific credentials + `get_restrictions_api(store)`, and its existing "no seller ID" 503 now names which store is missing one. `static/index.html` gained a store seg-radio ("Priority Pharmacy" / "Turba") in the ASIN Check tab; `static/app.js _refreshEligStoreStatus()` fetches `/eligibility/stores` when the Tools panel opens and shows a note when Turba isn't fully ready; `_startToolsRun` sends the selected store on the eligibility POST only.
- **Important caveat surfaced to the user, not silently worked around**: Turba's credentials give it working CATALOG SEARCH right now (verified live), but Eligibility specifically ALSO needs Turba's own Amazon Seller/Merchant ID — confirmed live this is a hard Amazon-side requirement (tried a blank `sellerId` and tried reusing Priority Pharmacy's `seller_id` with Turba's own token; both correctly 400 `"Invalid 'sellerId' provided"`). That ID wasn't among the .env additions. The UI plainly says so ("Turba: no seller ID configured yet"), and the backend cleanly 503s (not a crash) if Turba is selected before that's added — add `AMZ_SELLER_ID_TRB` (or `AMZ_CLIENT_TRB_SELLER_ID`) once available from Seller Central and the switch works immediately with no further code change.
- **Verified live (not mocked)**: `GET /eligibility/stores` correctly reports `default`=fully ready, `turba`=configured but `seller_id_configured:false`; `get_multi_store_catalog_api()` built 2 real clients, a live `search_by_identifiers` call succeeded, and the round-robin was confirmed to actually alternate which client answers consecutive calls; `start_check(store="default")` ran a REAL `getListingsRestrictions` call end-to-end (`CAN_SELL` came back for a real ASIN); `start_check(store="turba")` correctly raised a clean 503 naming the missing seller ID; full app still imports cleanly (134 routes, +1 for the new endpoint); `app.js` braces balanced (2347/2347), `index.html` div tags balanced (896/896).
- Scope: `services/spapi/{rate_limiter,catalog,config,client,__init__}.py`, `services/restrictions.py`, `services/analytics/runner.py`, `routers/{analytics,eligibility}.py`, `static/{index.html,app.js}` (cache-bust bumped `?v=52`→`?v=53`). **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-10-02c) — Brand Analytics: Keepa discovery now always searches brand AND manufacturer, not either/or:**
- Follow-on to 2026-10-02b (the Keepa+SP-API combination). User: "when we do manufacturer/brand, can we always search on both each time... if i search for '3M' I would like it to search by the brand, manufacturer, and title, and also sp api."
- **Keepa half**: `_keepa_brand_pipeline` previously picked ONE Keepa finder field per the wizard's Brand/Manufacturer toggle (`kw = {"manufacturer": term} if search_type=="manufacturer" else {"brand": term}`). Now every search term is queried as BOTH a `brand` AND a `manufacturer` selection (two separate `find_asins()` calls per term, unioned — Keepa's finder ANDs multiple fields within ONE selection dict, so a single combined query would narrow results instead of broadening them, the opposite of what's wanted). The wizard's toggle still labels the run and drives the AI sub-brand discovery step's `entity_type`, it just no longer gates which Keepa field gets searched.
- **Investigated adding a Keepa `title` field too and deliberately did NOT add it** — live-tested `title="3M"` against Keepa's finder: **896,900 results vs 96,500 for `brand="3M"`** (~9x noisier), which would also cost proportionally more tokens recursively partitioning past Keepa's 10k-per-query cap. The SP-API keyword-search supplement (already always-on per 2026-10-02b) already covers the "title" angle properly, WITH a strict brand-field verification post-filter (`_brand_matches(strict=True)`) that a raw Keepa title search has no equivalent for — so this is genuinely covered already, just not via Keepa directly. Flagged the live numbers to the user rather than silently declining or silently adding something that would've been both expensive and noisy.
- **Bug caught before it shipped**: removing `search_type` from the Keepa-field dispatch left one stale f-string reference to it in a log line (`log.info(...search_type)`), which would have raised a `NameError` on the very next Keepa-configured run. Caught by re-grepping the whole file for every remaining `search_type` reference after the edit, not just eyeballing the diff — found and fixed before committing.
- **Verified**: full app still imports cleanly (133 routes) after both the field-loop change and the dangling-reference fix.
- Scope: `services/analytics/brand_runner.py` only (`_keepa_brand_pipeline`'s Keepa-discovery loop + module docstring). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-10-02b) — Brand Analytics: combines Keepa + SP-API discovery instead of either/or (was relying solely on Keepa when configured):**
- User asked "does our brand analysis solely rely on keepa?" — answered: Keepa is only the discovery step (finds the full ASIN list past SP-API's own keyword-search recall ceiling); enrichment (title/BSR/category/UPC/EAN/GTIN/MPN/dims→storage) always comes from SP-API's real Catalog Items API; AI Fill (separate, optional) uses Claude/GPT — but **discovery itself was either/or**: `_brand_pipeline` ran Keepa discovery+enrichment and `return`ed immediately when `KEEPA_API` was configured, only falling back to the full SP-API keyword/brandNames search (Phases A-D) when Keepa was absent.
- User then pasted 79 ASINs from a completed "Torbot 10.02.2026" run (`search_type=manufacturer`) asking why 14 weren't found. **Investigated live, found a real gap**: all 14 genuinely have `brand="Torbot"` on Amazon (confirmed via live SP-API `get_by_asin`), but Keepa's own `manufacturer` field on these specific listings is blank or inconsistent (`"Med-Choice"`, `"MedC"`, `""`, `"TORBOTGROUPINC.1231"` — a mashed-together variant with no space, matching none of the 22 manufacturer-name variants searched) — so a manufacturer-only Keepa search can't find them even though the correct brand field clearly would via an Amazon-side keyword/brandNames search.
- **Fix** (`services/analytics/brand_runner.py`): when Keepa is configured, it still runs FIRST (keeps its recall advantage over SP-API's own depth-capped keyword search), but `_brand_pipeline` no longer `return`s afterward — it falls through into the SAME SP-API keyword+brandNames search (Phases A-D) that used to be Keepa-absent-only, now always running as a supplement, not a fallback. Both sources write through the SAME `_upsert_item` (idempotent, `ON CONFLICT(run_id, asin) DO UPDATE`), so an ASIN either source finds is just refreshed with the other's data — never duplicated; confirmed `ai_mpn`/`ai_upc`/`ai_ean`/`ai_gtin` (AI Fill's own columns) aren't touched by this upsert at all, so neither pass can clobber AI Fill's work.
- `_keepa_brand_pipeline` gained `mark_complete: bool = True` — when the caller is about to also run the SP-API supplement, it's passed `False` so the run stays visibly "Searching" (with a phase message explaining the handoff: "Keepa done — N products · T tokens — supplementing via Amazon SP-API search…") instead of the UI showing Complete partway through. The final completion message now reports the TRUE combined total queried fresh from `brand_analytics_items` (not just the SP-API phase's own `asin_count` counter, which would understate the real total whenever Keepa ran first) alongside how many came specifically via the SP-API supplement.
- **Honest tradeoff, documented in the module docstring**: every run is now slower, since it always does the full SP-API keyword/brandNames/category search on top of Keepa discovery, not just when Keepa is unavailable.
- **Verified**: full app still imports cleanly (133 routes); both functions' signatures confirmed via introspection (the `mark_complete` kwarg wired correctly end to end); the investigation itself (live SP-API lookups on the 14 missing ASINs) is real evidence of the gap this closes, not a hypothetical.
- Scope: `services/analytics/brand_runner.py` only. **Needs a backend restart.** Existing completed runs are unaffected until re-run; a NEW run (or a future "re-run" of a stale cached one) will now use the combined discovery.

**Changes this session (2026-10-02) — Brand Analytics: fixed stale "Amazon brand names found" panel showing the previous run's brand on a new run:**
- User: "when I do a brand analytics for one brand, and then want to do a second run for a different brand the same sub brands show up for the previous brand." Investigated two candidate areas before touching anything: the wizard's "Discover sub-brands" step (confirmed clean — `openBAWizard()` already fully resets `wiz.discoveredBrands = []` on open, and both the server `/discover` endpoint and `services/analytics/brand_discovery.py`/`services/keepa.py discover_sub_brands` key everything off the typed `name` with no module-level cache) and the run-detail view's **"Amazon brand names found" panel** (the chip list showing Amazon's own structured brand-field values for a Complete run) — the real bug was here.
- **ROOT CAUSE**: `renderBARun()`'s brand-names panel gates its fetch behind `container.dataset.loaded` — a flag set directly on the `#ba-run-brand-names-list` DOM node, deliberately so it only fetches once per run rather than re-fetching on every 2s poll tick while a run is still searching. But this is a single-page app — that DOM node is never recreated, it persists across every run ever opened in the session. So the flag, once set to `"1"` the first time ANY Complete run was viewed, stayed `"1"` forever: every later run's `renderBARun()` saw `!container.dataset.loaded` as false and silently skipped fetching its own brand names, leaving the FIRST run's chips rendered on screen indefinitely no matter which run you opened after it.
- **Fix** (`static/app.js openBARun`, the single entry point every "open a run" path funnels through — run-list row click, the cache-choice modal, and right after submitting the wizard for a new run): clears `bnContainer.dataset.loaded` + its innerHTML, and hides the "N new" badge, every time a run is opened. Each run's `renderBARun()` now genuinely re-fetches `GET /runs/{id}/brand-names` for that specific run. The poll-tick dedup this flag exists for is unaffected — it's still set back to `"1"` immediately once a run's own fetch kicks off, so repeated polls of the SAME still-active run still correctly skip re-fetching.
- **Verified**: braces balanced in `app.js` after the edit (2337/2337, no regression to the pre-existing documented paren-count baseline offset elsewhere in this file); full app still imports cleanly (133 routes); confirmed all 3 real call sites of `openBARun()` funnel through the single fixed function, so every path into a run's detail view is covered.
- Scope: `static/app.js` (`openBARun`), `static/index.html` (cache-bust bumped `?v=51`→`?v=52`). **No backend restart needed** — hard-refresh the browser tab.

**Changes this session (2026-10-01) — Reverted the attribute-floor matching/vetting changes ("everything got worse"):**
- User: "bring back how the matching and vetting was happening before the logic has changed." Asked a clarifying AskUserQuestion first (how far back, and what specifically broke) since blindly reverting could re-introduce bugs already fixed along the way — user confirmed: revert back to **before the attribute-floor rules** (the 6-commit run `8bd501b..e3c5cb9` from the earlier "CPG/Medical confidence-scoring spec" work in this same session), broadly noticed on the PDI run.
- **Reverted** (restored byte-for-byte to the pre-cluster git blob, confirmed no other commit touched these files in between):

  - `services/analytics/ai_check.py` — removes the "find the TRUE per-unit count first" two-step pack/bundle rewrite in the AI-check system prompt (reverts to the earlier, simpler pack/bundle rule).
