- **Deliberately did NOT revert `services/analytics/runner.py`'s `_rescore_pipeline` review_status-skip guard** (also bundled into the same ASIN-exclusivity commit, "plus two bugs found along the way") — that fix stops a rescore from silently overwriting a human/AI's past approve/reject decision, which is a data-integrity guard, not matching/scoring logic, and un-fixing it would re-expose a confirmed, serious bug (13,054 already-inconsistent rows found across 12 runs before that fix). Flagged this exception clearly to the user rather than silently keeping it without saying so.
- **Verified**: both restored files are byte-identical to their pre-cluster git blobs (confirmed via direct file diff, not just trusting the extraction); grepped the whole codebase for any dangling reference to the removed fields/flags (`colour_confirmed`, `count_confirmed`, `all_attributes_agree`, `asin_exclusivity_capped`, `_true_unit_count`, `_MULTI_PACK_OF_RE`) — none found anywhere; full app still imports cleanly (133 routes); spot-checked `calculate_confidence()` on a real Kotex count-mismatch case — scores 29.0 (Not Approved) exactly as the pre-cluster logic did, with the new field names correctly absent from its returned scores dict.
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py`, `services/analytics/ai_check.py`, `routers/analytics.py`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (3 Python files changed) + browser hard-refresh. Existing runs scored under the now-removed logic keep their stored verdicts until rescored; a rescore will now apply this restored (pre-attribute-floor) logic again.

**Changes this session (2026-09-30d) — Global visual refresh: soft-shadow "floating card" look (Ringku/Octom Figma reference), light sidebar, rounder/pill buttons:**
- User: "make the app have the same visual features as this [Figma link, "Ringku - Financial Web App and Mobile App (Community)"]." The Figma link is a JS canvas SPA — `WebFetch` only ever got back a bare "Figma" page shell, no usable visual content. Asked the user how to get the reference; they chose screenshots and added 2 PNGs to `C:\Users\RaianNair\Desktop\vetter\files\` — these turned out to be of a DIFFERENT-but-similarly-styled dashboard labeled "OCTOM." (a project-management/file-manager UI), not literally "Ringku," but treated as the ground-truth reference since that's what was actually supplied.
- **Extracted design language from the screenshots**: vivid indigo/violet primary accent, soft lavender-grey page background, white cards with soft "floating" drop shadows, generously rounded corners everywhere (cards ~16-20px, buttons pill-shaped), a light/white icon-plus-label sidebar with a SOLID colored pill background on the active nav item (not a border/tint), big bold numbers in stat cards with small sparkline charts, avatar-badge stacks, donut/bar charts with a small color-coded legend.
- **Confirmed via AskUserQuestion**: whole-app-in-one-pass (not a single-view pilot first).
- **Implementation choice**: did this at the SHARED CSS DESIGN-TOKEN level in `static/styles.css` rather than hand-editing individual views — every view in this app already builds on the same `:root` variables + a common set of component classes (`.card`, `.stat-card`, `.btn*`, `.sidebar`/`.nav-item`, `.side-panel`, `.modal-shell`, `.wizard-shell`, `.cs-menu`, `.drop-zone*`, `.toggle`), so changing those propagates the new look everywhere automatically, with far less risk than touching hundreds of scattered inline `style="..."` attributes in `app.js`.
- **`:root` shadows restored**: `--shadow-card`/`--shadow-lg`/`--shadow-xl` had been explicitly set to `none` in an earlier session's deliberate flat-design pass, and were otherwise UNREFERENCED anywhere else in the file (dead variables) — restored to real soft multi-layer shadow values and wired them onto `.card`, `.stat-card`, `.side-panel`, `.modal-shell`, `.wizard-shell`, `.cs-menu`, `.btn-primary`, and `.toggle button.active` (none of these had a shadow declaration before this session at all).
- **Sidebar flipped from solid dark-purple to a light/white chrome**: `.sidebar` background `var(--purple-900)` → `#fff` + a right border; `.nav-item` text/icon color (icons use `stroke="currentColor"` so they follow the text color automatically) went from light-lavender-on-dark to dark-grey-on-white; `.nav-item.active` is now a SOLID indigo pill (`var(--purple-500)` + a soft shadow) replacing the old dark-purple-block-plus-inset-accent-bar; `.nav-item:hover` is now a soft indigo tint instead of a darker purple; `.section-label` darkened for legibility against the new light background. `static/index.html`'s hardcoded sidebar text (`text-white` brand wordmark, a `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` divider, a hardcoded blue-grey status-text hex) all needed a matching flip since they were HTML-inline, not CSS-variable-driven, and would have been illegible against the new light sidebar otherwise.
- **Border radius bumped up throughout** for a rounder/softer feel: `.card`/`.stat-card` 12→16px, `.btn` 8→10px, `.btn-sm` 7→9px, `.icon-btn` 8→10px, `.toggle` 8→10px (its buttons 6→8px), `.drop-zone` 10→14px, `.drop-zone-lg` 14→18px, `.modal-shell` 14→18px, `.wizard-shell` 16→20px, `.history-actions button` 6→8px. **`.btn-primary` specifically made fully pill-shaped** (`border-radius: 999px`) — the reference's single most visually distinctive button treatment — while smaller/dense in-table action buttons deliberately stay moderately rounded (not full pill), since this app's toolbars/tables are far denser than the reference's spacious lifestyle-dashboard layout and a sea of small full-pill buttons side-by-side reads as visually noisy rather than polished.
- **Deliberately NOT touched**: verdict/status semantic colors (`badge-verified/review/not`, every `status-*` class) — those encode real meaning (approved/rejected/pending), not just decoration, so recoloring them purely for aesthetic match risked confusing users reading actual data. Also not touched: the newer feature panels built with raw inline styles directly in `app.js` (Walmart Catalog, MPN Check, Brand Check inputs, per this session's own established pattern of building new panels that way) rather than these shared CSS classes — those won't pick up the new look automatically and will read slightly plainer/older than the rest of the app until a separate pass sweeps their inline styles too; flagged to the user as optional follow-up, not done in this pass.
- **Verified**: CSS brace count balanced (630 open / 630 close) both immediately after editing and as a final check; `index.html` `<div>`/`</div>` tag count balanced (892/892); full app (`main.py`) still imports cleanly with all 133 routes after both file edits. **NOT verified with an actual rendered screenshot** — no browser-automation tool is available in this session, so this was a structural/logical review only (careful reading of every touched selector + its call sites), not a pixel-level visual confirmation. Flagged directly to the user to hard-refresh and confirm the real rendered result looks right.
- Scope: `static/styles.css` (`:root` shadows, `.sidebar`/`.nav-item*`/`.brand-mark`/`.section-label`, `.card`/`.stat-card`, `.btn*`/`.icon-btn`/`.toggle*`, `.drop-zone*`, `.side-panel`, `.modal-shell`, `.wizard-shell`, `.cs-menu`, `.history-actions button`), `static/index.html` (sidebar brand-text color, divider color, bottom status-text color, `styles.css` cache-bust bumped `?v=11`→`?v=12`). **No backend restart needed** — hard-refresh the browser tab to pick up the new CSS/HTML.

**Changes this session (2026-09-30c) — Vendor Offers: closed the "no delete path" gap for a wrong Price Desk ASIN pairing:**

Investigated live before touching anything (per house practice): the pair existed in THREE places — `vo_asin_listings` (Price Desk manual assignment, `source='manual'`), and its `pair_library`/`pair_library_ids` mirror. A SEPARATE, genuinely-correct `verified_items` row for the same ASIN (from a real Offer Analysis SP-API match, `review_status='Manually Approved'`) showed B000WOU414's real UPC is `046500618512` — visibly different from the wrongly-assigned `046500018428` — confirming the user's report.
- **This exact gap was already flagged in the 2026-09-24 session** ("the Pair Manager's existing '✕ Remove' button only deletes from pair_library/pair_library_ids — it has never covered vo_asin_listings ... a manually-assigned Price Desk pair like this one currently has NO delete path anywhere in the app") — closed it properly instead of a one-off script: new `vo.unassign_asin(asin)` (`services/vendor_offers.py`) deletes the `vo_asin_listings` row for that ASIN, then calls the existing `database.delete_library_pair(asin)` to clean up the Pair Library mirror (and any `'Pair Library Import'` `verified_items` shadow rows it seeded) — reusing, not duplicating, the removal logic the Pair Manager already had. New `POST /api/vendor-offers/unassign` endpoint (`routers/vendor_offers.py`), body `{asin}`.
- **Applied directly against the live DB** to fix the reported pair right away (didn't wait for a restart): `removed_listing=True, deleted_ids=1, deleted_asin=1, verified_cleared=3`. Verified afterward: the wrong pair is gone; the 8 *other*, legitimately-different ASINs that also share UPC `046500018428` (different pack sizes, e.g. `B01CRAZBFO` pack 6) are untouched; the genuine `046500618512 → B000WOU414` verified pair survived unchanged.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers.py` (`unassign_asin` added), `routers/vendor_offers.py` (new endpoint + docstring). Data fix already applied live. **Needs a backend restart** only for the new `/unassign` endpoint to be reachable via the API — the actual pair removal took effect immediately (plain DB write, no server involvement needed).

**Changes this session (2026-09-30b) — Walmart Offer Analysis: confirmed the ROI/Net Profit formula was already correct, and fixed the real bug making results look wrong:**
- User: "the roi and net profit are not calculated correctly. So it should be walmart price - vendor cost - referral fee = net profit. and roi is net profit/vendor cost." Read `services/walmart/offer_analysis.py` first — it already implements exactly that (`other_fees` defaults to 0, so it collapses to the 3-term formula); live-computed a real example (price $27.49, cost $12 → net_profit $11.3665, roi 0.9472) confirming the arithmetic itself was never wrong.
- **Found the REAL bug by live-testing against the actual Walmart API** (credentials were already in `.env`): a genuine keyword search for "tide pods" returned 39 items, but only **16/39 carried a `price` field at all** — and the single HIGHEST-scoring title match (the one `best_match()` was picking) was one of the 23 that didn't. A UPC-identifier search always includes `price`; this is specific to keyword/title search. Since a price-less item is useless for Offer Analysis, this silently produced blank Net Profit/ROI for query-based rows even when a nearly-identical, price-bearing listing existed a few slots down — exactly what "not calculated correctly" would look like to the user, without the underlying formula ever being wrong.
- **Fix (`services/walmart/catalog.py best_match`)**: a candidate can now only be returned if it clears the title-similarity floor, doesn't conflict on size, AND actually carries a `price`. The single best-scoring candidate overall (regardless of price/size) is still tracked separately so "no match" reporting stays honest. Return shape changed from a plain `size_conflict: bool` to an `exclude_reason: str` (`""` / `"size"` / `"no_price"`) — the old boolean couldn't tell "wrong size" apart from "right product, Walmart just didn't give it a price," and would have mislabeled the second case as a size conflict. `routers/walmart.py` updated to compose a distinct, accurate note for each reason; response gained a `no_price` field alongside the existing `size_conflict` (kept, since the CSV export already has that column).
- **Verified**: 6 synthetic cases covering all 3 `exclude_reason` values, a genuine match, and an empty result set; a live re-run of "tide pods original 76 count" now correctly returns the $27.49 priced item (previously would have returned the priceless top text-match, worth 0 for Offer Analysis purposes). Full app import still clean (133 routes).
- Scope: `services/walmart/catalog.py` (`best_match`/`search_best` return shape), `routers/walmart.py` (unpacking + note composition, `no_price` field added). **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-30) — Offer Analytics export: matched formatting exactly to a real reference file (`Victory HL Analytics 7.13.2026.xlsx`):**
- User: "not all the formatting is copied... I added an example in the files folder in the vetter folder... please use that formatting so its the same as it is." Investigated by comparing the EXACT reference file (4 sheets: Analytics/Order/Keepa/offer) against the app's own bundled template (`assets/offer_analytics_template.xlsx`, same 4 sheet names — confirms common lineage) AND the live export code's `TEMPLATE_COLS` styling definitions, using an automated column-by-column diff script (not manual eyeballing) keyed on header NAME so column-position drift from years of `insert_cols` calls couldn't cause a false match.
- **Header-row color blocks (theme fills per logical group — cost/profit/rank/BB-share/Amazon% etc.) were ALREADY correct** — confirmed by literally reconstructing the export code's header-building steps (`insert_cols` + `_NEW_HEADER_COLS` styling) standalone and diffing against the reference: every existing column's fill/font/bold matched exactly. This ruled out the header row as the source of the complaint.
- **The real gaps were in DATA-ROW number formats and alignment**, found via the automated diff (11 concrete mismatches): **BB Price 30d/90d** and **FBA Fee** were using the accounting `_ACCT` currency format when the reference uses a plainer currency format (new `_CUR` constant, `"$"#,##0.00_);\("$"#,##0.00\)`); **Prep & Out fee** was force-formatted as currency when the reference leaves it as plain `General`; and **FBA Fee, Rank (current), Rank 30d, BB share 30d/90d, Amazon 30d %/90d %, Total Ratings Count** were all center-aligned in our export but are NOT centered in the reference. Also found (by direct cell inspection, not the automated script, which only checked numfmt/bold/center/fill) that the **Link column's `=HYPERLINK()` data cells were missing the standard blue/underlined hyperlink font** (theme10 color + single underline) that Excel's own hyperlink style uses and the reference file has explicitly set — a plain `=HYPERLINK()` formula does NOT get this styling automatically, so it needed an explicit `_HYPERLINK_FONT` applied.
- **Fix** (`services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`): new `_CUR` number-format constant; `TEMPLATE_COLS` entries for the 8 affected columns corrected (2 number-format changes, 8 center→False changes — some columns had both); new `_HYPERLINK_FONT` (Calibri 11, theme10, single underline) applied to the Link column's data cells in the row-write loop (`elif header == "Link": cell.font = _HYPERLINK_FONT`, alongside the existing `if bold: cell.font = _BOLD` — mutually exclusive since Link is never bold).
- **Verified**: re-ran the automated column-by-column diff after the fix — every column with a reference equivalent now shows "OK" (0 remaining mismatches); a direct write-test confirms the Link cell gets the theme10/underline font while an unrelated bold column (amz pack) is unaffected. Full-app import check clean (132 routes).
- **NOT changed — flagged as separate, structural (not formatting) differences the user didn't ask about**: the reference file has 3 columns our export doesn't (**"Expiry date"**, a separate **"qty avail"** distinct from qty/case, and a **"case Cost"** column feeding `Product Cost` via a `=case_cost/qty_per_case` formula — our export computes Product Cost directly server-side instead), and our export has 10 columns the reference predates (Offer Vendor, SKU, FBA SKU, OR33, On order, FBA total, 30d sales, Last cost, Vendor, Available qty — later SellerCloud-driven additions). None of these were added/removed/renamed — only visual styling of the EXISTING shared columns was corrected. Also not touched: the reference computes Buy Box Price via an `XLOOKUP` formula against an embedded "Keepa" data sheet inside the same workbook (a different, more manual architecture than our server-computed value) — out of scope for a formatting-only fix.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py` only (`_CUR`, `_HYPERLINK_FONT` added; 8 `TEMPLATE_COLS` entries corrected; row-write loop gained the Link-font branch). **Needs a backend restart** to take effect. Next up (per the user, not yet started): the SKU/FBA SKU columns are reportedly "exporting incorrectly" — a data-correctness issue, separate from this formatting fix.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29i) — AI Check prompt: the LLM was itself wrongly calling a genuine count difference "just a bundle variation" (a different bug from every earlier one today — this is the AI's own reasoning, not the mechanical matcher):**
- User reported two more Kotex mismatches: `"Kotex Ultra Thin Regular Pads with Wings, 36 Count"` matched to both `"Kotex Ultra Thin Pads, Wings, Regular, 100 Count, 2 Packs of 50"` and (separately) `"Kotex Ultra Thin Pads, Wings, Regular, 50 Count"`.
- **Diagnosed from the live stored data — this was NOT a mechanical-scorer bug.** Both candidates' `data_json.scores.confidence_score` were already correctly `29.0` / `count_mismatch: true` (the mechanical matcher, with today's earlier fixes, already gets these right). The outer `verdict` was `'verified'` because `review_status='ai-accepted'` — i.e. the separate AI Check pass (a real LLM call, `services/analytics/ai_check.py`) had explicitly OVERRIDDEN the correct mechanical rejection. Pulled the stored `ai_reasoning` directly: *"per-unit count 50 vs 36 differs but bundle difference only"* and *"count difference is bundle variation only"* — the AI's own words show it explicitly weighed the count difference and still misclassified it as mere repackaging.
- **Root cause**: the AI check's prompt already had a rule saying a genuine differing per-unit count (no bundle language) should reject (its own example: "80 Count wipes vs 110 Count wipes") — but that correct exception was buried after a much more heavily-emphasized "pack/bundle differences are NEVER a mismatch, this is the most common false reject" instruction, and the prompt gave no explicit guidance on HOW to resolve a title that states BOTH a bundle total and a "Packs of K" breakdown (e.g. "100 Count, 2 Packs of 50") to find the true per-unit number before comparing.
- **Fix** (`services/analytics/ai_check.py` `_SYSTEM` prompt): rewrote the pack/bundle rule as an explicit two-step test — **Step 1**: find the TRUE per-unit count (the smaller, more specific number in a "M Packs of K" construct; a bare "case of N" wholesale quantity isn't a per-unit count at all). **Step 2**: compare the TRUE per-unit numbers; approve only if they match. Added the exact reported cases as new negative (MUST REJECT) examples: `"36 Count" vs "50 Count"` (plain counts, no bundle language) and `"36 Count" vs "100 Count, 2 Packs of 50"` (true per-unit 50, still ≠ 36) — explicitly stating these must reject "regardless of how many pack/multiplier words are present."
- **Verified live** (a real `claude-sonnet-4-6` call, not a mock, using the exact stored source/candidate data for these two real ASINs): both now correctly return `ai_verdict: reject`, `ai_confidence: high`, with reasoning that explicitly resolves "2 Packs of 50" to the true per-unit 50 before comparing to the vendor's 36.
- **Not automatically fixed**: these two specific candidates already have `review_status='ai-accepted'` (a real, applied decision) and are NOT part of the run-118 verdict repair from 2026-09-29h (verdict already correctly matches that review_status — no contradiction to repair). The user needs to either manually reject them, or re-run AI Check + Apply AI Decisions on this run now that the prompt is fixed (which will re-evaluate every review_status='' candidate fresh, but will NOT touch already-decided ones, per the 2026-09-29e/h convention — clearing review_status first would be needed to force a genuine re-check on these two specific rows).
- Scope: `services/analytics/ai_check.py` only (`_SYSTEM` prompt text — no code logic changed). **No backend restart strictly needed for the prompt text to apply to the NEXT AI Check run** (Python still needs to reload the module either way, so restart to be safe) — this does NOT need a rescore (rescore is the mechanical matcher; this is the separate AI Check pass, triggered by its own button).

**Changes this session (2026-09-29h) — Analytics matcher: "N ea"/"N each" wasn't recognized as a count unit at all; historical-verdict repair applied to run 118 (Kotex):**
- User asked why `"Kotex Daily Thong Liners, 58 Count"` matched `"U by Kotex Barely There Thong Pantiliners 50 ea (Pack of 2)"`. Diagnosed against the live stored scores first: `count_mismatch=False` AND `count_confirmed=False` — meaning the count comparison never even ran (both functions bail out early when either side resolves to ≤1, i.e. "no count found"). Traced it: `_PACK_RE`/`_pack_count` had **no "ea"/"each" alternative at all** — after correctly stripping "(Pack of 2)" (a genuine pack multiplier), the remaining "50 ea" was completely invisible to the regex, so the TRUE per-unit count (50) was never extracted; only brand (Kotex, via the "U by Kotex" sub-brand collapsing to the parent brand field, same class of issue as "Liv by Kotex" from 2026-09-29g) + a middling ~52% title similarity landed it at 67.2% (Review) — not an auto-verify.
- **This particular candidate's `verdict='verified'` was NOT an automated bug** — `review_status='Reviewed'` shows a human manually clicked Approve on it after it landed in Review at 67.2%. Said so plainly rather than attributing it to a scoring floor that wasn't actually the cause here.
- **Fix**: added `\b(\d+)\s*[-\s]?ea(?:ch)?\b` to `_PACK_RE` (handles both "50 ea" and "50 each"). Verified: the stripped Amazon title now correctly resolves to 50; full `calculate_confidence` on the real pair drops to 29.0/count_mismatch=True (58 vs 50 ≈ 14%, outside the 10% tolerance) — a genuine mismatch that was previously invisible. Safety-checked the new pattern doesn't false-match inside ordinary words sharing the "ea" substring ("Tea Bags", "Sea Salt Scrub" both still correctly return no count) since `\b` anchoring requires a true word boundary immediately before "ea". Re-ran the full regression suite (now 10 cases, all passing) covering every fix from today's session.
- **Applied the historical-verdict repair to run 118 only** (per the user's explicit choice, scoped narrower than the full 12-run/13,054-row problem found on 2026-09-29e) — a real, live example of that exact problem blocking the user directly: they rescored the Kotex run and a reported bad match (`B0BG3HGFWW`) stayed in Approved because it already had `review_status='ai-rejected'` from before, and the rescore fix (2026-09-29e) correctly refuses to touch any row with a recorded decision — so the pre-existing wrong verdict was "protected" from ever being corrected. Backed up `catalog_verifier.db` first (to the job's scratch dir), dry-ran the repair to preview exact counts, then applied: **103 rows flipped `verified`/`review` → `not_approved`** (their own `review_status` already said `ai-rejected`/`Manually Rejected`), **18 rows flipped → `verified`** (already said `ai-accepted`/`Manually Approved`/`Reviewed`/`Pair Verified`) — run 118 counts went from Verified 17/Review 116/Not Approved 1265 to Verified 30/Review 5/Not Approved 1363. Confirmed the specific reported ASIN (`B0BG3HGFWW`) is now `not_approved`. **Deliberately did NOT re-run the same repair on the other 11 affected runs** — the user chose "just this run" explicitly; the other 11 (13,054 total rows minus these 121) remain untouched pending a separate decision.
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py` (`_PACK_RE`, one new alternative). Live data repair applied directly to `catalog_verifier.db` for run 118 only (not a code change — no restart needed for that part, but the matcher.py change **does need a backend restart** + a **rescore** on any run relying on count comparisons involving "ea"/"each" phrasing).

**Changes this session (2026-09-29g) — Analytics matcher: count-alone can no longer satisfy "all attributes agree" (another real false-positive found in the same Kotex run):**
- User asked why `"Kotex Bamboo Liners Extra Coverage, 54 Count"` matched `"Liv by Kotex Period & Pee Daily Panty Liners, Extra Coverage, 50 Count"` — two clearly different Kotex product lines (a bamboo liner vs. a bladder-leakage-focused "Period & Pee" liner).
- **Diagnosed directly from the live stored scores (run 118, "Kotex 9.28.2026")**: `brand_confirmed=True` (Amazon's structured brand field is just "Kotex" — the sub-brand "Liv by Kotex" only appears in the title text, not the brand attribute) + `count_confirmed=True` (54 vs 50 falls inside `_count_match`'s 10% tolerance, meant to forgive rounding/labeling artifacts) + `attrs_agreeing=1` (count was the ONLY attribute in play) → **Rule 3** ("brand + all attributes agree → 100%") fired with **zero title-similarity check** — real title similarity was only ~76%, but Rule 3 (unlike Rule 4) never looks at it.
- **Root problem**: count is a materially weaker "attribute" than colour or apparel size for this purpose — it's not a bounded, meaningful vocabulary (products cluster around common round numbers), and the existing 10% tolerance (there to forgive genuine rounding noise) makes near-neighbor counts trivially "agree" even across unrelated products. Colour/apparel-size were already tightened to require EXACT agreement (2026-09-29e's PediFix fix); count inherited none of that scrutiny.
- **Fix**: `all_attributes_agree` (`services/analytics/matcher.py`) now requires at least one of the STRONGER signals — `apparel_confirmed`, `size_confirmed` (volume/weight/linear), or `colour_confirmed` — to be true; `count_confirmed` can no longer solely satisfy it. This affects Rules 3, 4, and 6 (all built on `all_attributes_agree`). Rule 2 (UPC + at least one attribute) is UNCHANGED — count-only confirmation is still fine there, since a real UPC/EAN match is already the primary, near-definitive signal and the attribute is just corroborating.
- **Verified**: the exact reported pair now scores 78.0 → Review (was 100.0 → Verified) — an honest, human-reviewable outcome rather than a false auto-approval or an outright reject (no explicit contradiction was found, just insufficient confirmation). Re-ran the full 8-case regression suite (Rules 2/3/6 with colour+count, 3M model-confirmed, FUTURO apparel hard-reject, pack/bundle-approves, JOBST conflict, the earlier Pedifix count-mismatch case) — all still pass, confirming genuine colour/apparel/UPC-based confirmations are untouched.
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py` only (`all_attributes_agree` computation). **Needs a backend restart** + a **rescore** on the Kotex run (and any other CPG/medical run relying on the 2026-09-29d attribute floors) to apply.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29f) — Analytics matcher: fixed a "M Packs of K" pack-count bug (Kotex 9.28.2026 run) that let a false per-unit-count match through:**
- User: on the "Kotex 9.28.2026" run, `"Kotex BioCare Ultra Thin Heavy Pads with Wings, 72 Count"` (vendor) was matching `"Kotex BioCare Ultra Thin Pads, Wings, Heavy, 72 Count, 3 Packs of 24"` (Amazon) — user pointed out the Amazon title explicitly says "3 Packs of 24" (3 SEPARATE 24-count packs bundled), a materially different retail product from the vendor's single 72-count package, even though both titles happen to also state a "72 Count" total.
- **Root cause**: `_PACK_OF_RE`/`_MULTIPLIER_RE` (the regexes meant to prefer an explicit "…of N" pack phrasing over a bare count, per the existing "authoritative form wins" priority) only match the SINGULAR "pack of N" — neither pattern recognizes the plural **"Packs of N"**, so "3 Packs of 24" was invisible to `_pack_count()` entirely. It fell through to the first BARE count number found scanning left-to-right in the title — "72 Count" (which appears before "3 Packs of 24" in the string) — so the matcher compared vendor 72 vs Amazon 72 and saw no count mismatch, completely missing that "72" on the Amazon side is just 3×24 restated as a marketing headline, not an independent per-unit fact.
- **Fix**: new `_MULTI_PACK_OF_RE` (`\b(\d+)\s+packs?\s+of\s+(\d+)\b`) + `_true_unit_count()` (`services/analytics/matcher.py`, right after `_pack_count`) — a NEW, more specific pattern requiring a LEADING multiplier digit before "packs" (distinct from the existing bare "pack of N", where the single number is itself ambiguous whether it's a multiplier or a per-unit count). When "M Packs of K" is found, K is always the true per-unit count, taking priority over any other bare count elsewhere in the same title. `_unit_count_mismatch`/`_count_match` now call `_true_unit_count` instead of `_pack_count` (same pack-multiplier-stripping step beforehand, unchanged).
- **Verified against the exact real titles**: `_true_unit_count` now correctly returns 72 (vendor) vs 24 (Amazon's real per-pack size) instead of 72 vs 72; `_unit_count_mismatch` now correctly fires (was False); full `calculate_confidence` drops to 29.0/not_approved (was auto-verified). Plus 4 regression controls: a genuine same-count match with no "packs of" construct still matches; a genuine count mismatch with no "packs of" construct anywhere still hard-rejects (unaffected by the new logic); the ORIGINAL Tiger-Balm-style case this stripping logic was built for (a bare "Pack of 2" pure multiplier over an independently-stated "4 CT") still correctly resolves as a pack difference, not a count mismatch (the new `_MULTI_PACK_OF_RE` requires a LEADING digit before "packs", so it doesn't fire on that case at all — confirmed by design, not just by accident); a "3 Packs of 24" with no competing bare count anywhere still resolves to 24 directly. Also re-ran the full 8-case regression suite from 2026-09-29d/e (new attribute-based floors, apparel/colour exact-match fix, JOBST conflict, pack/bundle-approves rule) — all still pass.
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py` only (`_MULTI_PACK_OF_RE`, `_true_unit_count` added; `_unit_count_mismatch`/`_count_match` call sites updated). **Needs a backend restart** + a **rescore** on the Kotex run (and any other run with this "N Count, M Packs of K" title pattern) to apply.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29e) — ASIN exclusivity for 100%-confidence matches (user's dedup ask), PLUS two bugs found investigating it (one caused by 2026-09-29d's own new matcher rules, one much older and much bigger):**
- User: "if you see the pedifix sales 52 week analytics, we need to find a way to dedup the asins, if there is a 100% correct asin for an mpn it cant be assigned for another mpn." Investigated run 119 ("Pedifix Sales 52 Week") directly against the live DB (read-only queries) before writing any code, per this project's established practice.
- **Bug #1 (caused by 2026-09-29d's own new matcher rules, found immediately): `_apparel_size_match`/`_colour_match` were too lenient for a POSITIVE confirmation floor.** They copied the "not disjoint" leniency from their `_mismatch` counterparts (correct for a reject-check: don't reject on ambiguous overlap) but that same leniency is unsafe for a confirm-check. Confirmed live: 6 CLEARLY DIFFERENT PediFix products ("Ball of Foot Cushion Left", "Toe Caps", "Ball of Foot Cushion Right", "GelStep Heel Pads Small", "GelStep Heel Pads Medium", "Toe Protector") all verified at 100% against the SAME ASIN, because a vendor's single size (e.g. "Medium") is "not disjoint" from an Amazon size-RANGE attribute like "S/M" (one ASIN covering a Small/Medium range) — every specific single size a vendor names is trivially "not disjoint" from any range that includes it. **Fix**: both `_colour_match` and `_apparel_size_match` (`services/analytics/matcher.py`) now require EXACT set equality (`src == amz`) instead of `not isdisjoint`. Verified against the exact real title pair that caused it: confidence dropped from 100.0 to 61.0; a genuine exact-size-match control case still confirms correctly; the full 12-case regression suite from 2026-09-29d still passes unchanged.
- **New ASIN-exclusivity rule (the actual ask)**, `routers/analytics.py get_run_detail`: repurposed the existing (previously disabled/inert) `_to_conflict_cap` mechanism with a narrower rule than the blanket one reverted on 2026-06-08. A confidence of exactly **100** only ever comes from the matcher's "definitive" floors (UPC+brand, UPC+attribute, brand+all-attributes) — a much stronger claim than the generic ≥90 auto-approve threshold the 2026-06-08 rule protects. When one catalog row hits a definitive 100 for an ASIN, every OTHER row matched to that SAME ASIN (any confidence, verified or not) is soft-capped to Review — never hard-rejected/deleted, so a human reassigns it. Ties among several genuine 100%-scorers for one ASIN are broken deterministically (highest stored title-similarity score, then lowest row_idx) rather than left unresolved.
- **Bug #2 (much older, much bigger, found while validating the above): `_rescore_pipeline` (`services/analytics/runner.py`) was silently overwriting `verdict` for EVERY candidate on every rescore, with ZERO regard for `review_status`** — i.e. a human's "Manually Rejected," an applied "ai-rejected" AI decision, a Pair Manager blacklist entry, etc. all got blown away and replaced with a fresh raw verdict from the recomputed confidence score alone. `get_run_detail` already treats a non-empty `review_status` as sacrosanct in three separate places (auto-promotion guards, the AI-apply endpoint) — rescore was the one path that never did. **Confirmed via a live read-only scan across the WHOLE database: 13,054 candidate rows across 12 runs currently have a verdict that contradicts their own recorded review_status** — run 95 alone has 11,435 rows marked `review_status='ai-rejected'`/`'Manually Rejected'` (a real, deliberate rejection) that are NOT currently verdict `not_approved`. This is a genuine, live data-integrity problem affecting real approve/reject decisions, not just Pedifix's ASIN duplication. **Fix**: `_rescore_pipeline` now `SELECT`s `review_status` and skips (leaves completely untouched — confidence, verdict, data_json, sales_rank) any candidate where it's non-empty, mirroring `get_run_detail`'s own convention exactly.
- **Explicitly did NOT run a bulk repair on the 13,054 already-inconsistent historical rows** — fixing the pipeline stops NEW corruption; whether/how to repair EXISTING corruption (a bulk UPDATE setting verdict to match each row's own review_status) is the kind of significant, cross-run historical data change that needs the user's explicit direction, not a unilateral call, especially at this scale and given run 95's real business impact. Flagged clearly to the user; awaiting their decision.
- **Verified all three fixes together end-to-end against a disposable scratch COPY of the live database** (never the live file itself — copied into the isolated worktree, whose `services/database.py DB_PATH` resolves locally, so every test ran against copies only): (1) the exact `apparel_size_match` fix confirmed against the real reported title pair; (2) `_rescore_pipeline` called directly on the Pedifix copy — confirmed a `review_status='ai-rejected'` row was left completely untouched (proving the fix works) while non-decided rows still got the benefit of the new, fixed matcher; (3) `get_run_detail` called twice in a row on the copy to prove the new exclusivity logic is idempotent (0 additional rows capped on the second call) — an EARLIER version of this fix was NOT idempotent (it kept oscillating because the pre-existing "confidence≥90 auto-promotes" block was immediately reversing the new cap within the same request) until the cap was also given its own `review_status='ASIN Exclusivity'` marker, exactly the same self-protection pattern `ai-accepted`/`Manually Rejected` already use elsewhere in this file.
- **UI**: `_verdictReason()` (`static/app.js`) gained a check for the new `asin_exclusivity_capped` flag → "ASIN already claimed by a 100% match on another row."
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py` (`_colour_match`, `_apparel_size_match`), `routers/analytics.py` (`get_run_detail`'s conflict-cap block), `services/analytics/runner.py` (`_rescore_pipeline`), `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (Python changed in 3 files) + browser hard-refresh. For the Pedifix run itself (and the other 11 affected runs) to see the full combined benefit, a **rescore** is required — and per the bug found, rescoring will now correctly leave every already-decided candidate exactly as a human/AI left it, only re-evaluating the rest with the fixed matcher.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29d) — Analytics matcher: new attribute-based confidence floors (user-specified CPG/Medical scoring spec), layered on top of the existing engine:**
- User pasted a new confidence-tier spec for "Verifying CPG" and "Verifying Medical" (7 rules combining brand/UPC-EAN/MPN with "attributes" — size, colour, volume, count — and fuzzy title thresholds). Confirmed via a clarifying question this targets the **existing Amazon Offer Analysis matcher** (`services/analytics/matcher.py`), not the new Walmart work — the spec's own "AMZ title" wording gave it away. Presented a full mapping plan (reconciling their 4 "attributes" onto this codebase's existing `_apparel_size_mismatch`=size, `_size_mismatch`=volume, `_color_mismatch`=colour, `_unit_count_mismatch`=count checks) before writing any code; user confirmed "yes."
- **New positive "attribute confirmed" helpers** (`services/analytics/matcher.py`, placed near `_has_vol_or_weight`): `_colour_match`, `_count_match`, `_apparel_size_match` — each the exact positive counterpart of an existing `*_mismatch` check (same colour-source precedence, same pack-multiplier stripping, same garment-size normalization), returning True on AGREEMENT rather than contradiction. The 4th attribute, "volume," reuses the already-existing `_size_match`/`size_confirmed` — no new function needed there.
- **Interpretation call, flagged rather than silently assumed**: "ALL attributes agree" could mean either "all 4 attributes are literally present and matching" (would almost never fire — most products don't carry an apparel size AND a volume AND a colour AND a count simultaneously) or "of the attributes actually stated on both sides, none contradict and at least one confirms." Went with the second, practical reading: `all_attributes_agree = (at least 1 of the 4 positively confirms) AND (none of the 4 contradicts)`.
- **New floors added in `calculate_confidence`** (all via idempotent `total = max(total, X)`, so they layer on top of — never replace — the existing brand/UPC/model-confirmed floors already there): Rule 2 UPC+≥1 attribute→100; Rule 3 brand+all-attributes→100; Rule 4 all-attributes+fuzzy≥67%→90; Rule 5 fuzzy≥90% alone→90; Rule 6 all-attributes alone→90; Rule 7 (**medical only**) vendor MPN found anywhere in Amazon title/bullets/description (broader than the existing `mpn_field_exact`, which only checks the structured SP-API MPN field) → 85, or 95 with ≥1 attribute also agreeing.
- **Found and flagged (not silently fixed away) an overlap in the user's own spec**: Rule 4 ("all attributes + fuzzy≥67%→90") is a strict subset of Rule 6 ("all attributes alone→90", no fuzzy requirement) — so Rule 4 can never fire on its own; Rule 6 already covers every case it would. Implemented both anyway (so the code traces 1:1 to the spec's numbered rules) with an inline comment explaining the redundancy, rather than unilaterally deleting a rule the user explicitly asked for.
- **Critical invariant preserved and explicitly regression-tested**: every existing hard-reject cap (size/gender/colour/count/scent/shade/apparel-size mismatch → 29, forcing Not Approved) still runs AFTER and OVERRIDES all of the above, exactly like every pre-existing floor in this function — a genuine contradiction (e.g. UPC match + an attribute agreeing, but a real scent conflict) still correctly rejects, it doesn't silently auto-verify through a new floor.
- **Refactor note**: `size_mismatch`, `color_mismatch`, `count_mismatch`, and `apparel_size_mismatch` are now computed EARLIER in the function (right after `size_confirmed`, alongside the new positive counterparts) instead of at their original later position, so the new floor logic can see them without duplicating the extraction logic — their `total = min(total, 29.0)` application stays at the exact same later position in the function, so this is a pure reordering of *computation*, not a behavior change to any existing check.
- **Verified with a 12-case test script** (not committed, thrown away after use) covering: all 6 new rule numbers individually (Rule 4 verified via Rule 6's identical outcome, per the redundancy above) + Rule 7's 85%/95% split; 2 critical-invariant checks proving a real hard-reject (scent, apparel-size) still overrides a brand-new floor that would otherwise have floored at 100; and 4 regression checks re-running EXACT historical bug-fix scenarios from this file's own history (3M "1530-1" model-confirmed match, JOBST item-id/MPN-conflict-stays-Review, FUTURO X-Large-vs-Medium apparel hard reject, and the pack-of-144-vs-3-Count "pack difference still approves" rule) — all 12/12 passed, confirming no regression to previously-fixed matcher behavior. Also re-ran the full-app import check (132 routes, no import errors).
- **Return dict gained 5 new keys** for transparency/debugging: `colour_confirmed`, `count_confirmed`, `apparel_confirmed`, `attrs_agreeing` (0-4 count), `all_attributes_agree`.
- Scope: `services/analytics/matcher.py` only (`calculate_confidence`, 3 new helper functions). This is the SAME scorer used by BOTH new analytics runs AND `rescore` (`services/analytics/runner.py` calls the same function for both), so **existing runs will need a rescore** to pick up these new floors, and **new runs need a backend restart** to load the change. Not yet applied to `score_amazon_item`'s own simplified hard-reject list (used by a different/older code path than the main analytics run pipeline) — flag if that path also needs the same treatment.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29c) — Walmart Offer Analysis: title search now also rejects a right-product-wrong-size match (reuses the Amazon size parser):**
- User reported a second, distinct real bug immediately after 2026-09-29b's fuzzy-text-match fix: searching `"L'Oreal Paris Eye Makeup Remover 0.4 fluid ounces"` matched `"L'Oreal Paris Clean Artiste Eye Makeup Remover Oil Free, for Adults, 4 fl oz"` — a real L'Oreal eye-makeup-remover, right product line, but **10x the requested size** (0.4 vs 4 fl oz). Pure text fuzzy-matching (`rapidfuzz.fuzz.token_set_ratio`) can't catch this: nearly every word overlaps between the two titles, so the score is high (~79-85%) regardless of the one differing number.
- **Fix reuses `_size_mismatch()` from `services/analytics/matcher.py` verbatim** (the exact function already solving this for Amazon — parses volume/weight/linear-dimension tokens from free text, 10% tolerance, handles per-unit-vs-pack division; it's pure text processing, no Amazon-specific data needed, so it imports cleanly into `services/walmart/catalog.py`). `best_match()` now excludes any candidate where `_size_mismatch(query, candidate_title)` is True from ever becoming the accepted match, even if it has the single highest text-similarity score.
- **`best_match()`/`search_best()` now return a 3-tuple** `(item_or_None, score, size_conflict)` — `size_conflict=True` specifically means "the best TEXT match would have cleared the similarity floor but got excluded for a size mismatch," distinguishing it from "nothing textually close enough at all" so the two failure modes get different, actionable messages: `routers/walmart.py`'s `walmart_offer_analysis` now reports **"Found a similar Walmart listing... but its size didn't match... treated as a different product"** vs the plain **"No Walmart result matched... closely enough."**
- **Verified** with a 3-case unit test reproducing the exact reported scenario: (1) the reported 0.4 vs 4 fl oz case is now correctly rejected with `size_conflict=True`; (2) a control case — the SAME product at the SAME size — is still correctly accepted (proves the size check isn't overly strict); (3) a regression check re-confirming 2026-09-29b's original NYX-eyeliner bug fix still works after this change. Also re-ran the full-app throwaway-server smoke test (still 400s cleanly — this session has no real Walmart credentials to test the live search itself).
- Scope: `services/walmart/catalog.py` (`best_match`/`search_best` return shape changed to a 3-tuple, new import of `_size_mismatch`), `routers/walmart.py` (`walmart_offer_analysis` unpacks 3 values, new size-conflict-specific note text, `size_conflict` added to the response), `static/app.js` (CSV export gained a `size_conflict` column — the results table's existing Note column already surfaces the new message with no HTML change needed). **Needs a backend restart** (Python changed) + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-29b) — Walmart Offer Analysis: title search now fuzzy-matched, not "trust the first result" (first live credentials confirmed working, and surfaced a real bug):**
- User's own live credentials worked for the first time this session (`WALMART_CLIENT_ID`/`WALMART_CLIENT_SECRET` now configured, confirming the API key IS scoped for `/v3/items/walmart/search`), and immediately surfaced a real bug: searching by title `"L'Oreal Paris Eye Makeup Remover"` returned a completely unrelated item, `"NYX Professional Makeup Vivid Rich Mechanical Eyeliner Pencil, Aquamarine Dream"`.
- **Root cause**: `WalmartCatalogAPI.search_one()` (`services/walmart/catalog.py`) just returned `items[0]` with zero relevance check. Walmart's keyword search does not reliably rank the actual best match first (verified — reproduced the exact reported case in a unit test), so a plain "take result 0" is unsafe for a title/keyword search. This is the exact same class of problem this app already solved for Amazon (brand/title fuzzy matching in `services/analytics/matcher.py`) — a raw keyword search hit needs a relevance floor before being trusted, an exact-identifier (UPC/barcode) hit does not.
- **Fix**: new `best_match(items, query, min_ratio=55.0)` + `WalmartCatalogAPI.search_best(query=...)` in `services/walmart/catalog.py` — scores every returned item's title against the query via `rapidfuzz.fuzz.token_set_ratio` (already a project dependency), returns the highest-scoring item ONLY if it clears `MIN_TITLE_MATCH_RATIO` (55), else `(None, best_score)` — a genuinely bad search returns "no match," never a guess. `routers/walmart.py`'s `walmart_offer_analysis` now calls `search_best()` for `query`-based rows (title search) and keeps `search_one()` only for `upc`-based rows (an exact barcode hit is trusted the same way a UPC match already is for Amazon — no fuzzy check needed there). The match score is threaded through as `match_score` in the response; when nothing clears the floor, the `note` explicitly reports the best score found (e.g. "best title similarity: 37%") instead of a generic message.
- **UI**: new **"Match"** column in the Offer Analysis results table (`static/index.html`/`static/app.js`) — shows `"exact (UPC)"` for barcode rows, or the fuzzy score (e.g. `"86%"`) for title rows, or `"—"` when nothing matched; added to the CSV export too. Panel help text updated to explain the relevance floor.
- **Verified**: a unit test reproducing the EXACT reported bug — feeding `best_match()` the two real titles from the report (the NYX eyeliner + the correct L'Oreal item) for the query `"L'Oreal Paris Eye Makeup Remover"` — confirms it now picks the correct L'Oreal item (score 85.7) instead of the NYX eyeliner that plain `item[0]` would have returned, plus a control case (a genuinely unrelated query) correctly returns no match rather than a low-confidence guess. Also re-ran the full-app smoke test (throwaway server, no real credentials in this session's own `.env`) to confirm the new code paths don't crash and the UI changes are present.
- Scope: `services/walmart/catalog.py` (`best_match`, `search_best`, `MIN_TITLE_MATCH_RATIO`), `routers/walmart.py` (`walmart_offer_analysis` branches on upc vs query), `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (Python changes) + browser hard-refresh.

The backend (`routers/walmart.py walmart_offer_analysis`) already accepted a `query` field and fell back to a keyword search when no UPC was given — this was a frontend-only gap: `_parseWalmartOaRows()` (`static/app.js`) unconditionally treated the first comma-separated field as a UPC and dropped any row without one.

Textarea placeholder/help text updated with a title-search example ("Tide Pods Original 42 ct, 8.20, Home").

**Changes this session (2026-09-28b) — Walmart Catalog: first working slice (auth, item search, Offer Analysis) — untested against real credentials:**
- Follow-on from 2026-09-28's research + placeholder scaffold. User answered the 4 open questions: (1) "Yes we gave access to search (I think)" — assume the key IS scoped for item search; (2) "Production for first testing" — no sandbox; (3) "No, lets just pull out the buybox and all the fees... if only price is available we can subtract the bb from the cost from all the fees" — explicitly rejected wiring in the Repricer buy-box-win signal; the model is the same one this app already uses for Amazon (`net_profit = price - cost - fees`), just adapted to Walmart's thinner data; (4) "I dont really know lets check... lets try it!" — rate limits to be discovered empirically once live, not pre-solved.
- **A second research fork (mid-session) confirmed Walmart has NO live per-item fee-estimate API** — unlike Amazon SP-API's `getMyFeesEstimateForASIN`. The Price API / Item Management API are seller-scoped, post-listing tools (not usable for pre-listing "what would this cost to sell here" research); the only fee-bearing endpoint (Price Incentives) only covers items already in your own enrolled catalog. Referral fees and WFS (fulfillment) fees are **published static rate cards only**, not queryable live. **Decision: build the fee side as a static rate table**, exactly mirroring how this app already hardcodes Amazon's referral fee as a tiered formula (`=IF(price>10, 15%, 8%)`) rather than calling a live endpoint that doesn't exist.
- **New `services/walmart/` package** (mirrors `services/spapi/`'s structure): `config.py` (`load_walmart_credentials()` reads `WALMART_CLIENT_ID`/`WALMART_CLIENT_SECRET` from `.env`, defaults `production=True` unless `WALMART_SANDBOX=1` — per the user's explicit production-first choice); `auth.py` (`WalmartTokenManager` — OAuth2 `client_credentials`, HTTP Basic auth header, `WM_SVC.NAME`/`WM_QOS.CORRELATION_ID` headers per Walmart's spec, caches the 900s token and refreshes 60s before expiry — same shape as `spapi/auth.py`'s `LWATokenManager` but a much shorter TTL since Walmart has no long-lived refresh token); `catalog.py` (`WalmartCatalogAPI.search()` — wraps `GET /v3/items/walmart/search` by query/upc/gtin/ean/isbn, with 429 backoff; `.search_one()` convenience returns just the first hit); `offer_analysis.py` (`compute_offer_analysis()` — the ROI calc: `net_profit = walmart_price - vendor_cost - referral_fee - other_fees`, `referral_fee` from `REFERRAL_RATE_TABLE` keyed by a substring-matched category name with `DEFAULT_REFERRAL_RATE=0.15` fallback — the table is a representative slice of Walmart's published schedule, NOT exhaustive, easy to extend; `other_fees` (e.g. WFS) has no sourced rate yet so it's a caller-supplied override defaulting to 0).
- **New `routers/walmart.py`** (registered in `main.py`): `GET /api/walmart/status` (configured? production or sandbox?); `POST /api/walmart/search` (body `{query?|upc?|gtin?|ean?|isbn?}` → raw Walmart search response); `POST /api/walmart/offer-analysis` (body `{items:[{upc?, query?, vendor_cost, category?, other_fees?}]}` → per-item Walmart price + title + the full `compute_offer_analysis()` breakdown, searched concurrently via `asyncio.gather`). All three degrade to a clear `400` (not a crash) when credentials aren't configured yet.
- **UI**: the "Walmart Catalog" placeholder view now has a REAL panel (`static/index.html`/`static/app.js`, mirroring MPN Check's textarea+table pattern) — a connection-status line, a `UPC, Vendor Cost[, Category]`-per-line textarea, "Run Offer Analysis" button, results table (UPC/Title/Walmart Price/Vendor Cost/Referral Fee/Net Profit/ROI/Note), and CSV export. `_refreshWalmartStatus()` runs on every visit to the view (wired into `switchView`).
- **Verified via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001, using the project's own `.venv` — this shell's bare Python has no uvicorn/fastapi installed)**: full app imports clean (132 routes), `GET /api/walmart/status` → `{"configured": false, "production": null}` (correct — no `.env` keys yet), `POST /api/walmart/search` → clean `400` with the exact missing-env-var message (not a 500), the new HTML panel + JS functions are present in the served static files. **NOT verified against real Walmart credentials or a live API call** — this session never had the actual Client ID/Secret values, only the user's confirmation they exist and are believed to be scoped for search.
- **Still needed before this does anything live**: the user must add `WALMART_CLIENT_ID` + `WALMART_CLIENT_SECRET` to `.env` (paste the actual values, or add them directly) and restart the backend. First real test should be a single `POST /api/walmart/search {"upc": "<a real UPC>"}` to (a) confirm the token endpoint + key scope actually work in production, (b) see the REAL response shape for `price`/`category` — `_extract_price`/`_extract_category` in `routers/walmart.py` are written defensively (handle both a flat number and a `{amount,currency}` dict for price) because this session could not observe a real response; may need adjusting once real data comes back. **Rate limits are genuinely unknown** — per the user's "let's try it," the plan is to just run it and read whatever `x-amzn`-style rate-limit headers Walmart returns, then size a token-bucket limiter (like `spapi/rate_limiter.py`) only if throttling is actually hit.
- Scope: `services/walmart/` (new: `__init__.py`, `config.py`, `auth.py`, `catalog.py`, `offer_analysis.py`), `routers/walmart.py` (new), `main.py` (router registration), `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (new Python module/routes) + browser hard-refresh. No `.env` change made by this session (no real credentials were available) — that's the next actual blocking step.

Token is short-lived (900s/15min) — no long-lived refresh token like SP-API's LWA, so a token manager needs to re-auth constantly, not once. **Catalog/item search** = `GET /v3/items/walmart/search` (market-wide, any seller, up to 40 results/query, searchable by keyword/upc/gtin/ean/isbn) — the direct equivalent of SP-API's Catalog Items search; `/v3/items/catalog/search` is a DIFFERENT, seller-scoped endpoint (your own listings only) and is not useful for market-wide lookup. **Pricing is thin** — item search returns only one `price` field, no competing-offer stack; Buy-Box signal only via the separate Repricer/Pricing-Strategy APIs (react to win/lose, no visibility into competitors' prices) — same opacity ceiling as Amazon SP-API, not better. **Rate limits** are per-seller token-bucket, shown in response headers, no fixed published ceiling for item search (item *feeds*/bulk-upload are capped 10/hr, unrelated to search). Adjacent APIs confirmed but not yet wired: Inventory, Price, Item Management (all seller-scoped).

- **Not yet built**: no `.env` keys (`WALMART_CLIENT_ID`/`WALMART_CLIENT_SECRET`), no token-manager service, no router/service files, no actual API calls — this session deliberately stopped at "research + scaffold the home for it," per the user's own "for now" framing and this project's established pattern (investigate → present a plan → ask questions → implement only after confirmation).
