**Changes this session (2026-09-25c) — Vendor Offers: stale offers (>6 days unrefreshed) now have their available qty cleared automatically:**
- Follow-on from confirming fbidb tracks a genuine per-item last-upload timestamp (`vendor_account_product_listings.updated_at`/`scraped_at`, refreshed whenever a new file upload touches that specific item — already the same field this session's DB sync uses as each offer's staleness marker). User: "maybe if the sku hasnt been updated for 6 days, then lets clear the available qty."
- **`services/vendor_offers.py`**: new `clear_stale_available_qty(days=6)` — `UPDATE vo_vendor_offers SET avail_qty=NULL WHERE last_seen < cutoff AND avail_qty IS NOT NULL`. Clears to **NULL** ("unknown"), not 0 ("confirmed out of stock") — matches how a missing avail_qty is already treated everywhere else in this module (`only_available` filter, `(win.get("avail") or 0) > 0`). Applies uniformly regardless of source — both file-upload and DB-synced offers share the same `last_seen` staleness marker, so a stale quantity is equally untrustworthy either way. `STALE_AVAIL_QTY_DAYS = 6` is a module constant, not hardcoded inline, in case the threshold needs tuning later.
- **Wired into the existing hourly DB-sync scheduler** (`services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py sync_all()`) rather than a new scheduler thread — runs once per hour alongside the 5 vendors' own syncs, which is far more granular than a 6-day threshold actually needs but costs nothing extra. Result recorded in the sync summary as `stale_avail_qty: {cleared, cutoff}`.
- **Verified live**: checked real numbers before implementing — 50,853 total offers, 29,146 already >6 days stale, of which **16,700 still had a non-NULL avail_qty** (i.e. a real, meaningful cleanup, not a no-op). Ran it once immediately: cleared exactly those 16,700 rows, confirmed 0 remain matching the stale+has-qty condition afterward.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers.py` (new function), `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py` (`sync_all` wiring). Data cleanup already applied directly. **Needs a backend restart** for the hourly scheduler to pick up the new step going forward.

**Changes this session (2026-09-25b) — UI polish: MPN Check mode + Brand Check sample-size radios styled as segmented pills:**
- User: "could you please make the 'per line' and 'one manufacturer' into one radio button to make it look more neat, also the brand check 'a fixed number' 'a %' please make that too into a radio to make it more cleaner." Both were already functional `<input type="radio">` pairs — the ask was purely visual: merge each pair into one compact pill instead of two separate circle-radio + label rows.
- **New reusable `.seg-radio` CSS component** (`static/styles.css`, right after `.toggle-pill`): a bordered, rounded container holding 2+ `<label>` segments; the native radio input is visually hidden (`position:absolute;opacity:0`) inside each label, and `label:has(input:checked)` styles the active segment (indigo fill, white bold text) — pure CSS, no JS needed, using `:has()` (broadly supported in current Chrome/Edge/Safari/Firefox, fine for this internal desktop tool).
- Applied to both: MPN Check's "per line (2nd column)" / "one manufacturer for all" mode toggle, and Brand Check's "a fixed number" / "a %" sample-size toggle. Kept the exact same underlying `<input>` `id`s/`name`s in both cases, so `_syncMpnCheckMode()`/`_syncBrandCheckMode()` (`static/app.js`) needed zero changes — this was a markup/CSS-only restyle.
- Verified: div/label nesting balanced (81/81), both `seg-radio` groups present, page renders cleanly via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001; user's own server on 8000 untouched).
- Scope: `static/styles.css`, `static/index.html` only. **No backend restart needed** — hard-refresh the browser tab.

**Changes this session (2026-09-25) — MPN Check: qty/case now sourced from AccessGUDID (free FDA database), not fbidb's unreliable "CS" tag:**
- Follow-on from the Quality King INNER×PACK investigation, which proved fbidb's `packaged_products` "CS" tag can mislabel an intermediate packaging tier as the full case. User: "please dont take the case values from FBI, would it be possible to get from some other place? how much would it cost to get from LLM, say for 100" — then, after I measured the LLM option live (2 real calls: $0.165–$0.572/item, ~$37 avg/100, driven by how many web searches a hard case takes) — user: "maybe we could use acess GUDID api for this?"
- **Investigated and verified AccessGUDID is real, free, and directly usable**: it's the FDA's public Global Unique Device Identification Database; the actual working API is **openFDA's `https://api.fda.gov/device/udi.json`** (a mirror of the same data — AccessGUDID's own `accessgudid.nlm.nih.gov` site returned 404s for every endpoint path tried; openFDA's is documented and live). Searches directly by **`catalog_number`** (= MPN), no API key needed for reasonable volume.
- **Case quantity requires walking a packaging hierarchy, not reading one field** — confirmed live against 5 real MPNs from our own vendor data: a device's `identifiers` array is a chain of package levels (Primary → intermediate → Case), each carrying its OWN `quantity_per_package` relative to its immediate child (via `unit_of_use_id`) — e.g. CURCHSIL0753's "CS"-tagged tier holds 6 of an intermediate package that itself holds 4 primaries → **24** real units/case (matches the device's own description text "24/CS" exactly), not 6. Implemented as `_case_qty_from_identifiers()` in the new `services/mpn_check_gudid.py`: finds the identifier tagged `package_type` in `{CASE,CS,CTN,CARTON,BOX}`, then multiplies `quantity_per_package` down the `unit_of_use_id` chain until it reaches the `Primary` node.
- **Two real correctness traps found and handled, both confirmed live:**
  1. **MPN collisions across unrelated companies are common in GUDID too** — "1271" (real Teleflex item in our data) returns 20 hits in GUDID, from Sontec Instruments/OsteoMed/Mercodia/etc., **none from Teleflex** — taking the first hit blindly would have silently attached a totally wrong device's case quantity. Fix: when a catalog number has multiple hits, `company_name` must fuzzy-match the given manufacturer or the lookup honestly reports "none from ‘Teleflex’" instead of guessing.
  2. **But a SINGLE hit should NOT be rejected just because company_name differs** — confirmed live: STL2644 (a real MPN in our data, manufacturer "Medline") has exactly one GUDID record, correctly labeled "CENTURION MEDICAL PRODUCTS CORPORATION" (the actual FDA-registered device manufacturer — Medline is the brand/distributor we order it as, an OEM/private-label relationship that's extremely common in medical distribution). An initial strict-company-match version wrongly rejected this genuinely-correct unique hit; fixed by only requiring a company match to DISAMBIGUATE when there's more than one result — a lone hit for a given catalog number is trusted regardless of company-name wording.
- **`services/mpn_check.py check_mpns()` rewritten**: `product_id` still comes from the local mirror (confirms the MPN/manufacturer combo exists in OUR OWN data — kept, still useful and free) but is now purely informational; **`qty_case` and `matched` now come from a live, concurrent (`ThreadPoolExecutor`, 8 workers) call to `mpn_check_gudid.lookup_case_qty()` per row** — fbidb's `packaged_products.qty_case` column is no longer read at all for this field. Response shape is UNCHANGED (`{mpn, manufacturer, matched, product_id, qty_case, note}`), so no router or frontend changes were needed — the existing "Search the web with AI" second-pass logic (`!matched || qty_case==null`) still gates correctly on the new semantics.
- **Verified live end-to-end** with the exact 7-pair test used throughout this investigation (5 real MPNs + a blank row + a fake-manufacturer collision case): all 7 behaved exactly as designed — 2 real case quantities found (100, 24), 1 found-but-no-case-data, 1 correctly-rejected cross-manufacturer collision (with local `product_id` STILL populated from our own data, showing the two signals are genuinely independent and complementary), 1 correctly not-found (non-medical vendor), took **2.17s total for 7 concurrent lookups**.
- Scope: `services/mpn_check_gudid.py` (new), `services/mpn_check.py` (`check_mpns` + `_local_product_id` rewritten; `ai_lookup_qty_case`/the AI fallback path untouched). No router/frontend changes. **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-09-24b) — Vendor Offers: fixed 3 wrong ASIN pairs, and closed the root cause (file-upload loaders now reject non-barcode "UPC" codes):**
- User walked through 3 real bad pairs live, corrected one at a time (each applied immediately via `vo.assign_asin`, which also re-mirrors into the Pair Library): **`B018IZA6GQ`** → `033200194606` (was wrongly `033200197140`, pack size 2 kept); **`B0199TEKPS`** → `033200194606` (was wrongly `033200197140`, pack size 6 kept — confirms a UPC legitimately serving multiple ASINs, which the desk already supports, isn't itself a red flag). `B00J5K670S` (the very first one raised) is still open — the stored UPC `033200197935` never matched what the user first typed (`33200001799`); awaiting the correct value before touching it.
- **Root cause found for a 4th, DIFFERENT class of bad pair**: user reported UPC `30521511` (an 8-digit code) and `305215070009` (12-digit) are actually the SAME real product (Q-Tips 170ct), "diamond sends us weird upcs." Traced live: Diamond Wholesale's THREE offers for this item (`vendor_item_id` `30521525100`/`30521525100C`/`30521525100D`) were sitting under the fake UPC `000030521511` (Diamond's raw "12DGTUPC" column literally contains their own internal/warehouse code here, not a real barcode) — completely disconnected from the SAME product's correct listing under `305215070009`, where Cencora/Quality King/Victory's offers for the identical Q-Tips already lived. **This is NOT the same bug as the 3 ASIN pairs above** — those were bad MANUAL ASIN assignments (`vo_asin_listings`); this is a vendor OFFER landing under the wrong UPC entirely (`vo_vendor_offers`), from an old CSV file upload that predates any checksum validation.
- **Confirmed this is exactly the class of bug the 2026-09-23 DB-sync work already solved for the 5 DB-synced vendors** (`vo.is_valid_upc_a()`/`vendor_offers_db_sync._valid_upc_a()` already correctly rejects `30521511` — verified live it returns `None`/`False`) — **but the OLDER, still-present file-upload loaders (`load_quality_king`/`load_cencora`/`load_diamond`/`load_bilo` in `services/vendor_offers.py`) had ZERO shape/checksum validation on their UPC column**, only a bare "is it non-empty" check. So a vendor's internal code that happens to be all-digits sails straight through on a manual "Choose a csv/xlsx instead" upload (still available today as a fallback for these 5 vendors, and the only path for any vendor beyond them), creating exactly this kind of silently-orphaned phantom UPC.
- **Cleanup (this specific item)**: `UPDATE vo_vendor_offers SET upc='305215070009' WHERE upc='000030521511'` (moved Diamond's 3 offers onto the real UPC) + deleted the now-empty phantom `vo_products` row for `000030521511`. Verified: Diamond's Q-Tips pricing ($3.34) now sits correctly alongside Cencora ($1.92)/Quality King ($1.87)/Victory in the price ladder for `305215070009`.
- **Systemic fix**: added `is_valid_upc_a(value)` (+ `_upc_a_check_digit`) as a SHARED helper in `services/vendor_offers.py` (right next to `norm_upc`, its natural home) — real UPC-A checksum validation, not just shape. Applied it to all 4 file-upload loaders' existing skip-reason chains (`load_quality_king`/`load_cencora`/`load_diamond`/`load_bilo`), each gaining one more `elif not is_valid_upc_a(upc): reason = "... isn't a valid UPC-A (likely an internal/warehouse code, not a real barcode)"` check — a row that fails now gets skipped with a clear, visible reason (already surfaced in the existing "skipped" count/log every loader reports) instead of silently creating a disconnected phantom product. `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py`'s own `_valid_upc_a()` now DELEGATES to this same shared validator instead of keeping an independent copy of the checksum math (drift risk — two copies of the same logic is exactly how something like this slips through later).
- **Verified**: `vo.is_valid_upc_a('30521511')` → False, `vo.is_valid_upc_a('305215070009')` → True; `vendor_offers_db_sync._valid_upc_a` re-tested against both the same way (identical results to before the consolidation — no behavior change there); re-ran Diamond's live DB sync end-to-end post-change — identical counts to the original 2026-09-23 run (1716/1720 valid, 4 skipped), confirming the consolidation didn't regress the DB-sync path.
- **Not yet done**: no live re-upload of a real vendor CSV file was performed to see the new loader-side skip message in action (would need an actual vendor file with a bad code to trigger it) — logic was verified via the shared validator's own test + careful review of each insertion point, not an end-to-end file upload. If a future CSV upload silently drops more rows than expected, check the "skipped" reasons first — this is now a real, visible category among them.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers.py` (`is_valid_upc_a`/`_upc_a_check_digit` added; all 4 loaders gained one `elif`), `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py` (`_valid_upc_a` now delegates). Data cleanup applied directly (not code — no restart needed for that part). **Needs a backend restart** for the loader validation change to take effect on the next file upload.

**Changes this session (2026-09-24) — Offer Analytics export: new "Offer Vendor" column (which of the 5 vendors an unfiltered row's price/data came from):**
- User reported a specific wrong Price Desk pair (UPC 33200001799 / ASIN B00J5K670S) and asked about unpairing — investigated live: it exists ONLY in `vo_asin_listings` (`source='manual'`, stored UPC is actually `033200197935`, not an exact match to what the user typed — flagged the discrepancy and asked for confirmation before deleting anything, since this is a destructive action). Also confirmed a REAL gap while investigating: the Pair Manager's existing "✕ Remove" button only deletes from `pair_library`/`pair_library_ids` — it has never covered `vo_asin_listings` (the Price Desk's own local UPC↔ASIN table), so a manually-assigned Price Desk pair like this one currently has NO delete path anywhere in the app. Not yet fixed — pending the user confirming the correct UPC first.
- Separately, user: "when exporting by only the filter of has asin and not picking a vendor we must get the info from which vendor the asin we are getting" — confirmed the export had no such column: when no `focus_vendor` is chosen, `build_template_xlsx` silently picks the alphabetically-first vendor among tied cheapest offers (`win = vmap[sorted(winners)[0]]`) to supply Item ID/Vendor Title/Product Cost for that row, with **no way to tell from the output which vendor that was**. The existing "Vendor" column is unrelated (SellerCloud's last-PO vendor from `analytics.purchases_view`, not a Price Desk vendor) — confirmed no collision in meaning before adding a new one.
- **FIX (`services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`)**: added a new **"Offer Vendor"** column (right after UPC/EAN) carrying the actual winning vendor's name for every row, always — not just when unfiltered (useful confirmation even when a single vendor IS selected). `kept[upc]` now stores `(win, win["cost"], win_vendor)` instead of just `(win, win["cost"])`; `win_vendor` flows into `records` and then `rowd["Offer Vendor"]`.
- **Careful column-math fix, not just a list append**: `TEMPLATE_COLS` (the single source of truth for column order, per its own docstring) drives BOTH the openpyxl `ws.insert_cols(...)` blank-column-insertion calls (hardcoded positions, since the base template file has its own fixed original column layout) AND the final per-row cell writes. Adding "Offer Vendor" as TEMPLATE_COLS' 2nd entry meant every later `insert_cols` position had to shift by exactly +1 to keep the ORIGINAL template's own styled columns landing where TEMPLATE_COLS now says: `insert_cols(2,2)→(2,3)`, `insert_cols(11,5)→(12,5)`, `insert_cols(18,2)→(19,2)`. Hand-derived the new positions by walking the full updated TEMPLATE_COLS order once (documented inline in the patch), then **verified by building a real 5-row export and checking actual cell values** rather than trusting the arithmetic alone — confirmed "Offer Vendor" correctly shows "Cencora"/"Quality King Distributors" per row with everything downstream (SKU, ASIN, Item ID, Vendor Title, Product Cost, and every formula column: `Product cost * amz pack`, `Net Profit`, `Net Margin`, `ROI`, `Referral Fee`) landing in the exact right cells with formulas referencing the correct (shifted) column letters — a silent misalignment here would have been a real correctness bug, not a crash, so this got an actual data check, not just a compile check.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py` only (`TEMPLATE_COLS`, `_NEW_HEADER_COLS`, the 3 `insert_cols` calls, the `kept`-building loop, the `records.append` dict, `rowd`). **Needs a backend restart.**

**Changes this session (2026-09-23g) — MPN Check: local mirror (fixed the slowness), separate AI web-search fallback, sliding toggle switches:**
- User: "For some reason it takes quite a while to search it. The qty/case value rarely changes so we wouldnt need to always make a request to the Azure DB, since we mirror it, we can just quick send it into our DB. Also lets add an LLM to scour the web to find out the exact qty/case" + (separately, same turn) "could you please change the check buttons to a sliding button" + (mid-turn correction) "The LLM should be a separate option."
- **Root cause of the slowness (confirmed by re-reading 2026-09-23f's own design)**: `check_mpns()` queried Azure `fbidb` LIVE on every single check — `manufacturers`/`manufacturer_names` alone (~7k + ~53k rows) had only a 30-min in-memory cache, so any check after a cold start or cache expiry paid a real multi-second-to-a-minute Azure round trip before even starting the MPN match itself.
- **Local mirror (`services/mpn_check_sync.py`, new)**: two lean local SQLite tables — `mpn_products(product_id, mpn, normalized_mpn, manufacturer_id, qty_case)` and `mpn_manufacturer_lookup(normalized_name, manufacturer_id)`. Checked real table sizes before committing to the approach: `products` team-wide is **1,027,914** rows (**792,128** with an MPN), CS `packaged_products` is **306,946**, `manufacturer_names` **52,634** — a full narrow-column pull timed live at **54.4s + 30.5s + ~5s ≈ 90s total**, confirming a full mirror is a one-time cost worth paying, not something to repeat per-request. `sync_manufacturers()` does a full rebuild each run (fast — ~5s, simpler than incremental for this size); `sync_products()`/`sync_packaged()` are **incremental** via each table's own `updated_at` (a watermark stored via the existing `database.get_setting`/`set_setting` kv pattern), falling back to a full pull only on `full=True` or first run. Hourly in-process scheduler mirrors `vendor_offers_db_sync.py`'s exact pattern (`start_scheduler`/`stop_scheduler`, wired into `main.py`'s lifespan next to the other two schedulers); the first tick does `full=True` automatically when the mirror is empty, every tick after is incremental.
- **`services/mpn_check.py` rewritten**: `check_mpns()` now reads ONLY the local SQLite mirror (`database._connect()`, `?` placeholders) — zero Azure calls in the request path. **Measured live: 0.0011s for 7 pairs** (was a multi-second-to-60s Azure round trip depending on cache state) — verified all results identical/correct to the pre-mirror Azure-live version on the same 5 real (MPN, manufacturer) pairs used in the original 2026-09-23f verification.
- **Ran the real initial full sync live**: `sync_all(full=True)` — **181.8s**, 792,128 products + 306,946 CS packages + 24,148 manufacturer aliases (name+alias pairs, more than the raw 52,634 alias ROWS since one alias can map to multiple manufacturer_ids in rare cases) — verified the local mirror's row counts and a spot-check (`1-0425N`→manufacturer_id 294/qty 25) match Azure exactly.
- **`services/mpn_check.py ai_lookup_qty_case()` (new) — the AI web-search fallback, kept STRICTLY separate per the user's mid-turn correction**: NOT part of `check_mpns()` or its `fields` set — a standalone function, called only by a dedicated endpoint, only for rows the caller explicitly hands it (the UI only sends rows the local mirror couldn't resolve). Uses `services.ai_recheck.make_client()` (Anthropic) with the `web_search_20250305` tool, mirroring the EXACT "verify rather than guess" pattern already validated for Brand Analytics AI Fill (2026-08-12): the prompt demands the model confirm the quantity belongs to the SPECIFIC (mpn, manufacturer) pair and return `qty_case: null` rather than a plausible-but-unverified number; a `confidence: "low"` or a null quantity both short-circuit to "no answer" — never a guess. **Verified live** against a deliberately fake MPN/manufacturer: correctly returned `qty_case: null` with a "could not verify" note (no hallucination); did not separately re-verify the positive-match path live (would need a real MPN this account's data doesn't already have qty/case for, to prove a genuine web-found answer) — worth trying against a real gap once you're using it.
- **`routers/mpn_check.py` gained**: `GET /mpn-check/sync-status` (mirror populated? last sync summary?), `POST /mpn-check/sync` (manual sync, body `{full?}`), `POST /mpn-check/ai-fallback` (body `{pairs}`, 4-way concurrent, returns `{qty_case, confidence, note}` per pair — completely separate from the main `/mpn-check` endpoint, matching the "separate option" requirement).
- **UI**: a NEW, separate "Search the web with AI" toggle in MPN Check's Find section (own row, own checkbox `#mpn-check-opt-ai`, off by default) — when checked, `_checkMpns()` runs the normal fast local check first, renders it immediately, THEN (only if the toggle is on) kicks off `_runMpnAiFallback()` as a visibly separate second pass over just the rows still unmatched/missing qty_case, updating those specific table rows in place (`_updateMpnRow`) rather than re-rendering the whole table, with its own progress text ("Searching the web for N unresolved…" → "X/Y matched (W via AI)"). A small status line (`#mpn-check-sync-status`) shows when the local mirror was last synced, refreshed on-demand when the MPN Check tab is opened (`_refreshMpnSyncStatus()`, hooked into `_switchToolsTab`).
- **Sliding toggle switches**: reused the app's EXISTING `.toggle-pill` component (already built for the Analytics wizard's "Match from Keepa" toggle — pure CSS, no new styles needed) in place of every plain checkbox across the Tools panel's "what to run/find" rows — ASIN Check's Eligibility/Storage/Generic (3) and MPN Check's Qty/Case + the new AI toggle (2) — 5 total. Kept the exact same underlying `<input type="checkbox" id="...">` elements/IDs so no other JS needed to change; only the surrounding markup (title+description now on the left, `<label class="toggle-pill"><input.../><span class="slider"></span></label>` on the right) and each row's layout (`justify-content:space-between`) changed.
- **Verified live end-to-end** via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001; the user's own server on 8000 untouched): sync-status endpoint correctly reports `mirror_populated:true` + the real sync summary; `/api/mpn-check` returns correct fast results on the fresh process; `/api/mpn-check/ai-fallback` correctly declines on fake data; all new HTML elements (`#mpn-check-opt-ai`, `#mpn-check-sync-status`) and JS functions (`_runMpnAiFallback`, `_refreshMpnSyncStatus`) present in the served files; div/label nesting balanced; brace/paren counts consistent with the established pre-existing baseline offset. Bumped `app.js?v=51`.
- Scope: `services/mpn_check_sync.py` (new), `services/mpn_check.py` (rewritten), `routers/mpn_check.py` (rewritten), `main.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (the first restart will run a fast incremental sync since the mirror is already fully populated from this session's live test) + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-23f) — NEW: MPN Check (Tools panel's 3rd sub-tab) — qty/case lookup from the fbidb database:**
- User's spec: "we paste in some amount of mpns and either as a second column add the manufacturer/brand or choose as an option write the brand/manufacturer for all the mpns. Then we enable whatever we need to find, so for now I want to know the qty/case for each of the mpn... This information we can get from the database also from the tables." Confirmed this is the SAME Azure `fbidb` database (server `fordmedical.database.windows.net`, `AZURE_SQL_SERVER_DATABASE=fbidb`) already reached via `services/azure_sql.py`'s connection — the same one `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py` reads `dbo.vendor_accounts`/`vendor_account_product_listings`/`packaged_products`/`products` from.
- **Investigated the exact join before writing any UI**, same discipline as the vendor-offers DB sync: (a) `products.manufacturer_part_number` is the MPN field, with a companion `normalized_manufacturer_part_number` column — discovered LIVE (not assumed) that this normalization is lowercase + alnum-only **then leading zeros stripped** (`'0000TD0320L' -> 'td0320l'`, confirmed against a real Filmop product that only matched once the leading-zero-strip was added — matches this app's own `norm_upc()` convention elsewhere, just independently confirmed for this table). (b) An MPN string is NOT globally unique across manufacturers — same lesson as Create SKUs' `_main_exists()` fix from 2026-07-17 — so every lookup here is scoped to (normalized MPN, resolved manufacturer_id), never MPN alone. (c) Manufacturer resolution needs BOTH `manufacturers.name` (canonical) AND every `manufacturer_names.normalized_name` alias — verified live that a manufacturer isn't always its own alias row (Filmop had zero self-referencing `manufacturer_names` rows, only the canonical `manufacturers.name` matched) — missing either source would silently under-match. (d) "Qty/Case" = `packaged_products.uom_unit='CS'` → `uom_quantity` (confirmed the unit-code vocabulary live: CS=case, EA=each, BX=box, PK=pack, …, `CS` is the 2nd-most-common code at 306,946 rows team-wide).
- **`services/mpn_check.py`** (new): `check_mpns(pairs, fields)` — given `[(mpn, manufacturer), ...]` (order + duplicates preserved), resolves ALL distinct manufacturer texts to `manufacturer_id` sets in one bulk pull (`_manufacturer_map`, 30-min in-memory cache — same TTL-cache pattern as `azure_sql.fetch_rows()`, since pulling ~7k manufacturers + their aliases takes real time and would otherwise be paid on every single check), then batches the MPN lookup (`normalized_manufacturer_part_number IN (...)`, chunked 400/query) and the qty/case lookup (`packaged_products WHERE uom_unit='CS' AND product_id IN (...)`, also chunked) — all scoped to `team_id=1` (Ford Medical, per the established finding). Returns one result per input row: `{mpn, manufacturer, matched, product_id, qty_case, note}` with a clear `note` distinguishing "manufacturer not found" vs "MPN not found for that manufacturer" vs "no case-pack (CS) record for this product" vs a real match. `SUPPORTED_FIELDS = {"qty_case"}` is the extension point for whatever field the user asks for next (per their "I'll let you know what it means soon").
- **`routers/mpn_check.py`** (new, registered in `main.py`): `POST /api/mpn-check` — body `{pairs:[{mpn,manufacturer}], fields?:["qty_case"]}` → `{results, matched, total}`.
- **UI** (`static/index.html` + `static/app.js`, Tools panel's 3rd sub-tab — replaces the placeholder from 2026-09-23d): a mode toggle **"per line (2nd column)"** (paste `MPN<tab/comma/spaces>Manufacturer` per line) vs **"one manufacturer for all"** (a single manufacturer field applies to every pasted MPN, textarea becomes MPN-only) — mirrors the exact UX pattern the user already asked for. A "Find" section with one checkbox so far: **Qty / Case** (checked; designed so more checkboxes can be added here later without restructuring). Upload-a-list button (mirrors Check Brand's). Results table (MPN / Manufacturer / Qty per Case / Note) + Download CSV, reusing the same `.abbr-section-title`/table/CSV-export visual conventions already established for Check Brand in this exact panel.
- **Verified live end-to-end**: (a) 5 real (MPN, manufacturer) pairs pulled fresh from the database itself all round-tripped to their exact real `qty_case` values (Teleflex 1271→20, Medline 1-0425N→25, Filmop 0000TD0320L→10, Medline CURCHSIL0753→24, Medline STL2644→100); (b) a nonexistent MPN, an unknown manufacturer, and a blank row each produced the correct distinct `note`; (c) hit the real running `/api/mpn-check` HTTP endpoint (not just the Python function) and got the identical correct JSON back; (d) confirmed via a throwaway smoke-test server that the new HTML/JS renders with no structural issues (div nesting balanced, brace/paren counts consistent with the pre-existing baseline). Bumped `app.js?v=50`.
- Scope: `services/mpn_check.py` (new), `routers/mpn_check.py` (new), `main.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-23e) — Offer Analytics export: fixed a 4-hour export dying at the very end and losing everything (Keepa token exhaustion was fatal, not recoverable):**
- User: "For some reason I was exporting the vendor offers for 4 hours, but then it just after completion said error." Asked for the exact error text; user recalled "something ran out" then "it was 12k" — that number matches this app's own earlier-logged Keepa account token-bucket size (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-26e/2026-09-03: "Token bucket is large (12,600 left...)"), pointing straight at Keepa token exhaustion.
- **ROOT CAUSE, confirmed by reading the code path**: `services/keepa.py fetch_products()` (the ONLY caller of Keepa's live `/product` data, used exclusively by the Offer Analytics export) makes **two** `_call_product` calls per 100-ASIN batch (stats=30 and stats=90 — double the token cost of a single call). `_call_product`'s error handling treated **any** Keepa-reported error — including Keepa's own recoverable "not enough tokens, retry later" response — as immediately fatal: `if j.get("error"): raise RuntimeError(...)`. That exception was never caught inside `fetch_products`'s batch loop, so it propagated all the way up through `build_template_xlsx` and killed the whole background job. Since the job's Excel workbook is only assembled and returned at the very end (`wb.save(buf)` after the full records loop), **a single exhausted batch near the tail of a multi-hour export discarded every hour of already-completed enrichment** — nothing was ever partially saved. Notably, the analogous Product Finder call (`_query`, used by Brand Analytics' `find_asins`) ALREADY had the correct pattern (`if "token" in str(err).lower(): sleep; continue`) — `_call_product` was simply never given the same treatment when it was written.
- **THREE-LAYER FIX, all in `services/keepa.py`:**
  1. **`_call_product`**: a Keepa error containing "token" (case-insensitive) is now treated as recoverable — waits (`min(60, 6*(attempt+1))`s) and retries, exactly mirroring `_query`'s existing pattern, instead of raising. Retry budget raised 6→12 attempts (token refills can legitimately take longer than a transient network blip). Also raised `_wait_tokens`' pre-flight check from a flat `need=20` to `max(20, len(asins)*2)` — 20 tokens was never enough headroom for a real 100-ASIN buybox batch, so the old code was routinely attempting requests it already knew were under-funded.
  2. **`fetch_products`**: if a batch STILL fails after every `_call_product` retry (a genuinely exhausted/rate-limited account, or any other fatal Keepa error), that ONE batch is now skipped with a logged warning (`log.warning(... "skipping (%d ASINs will have no Keepa data)" ...)`) instead of raising out of the function — those ASINs simply get no Keepa columns (identical to how any ASIN Keepa has no data for already renders: blank/0), and the loop continues to the next batch. A multi-hour export can no longer lose ALL its work because of ONE bad batch near the end.
  3. Left the rest of `fetch_products`/`_call_product` untouched (network-error retry, seller-name lookup, `_flatten_product`) — scoped strictly to the token-exhaustion failure mode that matches what the user described.
- **Verified with offline mocked tests** (no real Keepa tokens spent): (a) `_call_product` given a token-error response for 2 attempts then a real success on the 3rd correctly retried and returned real data instead of raising; (b) `fetch_products` given 3 batches (100/100/50 ASINs) where the MIDDLE batch permanently fails every retry correctly returned Keepa data for the other 150 ASINs with **no exception raised** — confirming a mid-export failure no longer kills the whole job.
- Scope: `services/keepa.py` only (`_call_product`, `_wait_tokens` call site, `fetch_products`). Only caller is `vendor_offers_template_export.build_template_xlsx` — no other feature touches this function. **Needs a backend restart.** Not yet verified against a REAL Keepa token-exhaustion event live (would require actually draining the account's real token budget) — flag if a similarly-shaped failure still happens after this restart; the server log will now show `[keepa] batch N–M permanently failed, skipping` instead of silently losing the whole job, which will confirm whether this is the same failure mode recurring or something new.

Split the panel's single scroll container into 3 independent panes: `#tools-tab-asin` (the existing ASIN textarea + eligibility/storage/generic checkboxes + Run + progress bars + Download CSV — unchanged, just re-scoped into its own pane), `#tools-tab-brand` (the existing Check Brand section, its old `border-top` divider removed since it's no longer a stacked sub-section), `#tools-tab-mpn` (a placeholder using the app's existing `.empty-state` pattern — "MPN Check / Coming soon" — user will specify what it should do later). Checked live via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001); the user's own dev server on 8000 was left untouched.
