**Changes this session (2026-09-23c) — Offer Analytics export: consolidated into ONE modal + new "Populate Keepa data?" toggle:**
- Follow-on from 2026-09-23b. User asked (a) whether Buy Box 30d/90d and seller-share % can come from SP-API instead of Keepa — explained no: SP-API's Product Pricing API (`getItemOffersBatch`/`getCompetitivePricing`) only returns a CURRENT-moment snapshot (a single `IsBuyBoxWinner` flag), never a historical window or win-percentage; that requires something that has been polling and archiving history over time, which is exactly what Keepa does and Amazon doesn't expose itself (also re-surfaced this app's own earlier finding: a 100-ASIN sample found 0 ASINs with a LIVE current Buy Box via SP-API at all, mostly suppressed for niche medical listings). (b) Asked to add a **"Populate Keepa data? Yes/No"** choice and **consolidate the multi-step export scope modal into a single modal**.
- **`static/price_desk.html`**: replaced the 2026-09-23b two-step modal (`#expStep1`→`#expStep2`) with ONE modal (`#expModal`) containing 3 independent mutually-exclusive chip-pair groups — **Scope** (Everything / Only available qty), **Keepa data** (Populate Keepa data / Skip Keepa), **Filters** (Apply filters / Show as a whole) — plus a single **"Start export"** button at the bottom that reads all 3 selections at once. Chips reuse the existing `.chip.plain[aria-pressed]` toggle pattern already used for the toolbar's Contested/Stale/Has-ASIN filters (`_expGroup()` wires each pair as an exclusive pick). Choosing **Skip Keepa auto-disables + visually dims the Filters group** (forces "Show as a whole", swaps its help text to explain why) since 2 of the 4 filter criteria (estimated units sold, Amazon 30d %) are Keepa fields — filtering without Keepa would silently produce an empty export. `_expResetOptions()` resets all 3 groups to their defaults (Everything / Populate Keepa / Show as a whole) each time the modal opens.
- **`services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`**: `build_template_xlsx()` gained `populate_keepa: bool = True`; the Keepa fetch (`keepa_api.fetch_products(...)`) is now gated on `populate_keepa` instead of the (still-present, still SP-API-eligibility-only) `live` flag — these were bundled under one `live` flag before this session and are now independent. Also added a defensive guard at the top of the function: `if not populate_keepa: apply_filters = False` — protects any OTHER caller (not just this UI) from the same silently-empty-export trap the UI already prevents by disabling that combination.
- **`routers/vendor_offers.py`**: `analytics_export_start` reads `populate_keepa` (default `True`) from the POST body and threads it through.
- **Verified live end-to-end** via a throwaway smoke-test server (port 8001, the user's own dev server on 8000 untouched): (1) `populate_keepa:false, live:false` on a 5-UPC scope completed in ~1 min (Azure SellerCloud-mirror view scans dominate the time regardless of Keepa/SP-API, a known pre-existing characteristic — not something this change affects) and produced a correctly-shaped 44-column sheet with every Keepa/eligibility column blank/0 and the vendor-side data (Product Cost, Vendor Title) intact; (2) `populate_keepa:true, apply_filters:true, live:true` on the same scope ran the real Keepa + SP-API eligibility + generic checks with no crash and correctly produced a **header-only** sheet (all 5 rows excluded) since these particular test UPCs are low-volume fixtures that don't clear the ≥50-units-sold / live-Approved bar — exactly the expected outcome, not a bug.
- Scope: `static/price_desk.html`, `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`, `routers/vendor_offers.py`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-23b) — Offer Analytics export: optional "sellable & selling only" filters (units sold, Amazon Buy-Box share, Restricted/Generic/DOG):**
- User: "when we export on the vendor offers, can we automatically have some filters added? For example, only estimated units sold >= 50, amazon 30d <90% and remove everything with eligibility status \"restricted\" where its \"generic\" and \"DOG\"". Asked two clarifying things first since they affect cost/UX: (1) always-on vs. a toggle — user wants a SECOND step after the existing "Everything" / "Only available qty" scope choice ("apply filters" or "show as a whole"), not baked in and not a single combined modal; (2) whether to add the Generic-ASIN check (not part of this export before — costs one more SP-API call per ASIN) — user confirmed yes, add it.
- **`services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`**: `enrich_asins()` gained `check_generic: bool = False` — when set, each ASIN (in the same 5-worker pool, alongside the existing eligibility/storage calls) also runs `services.generic_check.classify_generic(asin)` (skipped for an ASIN already known DOG from the storage-check 404, to avoid a wasted call); now returns a 3rd dict `{asin: generic_status}` (signature: `-> tuple[dict, dict, dict]`, only caller is `build_template_xlsx`, updated). `build_template_xlsx()` gained `apply_filters: bool = False`, threaded into `enrich_asins(..., check_generic=apply_filters)`; the row-build loop now skips a record when `apply_filters` and ANY of: `monthly_sold_quantity < MIN_MONTHLY_UNITS_SOLD` (50), `amazon_bb_30d_percentage >= MAX_AMAZON_30D_BB_SHARE` (0.90 — Amazon itself already dominates the 30d Buy Box), `approved in EXCLUDED_APPROVAL_STATUSES` ({"RESTRICTED","DOG"} — "Approved" already carries DOG as a value via the existing 404-on-catalog-call detection, no separate DOG check needed there), or the new `generic_status == "GENERIC"`. A UPC with no ASIN naturally gets excluded too (no Keepa data → monthly=0 → fails the units floor) — no special-casing needed. Constants are module-level (`MIN_MONTHLY_UNITS_SOLD`/`MAX_AMAZON_30D_BB_SHARE`/`EXCLUDED_APPROVAL_STATUSES`) for easy tuning later.
- **`routers/vendor_offers.py`**: `analytics_export_start` reads `apply_filters` from the POST body and passes it through to `build_template_xlsx`.
- **`static/price_desk.html`**: the export scope modal (`#expModal`) is now 2 steps — step 1 (unchanged: "Everything" / "Only items with available qty") advances to step 2 instead of starting the export immediately; step 2 asks "Apply filters" vs. "Show as a whole" (explains the thresholds + the generic-check cost tradeoff in the copy). `startAnalyticsExport(onlyAvailable, applyFilters)` sends both flags; `closeExportScope()` resets back to step 1 for next time.
- **Verified**: all 3 files compile/import cleanly (`main.py` imports OK with the new signatures); page HTML renders both new modal steps; `POST /analytics-export/start` with `{"apply_filters": true}` accepted the param and started a background job with no crash (confirmed via a throwaway smoke-test server on port 8001, cancelled before completion since an unscoped full-desk run with the extra generic-check call is a genuinely long live SP-API job — not something to run to completion just for a smoke test). Did not do a full live end-to-end run confirming the actual row counts before/after filtering — the filter logic itself was carefully hand-traced against the exact Keepa/eligibility fields already used elsewhere in this same file (`k.get("monthly_sold_quantity")`, `k.get("amazon_bb_30d_percentage")`, `live_elig.get(asin) or elig_cache.get(asin)`), so flag if the first real filtered export looks off.
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py`, `routers/vendor_offers.py`, `static/price_desk.html`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-23) — Vendor Offer Analytics: 5 vendors now sync directly from Azure (no more manual file upload for them):**
- User: "whenever we receive an offer someone from our team uploads it to the database, so instead of manually uploading the offer, we can just take it from the database" — asked to investigate `dbo.vendor_accounts` + `dbo.vendor_account_product_listings` (join on vendor ID), then plan and implement. Mid-investigation user also flagged Ford Medical's internal "FBI" REST API (`fbi.fordmed.com`, key already in `.env` as `FBI_API_KEY`) as a possible alternative.
- **Investigated BOTH paths before building anything, per the user's "look into this first" instruction:**
  - **Raw SQL** (`AZURE_SQL_SERVER_DATABASE=fbidb`, same Azure SQL server/connection `services/azure_sql.py` already uses for the SellerCloud mirror, different schema `dbo`): confirmed live -- `vendor_accounts` (13 cols) + `vendor_account_product_listings` (28 cols, `vendor_account_id` FK) + `packaged_products` (`uom_quantity` = case-pack size) + `products` (`manufacturer_part_number` -- the ONLY identifier field, no dedicated UPC column). `vendor_price_in_currency` is the real cost (`vendor_price_usd` is ALWAYS NULL); all 5 target vendors are 100% `currency_id=1` (USD) on their active listings.
  - **FBI REST API** (`Authorization: Bearer <FBI_API_KEY>` → 200; other header styles all 401): fetched + inspected the full OpenAPI spec. Its `Offer`/`Package`/`Product` schemas are a direct wrapper around the SAME 4 tables above -- `Product.manufacturer_part_number` is still the only identifier field, so it does **not** solve the UPC problem either. Docs claim `/browse/products` search "reaches the barcodes a product's packages carry" -- tested live against 3 real vendor UPCs from our own data and got back unrelated products (noise, not a real barcode index). More decisively: **`/offers` has no vendor filter at all** (only `product_id`/`package_id`), so pulling just our 5 vendors means paging through the ENTIRE team's active offers (many more vendor accounts than ours -- saw Medline/McKesson/Dynarex/etc. on page 1) and filtering client-side; measured live at ~100 offers/1.6s, which would take many minutes-to-hours vs. the direct SQL join's <70s for all 5. **Decision: raw SQL join, not the FBI API** -- the API's only real advantage (no direct DB credential dependency) doesn't outweigh the missing vendor filter and the identical data-quality ceiling.
- **UPC validity, empirically checked, not assumed:** `manufacturer_part_number` is a genuine barcode for some vendors and an arbitrary internal code for others. New `_valid_upc_a()` (`services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py`) requires a full UPC-A checksum match (not just "12 digits") before accepting a value as `upc` -- verified live pass rates per vendor: Diamond 1716/1720 (99.8%), Victory 7629/7629 (100%), BILO 3575/3922 (91%), Quality King 13468/17042 (79%), **Cencora 23547/42051 (56%)** -- Cencora's ABC warehouse item codes are the messiest, as flagged in the original raw-SQL investigation, but checksum validation still recovers real, usable barcodes for the majority rather than rejecting the vendor outright. A row that fails the checksum (or has no/zero cost) is skipped, not faked.
- **`services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py`** (new): `AZURE_VENDOR_ACCOUNT_ID` maps our internal vendor names (`vo.VENDOR_NAMES`) to `dbo.vendor_accounts.id` (looked up live: Quality King Distributors=171, Cencora=45, Diamond Wholesale=173, BILO=187, Victory Wholesale Grocers=172). `fetch_vendor_rows(vname)` joins the 4 tables (filtered to `trashed_at IS NULL`, `discontinued_at IS NULL`, `currency_id=1`, not-yet-expired/already-activated), groups rows into `(stamp, rows)` batches by each row's real `updated_at` date (not today's date -- keeps the existing date-guarded merge-not-overwrite semantics meaningful for live data). `sync_vendor(vname)` / `sync_all()` feed those batches into the **existing** merge pipeline via a newly extracted `vo.merge_batches()` (refactored out of `vo.ingest_vendor_file` so file-upload and DB-sync share one code path -- no duplicated merge logic). Status persisted via the existing `settings` kv table (`database.get_setting/set_setting`), same pattern as other app settings.
- **Victory Wholesale Grocers is now syncable for the first time** -- it has no working file-upload parser (`vo.VENDOR_LOADERS` has no Victory loader, "no parseable offer file format yet"), but the DB has no such limitation, so this sync is the first way to get Victory's 7,629 active offers onto the desk at all.
- **Hourly scheduler** (`start_scheduler`/`stop_scheduler`/`scheduler_status`, daemon thread, mirrors `services/health_check.py`'s established pattern): fires immediately on app startup (confirmed live: `running_now:true` right after boot) then every `INTERVAL_SECONDS=3600`. Wired into `main.py`'s lifespan next to the existing health-check scheduler startup, gated on `azure_sql.is_configured()`.
- **Endpoints** (`routers/vendor_offers.py`): `GET /api/vendor-offers/db-sync/status` (scheduler state + last-run summary), `POST /api/vendor-offers/db-sync/run` (body `{vendor?}` -- omit for all 5, or name one). Both verified live: `sync_all()` for all 5 real vendors completed in **33.9s** (well under the ~41s the user already approved for the raw-SQL plan), `sync_vendor("Diamond Wholesale")` alone in 4s.
- **UI** (`static/price_desk.html`): a **"Sync from Database"** button in the main toolbar (next to Import/Export) runs `sync_all()` immediately and toasts a summary; the Import modal's "Vendor offer file" tab gained a matching "Sync now" button + a live status line ("...sync automatically every hour -- last sync <time>") reusing the existing `toast()` helper and the same `.m-out`/`.m-ghost` styling as the pre-existing "Sync from Pair Library" panel. Manual file upload is left in place (for vendors outside these 5, and as a fallback) but the status line makes clear the 5 DB vendors no longer need it.
- **Deliberately deferred per user's own instruction ("lets add the auto discover later, becaus ehte data on there is a bit messed up")**: auto-discovery of NEW vendor accounts beyond these 5 is not implemented -- `AZURE_VENDOR_ACCOUNT_ID` is a fixed, hand-verified map.
- Verified live end-to-end: full `sync_all()` run (33.9s, 0 errors), single-vendor run via the API, `db-sync/status` reflects `running_now`/`scheduler_alive`/`next_run`/per-vendor last-run counts, price_desk.html renders both new buttons + wired JS (checked via a throwaway smoke-test server on port 8001 -- the user's own dev server on 8000 was left untouched/unrestarted). Scope: `services/vendor_offers.py` (`merge_batches` extracted, `ingest_vendor_file` now a thin wrapper -- no behavior change for file uploads), `services/vendor_offers_db_sync.py` (new), `routers/vendor_offers.py`, `main.py`, `static/price_desk.html`. **Needs a backend restart** (the user's own running server) to pick up the new module/routes/scheduler + a browser hard-refresh for the UI.

**Changes this session (2026-09-22b) — Quick Search: surfaced dimensions/price/storage fee already sitting in the search response:**
- User: "when searching for asins in the roi & cost dont we already get info for these items like dimensions for example on the same api call?" ("ROI & Cost" = the sidebar label for the full Analytics matching run, `routers/analytics.py`, whose `_tier1_upc`/`_tier2_itemid`/`_tier3_title` Quick Search shares.)
- **Confirmed live, exactly right:** `search_by_identifiers`/`search_by_keywords` (default `included_data`) already return `item_dimensions`, `item_package_dimensions`, `item_package_weight`, `item_weight`, and `list_price` in the SAME response used for ASIN matching -- verified against a real UPC, `extract_dimensions()` parsed real values (4.39x4.60x10.69cm, 81.6g) straight out of it, no extra call.
- Also confirmed: the full "ROI & Cost" run's storage-fee enrichment (`services/analytics/eligibility_check.py`) ALREADY does this correctly -- its own docstring: "computed from the Amazon dimensions ALREADY stored ... no extra API call" (reused from `data_json.amazon._raw.attributes`, persisted per candidate). **But Quick Search -- transient, no DB write -- fetches the identical raw data and was discarding all of it**: `normalize_amazon_item()` keeps the full raw item as `_raw`, yet `analytics_quick_search`'s per-candidate result dict never read it for anything beyond title/brand/upc/etc.
- **FIX (`routers/analytics.py`):** each Quick Search candidate now also computes `list_price`, `length_in`/`width_in`/`height_in`/`weight_lb`, and `storage_fee_offpeak`/`storage_fee_peak` from that same already-fetched `_raw.attributes`, using the exact same `extract_dimensions`/`calc_storage_fee` helpers the ROI & Cost pipeline uses -- zero additional API calls. Verified live: real values for 2 of 3 candidates, correctly `None` for the one lacking dimension data (graceful, matches existing behavior elsewhere).
- **Frontend** (`static/index.html` + `static/app.js`): new "Price & Storage" column in the Quick Search results table, stacked list price / dimensions+weight / storage fee-per-month, using the same already-shipped data.
- Scope: `routers/analytics.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-22) — Quick Search: fixed a crash on every UPC-based search:**
- User: "update the way it [Quick Search] searches and matches asins to our updated version standard" + "if I add only a upc then only the upc should be searched for an asin." Traced "Quick Search" to `routers/analytics.py analytics_quick_search` (`POST /api/analytics/quick-search`) -- a transient single-item version of the full Analytics pipeline, sharing the same `_tier1_upc`/`_tier2_itemid`/`_tier3_title` search tiers and `calculate_confidence()` matcher from `services/analytics/runner.py` (not a forked/stale copy).
- **ROOT CAUSE, confirmed live:** `_tier1_upc()` was upgraded at some point to return `(matched, kw_unverified)` -- a 2-tuple splitting barcode-CONFIRMED UPC hits from Pass-4 keyword-fallback hits whose barcode does NOT match the searched UPC (kept for title/brand vetting without UPC credit, tagged "UPC-KW" -- lets a same-product listing under a different Amazon barcode surface for review instead of being silently dropped). The full Analytics run's caller (`runner.py` ~line 1691) was updated to unpack and tag both halves correctly. **Quick Search's caller was never updated** -- still did `tier1 = _tier1_upc(...)` then `.values()` on what is actually a tuple. Verified live: `analytics_quick_search({"upc": "074312025327"})` raised `AttributeError: 'tuple' object has no attribute 'values'` on every single UPC search -- a full crash (500), not a subtle bug.
- **FIX:** unpacks `tier1_matched, tier1_kw = _tier1_upc(...)`, tags `tier1_matched` as `"UPC"` (full UPC credit in scoring) and `tier1_kw` as `"UPC-KW"` (no UPC credit, still vetted) -- matches the full-run convention exactly. This also directly satisfies the "only the UPC should be searched" ask: both matched and kw_unverified paths are scoped to the UPC/barcode string itself (Pass 4's keyword fallback searches the UPC digits as the query, not title/brand) -- no title/brand tier is ever involved when only `upc` is provided, since `itemid`/`title` tiers are separately gated by `if itemid:`/`if title:` (already correct, untouched).
- **Verified live end-to-end:** UPC-only search now returns 3 real candidates, all correctly `sources=['UPC']`, no crash. ItemID-only search (a different, already-correct code path) still works. Missing-all-fields still correctly 400s.
- Scope: `routers/analytics.py` only (single function, `analytics_quick_search`). **Needs a backend restart** to take effect.

**Changes this session (2026-09-10b) — Create SKUs: flag a reused shadow whose SKU stem doesn't match the generated Main:**
- User noticed a real row: Main=MDLMSC811226, FBA=BAY811226_QY2-FBA (different prefix), FBM=MDLMSC811226_QY2-FBM (matches Main). Explained the mechanism first: an ASIN can only have ONE live FBA/FBM listing, so `_existing_shadow()` correctly REUSES an already-existing shadow rather than minting a duplicate -- the FBA shadow here was created earlier under an old brand/prefix ("BAY") and is reused as-is, while the FBM (no existing shadow found) got freshly minted under the CURRENT prefix ("MDLMSC"), hence the mismatch. Not a bug in the reuse logic itself, but a real signal worth a human glance -- user asked for exactly that: flag it and let them decide.
- **Implemented** (`services/create_po.py`): `RowResult` gained `fba_mismatch`/`fbm_mismatch: str | None` -- set in `derive_rows()` by comparing each reused shadow's stripped-to-main stem (`_strip_to_main`) against the row's actual generated Main SKU; `None` when they match or no shadow was reused. Included in `build_groups()`'s row dict.
- **Verified live** with a simulated CatalogIndex reproducing the exact reported scenario (Main=MDLMSC811226, existing FBA shadow BAY811226_QY2-FBA, no existing FBM shadow): `fba_mismatch` correctly fires with a clear message, `fbm_mismatch` correctly stays `None`.
- **Frontend** (`static/create_po.js`): a "⚠ Doesn't match Main" badge now appears on the FBA/FBM child-SKU cell when its channel is flagged, using the existing `modal-backdrop`/`modal-shell` CSS (already defined globally in `static/styles.css`, used elsewhere in the app) for a genuine modal per the user's ask -- shows the ASIN, brand, and the full explanation, with "Close" or "Edit \<channel\> SKU" (routes straight into the existing inline-cell-edit mechanism, so a correction flows through the same `edits` override path as any other manual SKU edit).
- Scope: `services/create_po.py`, `static/create_po.js`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-10) — Create SKUs: never surface a wholesale CASE quantity in the product title:**
- User's rule: "if a title specifies a case count, please dont add it in the title -- we need to always breakdown the lower UOM, like the box or eaches whatever is lower than CS." (e.g. '6/CS' means 6 units ship per wholesale case, NOT that the product is sold as a 6-pack.)
- Pulled real vendor descriptions from the actual incoming files (`data/vendor_incoming/`) to find genuine examples before writing anything: Quality King ('SEA BREEZE ORIGINAL 10 OZ CS/6', 'PEDIALYTE ... 8/CS', 'SOUR PATCH ... CS/288'), Cencora ('COMMODE 8450 3 IN 1 4/CASE NOVA', 'GILL LABS ... TRVL CASE G/S 6CT DS'), Diamond ('MAYB CS LL MGNTC MAUV 125' -- Maybelline "CS" line prefix, unrelated to case-pack).
- **Two-layer fix, both verified against the real examples above:**
  1. **`routers/create_po.py _apply_ai_titles`** (the primary path -- an AI/Haiku call that rewrites each vendor title into the clean `clean_name` used for the SKU's ProductName): added an explicit rule that CASE quantities ('CS/6', '6/CS', '4/CASE', 'Case of 12', '1X12 CS') are a wholesale shipping unit and must be DROPPED entirely, never turned into a count/pack phrase -- but a genuine SEPARATE smaller unit stated in the same title (e.g. a real per-box count) should be KEPT. Verified live: 'SEA BREEZE ORIGINAL 10 OZ CS/6' -> 'Sea Breeze Original 10 fluid ounces' (case dropped); "Q-TIPS 375'S CS/12" -> 'Q-Tips 375 Count' (case-of-12 dropped, but the genuine 375-swab count correctly kept -- the AI correctly told these apart); a control case with a genuine count and NO case wording ('BANDAGES ASSORTED 30 COUNT BX') was left untouched, confirming the rule doesn't over-suppress real counts.
  2. **`services/create_po.py _title_pack`** (the regex-based fallback, only used when `clean_name` isn't set -- i.e. no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` -- and the raw vendor title is used directly): a matched count/pack number is now discarded if 'case'/'CS' appears within a 10-character window of the NUMBER itself (not the whole matched phrase -- an earlier version checked proximity to the whole match, which wrongly vetoed a genuine separate pack phrase sitting near an unrelated case notation, e.g. "4/CASE pack of 6" should still find the 6). Window size (10) was picked empirically: tight enough that a real separate pack phrase isn't falsely rejected, wide enough to catch all 5 real case-notation examples pulled from actual vendor data, including the least obvious one ('...TRVL CASE G/S 6CT...', case word ~6 chars from the count). The scan then keeps looking for a genuine lower-UOM count elsewhere in the title if the case-adjacent one is discarded; if none exists, the existing "Each" default applies -- so this fallback path is coarser than the AI layer (raw shorthand like "375'S" isn't recognised as a count at all) but is safe-by-default: it can miss a real count, but can no longer leak a wrong case number.
- Scope: `routers/create_po.py`, `services/create_po.py`. **Needs a backend restart** to take effect.

**Changes this session (2026-09-09) — Check Brand: found + fixed dangerous false-positive resolutions using 48 real vendor brands as a test set:**
- User asked whether Check Brand spell-checks input, then supplied a real list of 48 toy/collectibles vendor brands and asked to (a) look up their approval status and (b) use them to build proper brand-name resolution ("spell check"), suggesting Keepa as a possible source.
- **Ran all 48 brands through the existing (2026-09-04i) resolver first** -- surfaced a serious problem: some resolutions were confidently WRONG. "Alpha Toys" resolved to "Siiziitoo" (2/20 coincidental votes -- "Alpha" alone matched 4,133 unrelated alphabet-toy/RC-helicopter/action-figure listings); "OUTFIT7" resolved to "Drag Racing Outfit7" (a random novelty t-shirt brand from only 3 total keyword matches, nothing to do with the real Outfit7/Talking Tom); "TIGERHEAD TOYS" resolved to "ZhoJee" (3/20, similarly scattered noise). Directly asking Claude the same question got these right (declined Alpha Toys/TIGERHEAD TOYS as genuinely unsure, correctly said "Outfit7") -- proving the resolver's own gate, not the underlying resolution problem, was the bug: it accepted whatever the top vote was with NO confidence check at all.
- **Tried Keepa's `discover_entities()` (services/keepa.py) as a replacement**, per user's suggestion -- it queries Keepa's STRUCTURED brand/manufacturer fields exactly rather than free text, so it's immune to keyword-noise (correctly found NOTHING for Alpha Toys/TIGERHEAD TOYS/the "Bodumm" typo, matching Claude's own honest uncertainty). But swapping it in as the ONLY resolver caused a real regression: 5 previously-correct resolutions (Buzz Bee Toys->Buzz Bee, Educa Borras->Educa, Micro Scooters International->Micro, NMR Distribution America->AQUARIUS, PlayShifu->Shifu) broke to 404, because Keepa needs an EXACT existing field value to seed from and can't bridge "close but not exact" naming (a vendor's own name with an extra generic word, e.g. "Buzz Bee Toys" vs Amazon's actual "Buzz Bee"). Also found Keepa itself isn't immune to a DIFFERENT failure mode: for "Alpha Toys" it confidently returned "Siku" -- a real, populous (894 total, 44% of its own sample), but completely UNRELATED brand.
- **Root insight, confirmed via `rapidfuzz` similarity scoring across all 9 calibration cases:** "Buzz Bee Toys"->"Buzz Bee" (correct) and "Alpha Toys"->"Siiziitoo" (wrong) have the IDENTICAL vote signature (2/20, 10%) -- vote count/fraction alone cannot separate them. The real difference is textual: "Buzz Bee" is a clean prefix of the query; "Siiziitoo" bears zero resemblance. But pure substring containment (`rapidfuzz.fuzz.partial_ratio`) isn't sufficient either -- "OUTFIT7" is fully contained in "Drag Racing Outfit7" too (partial_ratio 100, identical to the genuinely good cases) -- confirmed a LENGTH-RATIO floor (shorter/longer >= 0.4) is also required: Buzz Bee/Buzz Bee Toys = 0.62, Outfit7/Drag Racing Outfit7 = 0.37.
- **FINAL FIX (`routers/eligibility.py`):** both resolvers now return `(candidate, fraction)` instead of a bare string. A candidate is only tried if `fraction >= 0.5` OR `_related(query, candidate)` (partial_ratio >= 90 AND length-ratio >= 0.4) -- applied identically to the SP-API vote resolver AND the Keepa resolver, closing both of their distinct false-positive modes with one shared gate. `check_brand()` now tries SP-API-votes -> Keepa -> AI in order (not just "A or B"), and EVERY candidate from EVERY source (including AI, which has no fraction to gate on) must additionally clear `_MIN_VERIFIED_TOTAL = 5` real Amazon results under its own name before being trusted -- this is what catches "OUTFIT7" candidates outright regardless of source (verified totals: "Drag Racing Outfit7"=1, "Outfit7" itself=0, "Talking Tom Heroes: Suddenly Super" didn't clear the relevance gate either) -- correctly falls through to an honest 404 rather than any guess.
- **Verified live against all 11 calibration cases** (the 3 bad + 5 regressed + ColorStay/Bodum/Babor as baselines): all 8 real resolutions restored (Buzz Bee, Educa, Micro, AQUARIUS, Shifu, REVLON, + baselines), all 3 known-bad cases now correctly 404 instead of a wrong guess. **Re-ran the full 48-brand batch as the final validation** -- 1 RESTRICTED, 34 NEEDS_APPROVAL, 2 CAN_SELL, 7 MIXED (genuinely inconsistent across the 3-ASIN sample -- worth a larger sample or manual check), 4 NOT_FOUND (Alpha Toys, Beverly Hills Teddy Bear Co, OUTFIT7, TIGERHEAD TOYS -- the resolver correctly declined rather than guessing; a human needs to confirm these brands' real Amazon presence manually).
- Scope: `routers/eligibility.py` only (`_resolve_brand_spapi_votes` replaces the old ungated `_resolve_brand_mechanical`, `_resolve_brand_keepa` now returns a fraction too, new `_related()` helper, `_MIN_VERIFIED_TOTAL` constant, rewritten resolution loop in `check_brand()`). Added `from rapidfuzz import fuzz` import (already a project dependency elsewhere). **Needs a backend restart** to take effect.

**Changes this session (2026-09-07c) — Price Desk lookup-export: fixed false "too many identifiers" on a real vendor file:**
- User: uploading a vendor offer file with ~5,000 UPCs to the Price Desk's "price check a want-list" feature (`/api/vendor-offers/lookup-export`) hit "Too many identifiers (max 20,000)". ROOT CAUSE: the "Choose a csv/txt instead" file-upload path (`static/price_desk.html`) sends the ENTIRE raw file text through unchanged, no column extraction -- and `parse_identifiers()` (`services/vendor_offers_export.py`) tokenized on ANY whitespace/comma/semicolon/pipe, so every word of every product description, every cost, every qty in a real multi-column vendor file became its own bogus "identifier". ~5,000 real UPC rows with a handful of other columns each easily exceeds 20,000 fake tokens this way.
- **FIX:** `parse_identifiers()` is now column-aware. Multiple lines forming a consistent multi-column shape (comma or tab delimited) are treated as real tabular data: the identifier column is picked by header name (UPC/ASIN/Barcode/SKU/GTIN/EAN/Item#) when present, else by which column's values are mostly UPC-shaped (6-14 digits) or ASIN-shaped (`B0` + 8 alnum). A single line (even one mixing commas and spaces as casual separators) still falls back to the original simple whitespace/comma/tab/pipe tokenizer -- column-extraction only triggers for genuinely multi-row input, since a lone ambiguous line can't reliably be told apart from a real 2-column CSV row otherwise.
- **Verified** against a header'd CSV, a header-less TSV (UPC in a non-first column), the original simple one-per-line/mixed-delimiter list (regression-checked), and a realistic 5,000-row/5-column synthetic vendor file -- correctly extracts exactly 5,000 identifiers (previously would have produced ~25,000 and hit the cap).
- Scope: `services/vendor_offers_export.py` only (`parse_identifiers`, its only caller is `routers/vendor_offers.py`'s `lookup_export`). **Needs a backend restart** to take effect.

**Changes this session (2026-09-07b) — Tools panel: fixed Download CSV button stranded below Check Brand:**
- User: the ASIN eligibility/storage/generic "Download CSV" button was sitting at the very bottom, below the whole Check Brand section. Cause: the 2026-09-04i reorder (moved the progress/stats bars above Check Brand, per that session's request) moved the bars but left `#tools-export-btn` sitting in its original DOM position, after the Check Brand block -- separating it from the stats it belongs to.
- **FIX:** moved `#tools-export-btn` (static/index.html) to sit directly after `tools-progress-section`'s closing tag, before Check Brand -- restores Run button -> progress bars/stats -> Download CSV -> Check Brand.
- Scope: `static/index.html` only. **No backend restart needed** -- hard-refresh the browser tab.

**Changes this session (2026-09-07) — Fixed a VM deploy crash: missing tzdata dependency:**
- User pulled the 2026-09-04 commit onto a VM and got `zoneinfo.ZoneInfoNotFoundError: No time zone found with key America/New_York` on startup. ROOT CAUSE: `services/health_check.py` (committed 2026-09-04, part of the Vendor Offer Analytics batch) runs `ZoneInfo("America/New_York")` at MODULE IMPORT time for its weekly scheduler, and `main.py` imports it unconditionally -- so this fires on every app startup, everywhere.
- Windows (and some minimal Linux images) ship no system IANA timezone database, so Python's `zoneinfo` needs the `tzdata` PyPI package as a fallback data source. It happened to work on the dev machine only because `tzdata` was ALREADY installed there as a transitive dependency of another package (`pip show tzdata` -> "Required-by: Faker, pandas") -- not because catalog-verifier's own `requirements.txt` ever declared it. The VM's dependency resolution didn't pull it in the same way, so it was genuinely absent there.
- **FIX:** added `tzdata>=2024.1` directly to `requirements.txt` (after the pandas/xlrd block). **Immediate unblock on the VM:** `pip install tzdata` right now (don't need to wait for a full reinstall), or re-run `pip install -r requirements.txt` to pick up the pinned version.
- Scope: `requirements.txt` only.

**Changes this session (2026-09-04j) — Bulk Check Brand: fixed silently stopping after exactly 2 brands:**
- User: "for some reason it only checks 2 and then cancels." Traced it to a real double-handler bug in `static/app.js`: the Check-Brand button had a PERMANENT `addEventListener("click", _checkBrands)` set up once at page load, but `_checkBrands()` ALSO reassigns `btn.onclick = ...` at runtime (to a Cancel handler while a bulk run is in progress, then back to `_checkBrands` when done) -- `.onclick` assignment does NOT replace a handler added via `addEventListener`; both fire on every click.
- **Mechanism, confirmed by tracing the exact reported symptom (2 = the CONCURRENCY constant):** a second click on the button during a bulk run -- the button now reads "Cancel" -- fires BOTH handlers for that one click. The addEventListener's `_checkBrands` runs first (its synchronous setup resets `_brandCheckState.cancel = false` and launches a fresh pool of 2 concurrent workers, each already past their first `await` before yielding back to the browser), THEN the onclick handler for that same click fires and sets `cancel = true`. The 2 in-flight requests (matching `CONCURRENCY = 2`) still complete and get recorded, but every worker's loop then sees `cancel === true` on its next iteration and stops -- so it always halts at exactly 2, regardless of how many brands were queued.
- **FIX:** removed the `addEventListener` entirely; the button now uses ONLY `.onclick`, set once at setup to `_checkBrands` -- every later reassignment (Cancel handler, then back to `_checkBrands`) now correctly REPLACES the single handler instead of stacking a second one alongside it.
- Scope: `static/app.js` only, no backend change. **No backend restart needed** -- hard-refresh the browser tab.

**Changes this session (2026-09-04i) — Check Brand: adjustable sample size (count or %), reordered panel, bulk upload, and a real brand-name-resolution fix:**
- User asked for 3 things: (1) a settings control for how many/what % of ASINs to sample, (2) move the eligibility/storage/generic progress+stats section ABOVE Check Brand in the panel, (3) bulk upload for Check Brand (multiple brands at once).
- **(1) Sample-size settings** (`routers/eligibility.py` + `static/index.html` + `static/app.js`): `CheckBrandBody` gained `sample_pct: float | None` alongside `sample_size` -- when `sample_pct` is set it takes precedence, computed as `round(total_amazon_results * pct/100)` (total learned from the search's first page before deciding the cap, no double-fetch). Both modes share one hard safety cap `BRAND_CHECK_MAX = 300` (up from the old fixed 25) so a runaway request/huge brand cannot blow up API usage. UI: a "Check: [count] / [%]" radio toggle in the Tools panel, backed by two number inputs (the inactive one greyed out).
- **(2) Reordered panel**: moved the whole `tools-progress-section` block (eligibility/storage/generic bars+stats) to sit right after the Run button, with the Check Brand section now below it -- was previously the reverse.
- **(3) Bulk upload**: `#brand-check-input` is now a multi-line textarea (one brand per line, comma-tolerant for pasted CSV rows) + an "Upload list..." file button (.txt/.csv) that appends into the textarea. `_checkBrands()` (renamed from `_checkBrand`) parses the list, and for 2+ brands runs them through a 2-concurrent-worker pool hitting the SAME `/api/eligibility/check-brand` endpoint per brand (no new bulk backend endpoint -- reuses the single-brand one), rendering a growing results table live as each completes, with a Cancel button and a "Download CSV" export once done. A single brand still shows the original one-box result view.
- **(4) Found + fixed a real accuracy bug while testing (1)+(3)**: re-tested ColorStay (used as the running example in earlier sessions) and got a false 404 "no ASINs found" -- traced it live: Amazon's actual catalog `brand` attribute for those ASINs is **"REVLON"**, not "ColorStay" (ColorStay is a Revlon product-line name that only appears in the title). The `brand_names`-filtered search only matches Amazon's own canonical brand field, so a sub-brand/product-line name silently returns nothing. Tried a plain keyword-only search as a naive fix -- too noisy (2,213 results for "ColorStay", didn't even surface a known ASIN on page 1) -- not usable alone.
  - **FIX (`_resolve_brand_mechanical` + `_resolve_brand_ai` in `routers/eligibility.py`)**: on a 0-result direct search, first try a FREE mechanical resolution -- run the plain keyword search anyway, but instead of using its ASINs directly, tally the ACTUAL `brand` attribute values Amazon returns on those matches (verified live: "REVLON" won 17/20 votes for "ColorStay") and re-search using that resolved name. Only if mechanical resolution ALSO finds nothing does it fall back to asking Claude (**Sonnet**, per user's explicit request -- "sonnet by default... wouldn't take much tokens", distinct from the Haiku already used elsewhere in this file for bulk brand/manufacturer guessing) what the real Amazon brand name is; cached per brand, best-effort, never breaks the check on failure. The resolved name (if any) is echoed back as `resolved_as` and folded into `summary` ("...resolved to Amazon brand \"REVLON\"...").
- **Verified live end-to-end**: ColorStay -> resolves to REVLON (19.1s, mechanical only, no AI call needed), 5/5 sampled RESTRICTED, `total_asins_in_catalog` correctly jumps to Revlon's real 1,356-ASIN Amazon catalog (not the 6 SellerCloud-tracked ones). A genuinely nonexistent brand still correctly 404s after both resolution attempts fail. 5%-of-Bodum (~497 ASINs) sample-pct mode verified earlier at 25/25 checked, matches 5% x 497 rounded.
- Scope: `routers/eligibility.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-04h) — Check Brand: dropped the SellerCloud/Azure dependency entirely (user: "wait why are we looking up sellercloud?"):**
- User ran "Check Brand" for Babor and it appeared to hang. Checked live: NOT frozen -- their server had 2 genuine established TCP connections to Azure SQL (port 1433) at that moment, actively pulling the ~182k-row live catalog view (first catalog-touching request since their restart, so the 30-min in-memory cache was cold). That pull has been observed to take 1-3+ min even with VPN on -- but the user's underlying question was sharper: why does an AMAZON gating check need OUR SellerCloud data at all?
- **Answer: it never did.** `getListingsRestrictions` answers "can this seller list THIS ASIN" for ANY Amazon ASIN, ours or not -- SellerCloud/Azure was only ever used to get a list of ASINs for the brand, which is exactly what SP-API's own Catalog Items search does natively, with no Azure/VPN dependency at all.
- Tried the obvious `search_by_brand_names()` (already in `services/spapi/catalog.py`, brandNames-only) first -- Amazon rejects it: 400 `"Missing required 'identifiers' or 'keywords'"`, despite the function's own docstring claiming brandNames works standalone. The WORKING call (also already in this codebase) is `search_by_keywords(keywords=brand, brand_names=[brand])` -- verified live for Babor: 8.2s, 128 total Amazon results.
- **FIX:** rewrote `POST /api/eligibility/check-brand` (`routers/eligibility.py`) to find the brand's ASINs via `search_by_keywords(...)` (paginated, up to 6 pages) instead of `azure_sql.asins_for_brand()` -- removed that now-dead function from `services/azure_sql.py` entirely, and dropped the now-pointless `company` field from the request (Amazon's own catalog isn't scoped by our internal company, and the bulk `/eligibility/check` endpoint never had one either). Frontend (`static/app.js _checkBrand()`) updated to match the response shape (no more `r.company`).
- **Verified live end-to-end, timed:** `check_brand("Babor")` -> **39.1s total** (vs. minutes-to-hanging before), 128 Amazon ASINs found, 8/8 sampled RESTRICTED (not accepting applications) -- Babor is genuinely brand-gated for this account.
- Scope: `routers/eligibility.py`, `services/azure_sql.py` (removed function), `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** + browser hard-refresh.

**Changes this session (2026-09-04g) — New "Check Brand" tool: brand-wide gating check:**
- User asked: is there a way to quickly see if we're gated brand-wide, rather than checking one ASIN at a time? Researched Amazon's SP-API surface: there is NO brand-level restrictions endpoint -- `getListingsRestrictions` is strictly per-ASIN (+ condition/marketplace); Seller Central's brand-approval UI pages aren't exposed via SP-API at all.
- **But the restriction message itself is inherently brand-wide** ("You are not approved to list this brand...", not listing-specific) -- verified live: all 4 non-DOG ASINs of a known-gated brand (ColorStay, 6 total ASINs, 2 of them DOG) came back IDENTICALLY RESTRICTED/NOT_ELIGIBLE. So a brand-wide answer doesn't need a brand-level Amazon API -- checking a handful of the brand's live ASINs (skipping DOG ones, which say nothing about the brand) reliably answers it.
- **Built as a real feature** (not just explained manually), per user's choice of a dedicated "Check Brand" tool:
  - `services/azure_sql.py asins_for_brand(brand, company)` -- brand -> distinct ASINs from the live SellerCloud catalog (exact case-insensitive match preferred, falls back to substring match).
  - `routers/eligibility.py POST /api/eligibility/check-brand` -- takes `{brand, company, sample_size=8}`, pulls that brand's ASINs, checks up to `sample_size` non-DOG ones (up to 3x that many attempted, to skip past DOG), and returns ONE verdict: CAN_SELL / NEEDS_APPROVAL / RESTRICTED (uniform) / MIXED (genuinely inconsistent results) / ALL_DOG (every sampled ASIN was dead -- inconclusive, raise sample_size). Still only the 3 real statuses per-ASIN; the verdict just rolls them up.
  - Frontend: new "Check Brand (gating)" box in the Tools panel (`static/index.html` + `static/app.js _checkBrand()`) -- type a brand, get one colour-coded verdict instead of per-ASIN rows.
- **Verified live end-to-end**: `check_brand("ColorStay")` -> 4/4 checked RESTRICTED (NOT_ELIGIBLE), matches the manual sample from the 2026-09-04e/f investigation.
- Scope: `services/azure_sql.py`, `routers/eligibility.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (Python) + browser hard-refresh (JS/HTML).

**Changes this session (2026-09-04f) — Eligibility: broke DOG out from RESTRICTED as its own bucket:**
- Follow-on from 2026-09-04e: user confirmed `ASIN_NOT_FOUND` = a deactivated ASIN, same DOG concept storage-fees/generic-check already use -- verified live (both sample ASINs 404 on the catalog API too, the exact signal those other tools use for DOG).
- **FIX:** `classify()` (`services/restrictions.py`) now returns a top-level `"dog": bool` flag (true iff any reason has `code == "ASIN_NOT_FOUND"`) alongside the existing 3-value `status` -- status is UNCHANGED (still exactly CAN_SELL/NEEDS_APPROVAL/RESTRICTED, dog is a sub-flag on RESTRICTED, not a 4th status, per user's "we technically only need the 3 statuses"). `routers/eligibility.py`'s two exception fallbacks got `"dog": False` added for schema consistency.
- Frontend (`static/index.html` + `static/app.js`): added a DOG badge to the Tools-panel eligibility stats (matching the existing generic-check DOG badge styling); `_toolsUpdateEligStats` now splits RESTRICTED into `rest` (genuine restriction) vs `dog` counts; CSV export shows `"RESTRICTED (DOG)"` instead of plain `"RESTRICTED"` for those rows.
- Scope: `services/restrictions.py`, `routers/eligibility.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`. **Needs a backend restart** (Python change) + browser hard-refresh (JS/HTML change).

**Changes this session (2026-09-04e) — Eligibility: fixed RESTRICTED items misclassified as NEEDS_APPROVAL (RevSeller research):**
- User: RevSeller shows B002SV32VI as RESTRICTED; our tool shows NEEDS_APPROVAL -- asked why RevSeller is more accurate. Pulled the RAW SP-API `getListingsRestrictions` response live for this ASIN: `reasonCode: "NOT_ELIGIBLE"`, message `"You are not approved to list this brand and we are currently not accepting applications."`, **`links: []`** (empty -- Amazon is NOT offering a self-serve request action). This is a genuine hard block; RevSeller reads it correctly.
- **TWO compounding bugs in `services/restrictions.py classify()`:**
  1. `can_request` checked `link.get("verb") == "REQUEST_APPROVAL"` -- but Amazon's real `verb` field is the HTTP method (`"GET"`), never that string. Sampled 20 live ASINs + the reported one: EVERY reason, approvable or not, has `verb: "GET"` when a link exists. **`can_request` was DEAD CODE, always False, for every ASIN ever checked** by this tool.
  2. Reason "type" was guessed by keyword-matching the free-text `message` (`_detect_reason_type`) -- `"brand" in msg` labelled this NOT_ELIGIBLE/no-link case as `BRAND_RESTRICTION`, which is hardcoded into `APPROVABLE_TYPES`. Since (1) made `can_request` useless, status fell through entirely to this text-guessed type, so a hard "not accepting applications" block got the exact same treatment as a real open brand-approval request -- both said "brand restriction" in the message, but only one has an actual path.
- **Sampled 20 random ASINs from the local scan cache to find the real pattern:** `reasonCode="APPROVAL_REQUIRED"` ALWAYS came with a real `links` entry (a working `sellercentral.amazon.com/hz/approvalrequest/...` URL); `reasonCode="NOT_ELIGIBLE"` ALWAYS came with `links: []`. Amazon's own `reasonCode` is the authoritative, structured signal -- no need to guess from text.
- **FIX:** (a) `can_request` now = `bool(links)` (a link's mere presence, not a specific verb string, since Amazon only populates it when a real action exists) and `approval_url` reads Amazon's actual `links[0]["resource"]` directly; (b) reason `type` now prefers Amazon's `reasonCode` via a small `_REASON_CODE_TYPE` map (`APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE` catalogued so far), falling back to the old message-keyword heuristic only for uncatalogued codes; (c) overall status is now driven solely by `can_request` (real link presence), not the text-guessed type. Also now returns the raw `code` field in each reason for future debugging. Checked no frontend/export code depends on the specific old "type" strings (only `status` is read for badges) -- safe to change.
- **Verified live, end-to-end:** `B002SV32VI` and `B0754LMN5B` (both previously misreported as NEEDS_APPROVAL, same NOT_ELIGIBLE/empty-links shape) now correctly return **RESTRICTED**. Three known-good approvable ASINs (`B00NWVGSX2`, `B00JY4A30U`, `B09643WCGM`, reasonCode=APPROVAL_REQUIRED) still correctly return **NEEDS_APPROVAL**, now with Amazon's real approval URL instead of a guessed one.
- Scope: `services/restrictions.py` only. **Needs a backend restart** (user's own terminal) to take effect. Only 2 reasonCodes catalogued so far (APPROVAL_REQUIRED, NOT_ELIGIBLE) -- an uncatalogued future code still falls back to the old text-heuristic for its "type" label, but status now always correctly follows real link presence regardless.
- **User asked "do you need more samples to make it better?"** Answered directly: the STATUS decision (CAN_SELL/NEEDS_APPROVAL/RESTRICTED) is structurally correct regardless of sample size now -- it's driven by actual `links` presence, not a lookup table, so no amount of sampling changes its correctness. More sampling only helps catalog additional `reasonCode` values for nicer/clearer "type" labels (cosmetic, not correctness). Tested this by sampling 103 more live random ASINs (81 CAN_SELL, 16 APPROVAL_REQUIRED, 6 NOT_ELIGIBLE) and found exactly that: one new code, **`ASIN_NOT_FOUND`** ("ASIN does not exist in this marketplace") -- a dead/delisted ASIN, not a brand gate. Status was already correctly RESTRICTED for it; added it to `_REASON_CODE_TYPE` so it's labelled clearly instead of the generic fallback `"RESTRICTION"`.

**Changes this session (2026-09-04d) — Create SKUs purchaser: found + fixed the REAL root cause (2026-09-04c's fix was necessary but not sufficient):**
- Azure SQL firewall was rejecting this machine's IP mid-session ("Client with IP address '81.88.144.147' is not allowed") -- user's VPN fixed it (confirmed live: 182,144 rows pulled). While Azure was unreachable, `routers/create_po.py _catalog_source()` was silently falling back to the LOCAL snapshot: `ci.local_index()` + `brand_map.get_map()` (the `brand_mappings` SQLite table, populated by a one-time old Excel import, NOT live Azure) -- this fallback is almost certainly what the user was actually hitting when they first saw NIVEA's purchaser as "Other".
- **Checked live Azure NIVEA data (34 SKUs):** raw purchaser distribution is `VictoriaV: 29` vs `Other: 5` -- VictoriaV was ALREADY the majority. So 2026-09-04c's mode-exclusion fix, while correct and worth keeping, was NOT what actually explains the bug for NIVEA (it would have picked VictoriaV either way once read from live Azure).
- **REAL root cause found:** `brand_mappings` (the stale fallback table) has a literal row `NIVEA -> purchaser='Other'` from a much older import timestamp than the live-built `sc_manufacturer` table (which already correctly has Beiersdorf Inc. -> VictoriaV). In `resolve_brands()` (`services/create_po.py`), `purchaser = _s(mp.get("purchaser"))` reads that stale `"Other"` string -- which is TRUTHY in Python -- so the later `if not purchaser: purchaser = mref.get("purchaser", "")` fallback to the correct, fresher `sc_manufacturer` value NEVER FIRES. A literal cached "Other" permanently locks out the correct answer, independent of any vote/mode logic.
- **FIX:** added a `_real()` helper in `resolve_brands()` that normalises a mapping-derived `"Other"` to blank (mirrors the vote-exclusion already added to `azure_sql.brand_map()`/`sc_reference.py`/`brand_map.py`) BEFORE the truthy check, for both `purchaser` and `sourcer` pulled from `mp`. An explicit user override of "Other" is still respected as-is (only the auto-resolved mapping value is normalised). **Verified end-to-end**: fed `resolve_brands()` the exact stale local table (`mapping.get("nivea") == {'purchaser': 'Other', ...}`) and it now correctly resolves to `purchaser: VictoriaV@fordmed.com`.
- Scope: `services/create_po.py` only. **Needs a backend restart** to take effect (user has their own terminal running `main.py` now -- this Python change needs that process restarted, unlike the earlier pure-JS progress-bar fix).

**Changes this session (2026-09-04c) — Create SKUs: fixed "Other" placeholder winning the purchaser field:**
- User: NIVEA's prefix (BSF) was correct, but the assigned Purchaser came back as "Other" -- checked SellerCloud directly and an actual NIVEA SKU (BSF024666) has Purchaser = VictoriaV@fordmed.com. ROOT CAUSE: `services/azure_sql.py brand_map()` (the LIVE mapping `_resolve_brands()` in `services/create_po.py` actually uses -- confirmed via `routers/create_po.py _catalog_source()` -> `azure_sql.catalog_source()`) picks the **mode** (most-common) `Purchaser` value across **every** SKU ever recorded for that brand, active or long-dead. `"Other"` is a real SellerCloud placeholder value for "unassigned" (per `services/brand_map.py fordmed_email()`, which already special-cases it to not append `@fordmed.com`), not a person -- but it was never excluded from the vote the way blank/`"0"` already are, so a brand with enough old unassigned SKUs has "Other" outvote the real, currently-assigned purchaser.
- **FIX:** excluded `"Other"` (case-insensitive) from the purchaser/sourcer tally in all 3 places that do this same mode-aggregation: `services/azure_sql.py brand_map()` (the live path Create SKUs actually reads), `services/sc_reference.py _build_company()` (the manufacturer-level fallback used when a brand has no purchaser yet), and `services/brand_map.py import_from_sku_extended()` (the older Excel-import path, fixed for consistency). Same pattern as the existing `!= "0"` exclusion. If a brand's ONLY purchaser values are "Other"/blank, the field now comes back empty (prompting the user to fill it in) rather than silently wrong.
- Scope: 3 Python files, no schema change. `azure_sql.brand_map()` is computed live (in-memory, 30-min TTL, not persisted) so a **backend restart** picks up the fix immediately with no rebuild step; the `sc_reference.py` SQLite fallback tables (`sc_manufacturer`) still hold pre-fix values until their next `build_reference()` rebuild -- lower priority since the brand-level mapping supplies purchaser first. **Backend restarted** (fresh `python main.py`, replacing the orphaned process from earlier).
- NOT YET verified against live Azure data in this session (no Create SKUs run against NIVEA was re-executed) -- logic reviewed against the exact code path confirmed to be live; flag if NIVEA (or another brand) still shows "Other" after this.

_Last updated: 2026-09-04_

**Changes this session (2026-09-04b) — Tools panel: fixed progress bars permanently freezing mid-run:**
- User: "when I hit Check eligibility and storage fees, the progress bar for some reason never progresses so it always stays at one point even though the whole research has been completed." ROOT CAUSE: `_toolsPoll()` in `static/app.js` polls each of the 3 sub-jobs (eligibility, storage-fees, generic-check) independently, and on **any** poll error (a single dropped connection, one transient non-2xx) did `catch { t.sfDone = true; }` (etc.) — permanently marking that sub-job done and **stopping polling forever**, with no error shown. The backend job (`routers/eligibility.py` / `routers/storage_fees.py`) keeps running and finishes correctly (their `done`/`total` counters are fine) but the frontend had already given up listening, so the bar just froze at whatever % it was on when the one bad poll hit.
- **FIX:** added per-job fail counters (`t.eligFails`/`t.sfFails`/`t.genFails`, reset to 0 in `_startToolsRun` and after every successful poll). A poll error now just increments the counter and the loop keeps retrying next tick (already scheduled via the trailing `setTimeout(_toolsPoll, 1000)`); only after `_TOOLS_POLL_MAX_FAILS` (5) **consecutive** failures does it give up — and now it visibly shows "⚠ lost connection" on that bar's label + a toast, instead of silently freezing.
- Scope: `static/app.js` only (`state.tools` init block + the 3 poll blocks in `_toolsPoll`), no backend change. **No backend restart needed** — hard-refresh the browser tab to pick up the new JS.
- NOT YET independently verified against a live flaky-connection repro (couldn't force a transient poll failure in this session); logic reviewed line-by-line and is symmetric across all 3 sub-jobs. Flag if the bar still freezes after this — would mean the failure isn't a poll-fetch error (worth checking Windows dev-server behavior under concurrent load, e.g. the 2-worker storage-fees ThreadPoolExecutor sharing the event loop with polling GETs).

**Changes this session (2026-09-04) — Tools ASIN-lookup: storage-fee cache added; explained the eligibility "2 different answers":**
- **User Q1 — "why does eligibility give 2 different answers 2 different times?"** Compared the user's two `asin_lookup` CSV exports (9/3 = 531 ASINs, 9/4 = 100; 100 common): **only 1/100 differed in eligibility** (`B0754LMN5B` CAN_SELL→NEEDS_APPROVAL) and **1/100 in storage** (`B01K7MW3SM` 0.0543→0.0652). BOTH are **genuine Amazon-side changes, not bugs**: live re-check showed B0754LMN5B is now `NEEDS_APPROVAL` reason **BRAND_RESTRICTION** ("You are not approved to list this brand and we are currently not accepting applications") — Amazon gated the brand between the two days; B01K7MW3SM's **package dimensions changed** (now 2.17×7.44×8.94 in / 1.08 lb → 0.0652). `classify()` (services/restrictions.py) is deterministic given Amazon's response (empty `restrictions`→CAN_SELL, else NEEDS_APPROVAL/RESTRICTED), so eligibility is a **live, time-varying snapshot** of the seller's current gating — which is exactly why the user wants it never cached. Nothing to fix.
- **User Q2 — "the storage cache isn't working, same ASINs still take ages."** ROOT CAUSE: the storage cache built earlier (2026-09-02b) lives ONLY in the Offer-Analytics **export** path (`vendor_offers_template_export.enrich_asins`). The **Tools ASIN-lookup** uses a DIFFERENT path — `routers/storage_fees.py` → `get_by_asin` per ASIN at **2 workers** (getCatalogItem 2 req/s) — which had **no cache at all**, so every lookup re-fetched everything (531 ASINs ≈ minutes, every time). **FIX:** added a shared 60-day storage cache. New `services/storage_fees.py` helpers `storage_cache_get(asins)` / `storage_cache_put(results)` + `_ensure_cache_table` — they **extend the existing `storage_fee_cache` table with a `data_json` column** (holds the full per-ASIN result: fees, dims, tier, DOG) while still writing the `storage_fee` column the export reads, so both features now share ONE cache. `routers/storage_fees.py` `run()` now seeds cached rows instantly (`from_cache: True`, counts toward done), fetches ONLY the uncached ASINs, then `storage_cache_put`s the fresh ones. Only `ok`/`no_dimensions`/`dog` statuses are cached — transient `error` rows are never cached (so they retry). **Eligibility stays 100% live** (separate `routers/eligibility.py`, never cached — per the user's requirement). Verified end-to-end via the live endpoint: 6 ASINs cold=5.1s (0 cached) → repeat=1.0s (6/6 from_cache). **Backend restarted** (server serves the cache). NOTE: the generic-check 3rd column is still uncached (user didn't flag it); storage TTL is 60 days (bi-monthly) so an Amazon dims change is only picked up after the TTL or a cache clear.

**Changes this session (2026-09-03) — Offer Analytics export: fixed the live-Keepa CRASH (ChunkedEncodingError), added export ETA + phase, confirmed exact-ASIN scoping:**
- **ROOT CAUSE of the crash:** `keepa.fetch_products` requested **`offers=20`**, which ships every offer object per ASIN (~50 KB each). At 100 ASINs/call the responses were ~5 MB and broke mid-download → `requests.exceptions.ChunkedEncodingError`, killing the user's live QK export. **FIX:** dropped `offers` and switched to Keepa's **`buybox=1`** parameter (`services/keepa.py._call_product`), which returns the Buy-Box columns we actually need — `stats.buyBoxPrice` (current BB), `buyBoxSellerId`, `buyBoxStats` (seller-share %) and the avg30/avg90 index-18 prices — in a **small ~5 KB/ASIN** payload. `current_buybox_usd` now reads `stats.buyBoxPrice` (falls back to `current[18]`); `buybox_seller_id` = `buyBoxSellerId` else the dominant 90d winner from `_bb_window` (which now returns a 3-tuple `(top_seller_id, top_pct, amazon_pct)`). Also hardened earlier: retry on **any `requests.RequestException`** (not just Timeout/ConnectionError), **timeout 120s**. With small payloads `_PRODUCT_BATCH` restored to **100**.
- **Measured (live, 100 ASINs):** 17.5s, ~708 tokens (~7/ASIN), **no crash**; Buy Box populated for every ASIN that has one (dead/no-BB listings correctly blank). **ETA for a full ~1,896-row QK export: ~5–6 min of Keepa** (~13k tokens; the 12,600 bucket + 210/min refill means a little token-wait near the end), plus the SP-API eligibility/storage enrichment. Verified end-to-end: a 3-UPC live export writes real BB data (Systane $19.48 seller Amazon.com rank 408; Neutrogena $6.49; Dakin's $19.82 seller Honest Medical) — no `offers`, no crash.
- **ETA + phase on the progress** (already added, verified): router `_progress(done, total, phase)` stamps `phase_started` when the phase changes; `GET …/jobs/{id}` returns `eta_seconds` computed within the current phase (`phase_started`). `build_template_xlsx` passes phase-labelled progress wrappers — **"Keepa live prices"** then **"eligibility check"**. Frontend export button shows `Cancel · <phase> <pct>% (done/total) · ~Ns/m left`.
- **ALL ASINs per UPC (reverted the 2026-09-01c one-per-UPC), scoped to the filtered set** — user: "I NEED ALL ASINS PER THE UPC … find the keepa data only for the filtered items (QK + cheapest + on-hand qty)." `build_template_xlsx` now expands each kept UPC to **every** ASIN (Pair-Library assignments ∪ Keepa-barcode): `chosen = {upc: asins_for(upc)}` (a LIST), `chosen_asins = sorted(union)`, and the records loop emits **one row per (UPC, ASIN)**. The QK filtered area (focus=Quality King cheapest/sole + `only_available` + `has_asin`) = **2,022 UPCs → 6,619 distinct ASINs** (avg 3.3 ASINs/UPC, max 17). Only those ASINs are pulled from Keepa/SP-API — nothing outside the filter. Verified: 2 multi-ASIN QK UPCs (6+8) → exactly 14 rows, each ASIN with its own live BB.
- **The 6,619 is REAL and expected** — breakdown of the QK filtered area: **2,022 UPCs**, of which **1,896 have a Pair-Library ASSIGNED ASIN** and 126 get ASINs only from `keepa_barcodes`. Distinct ASINs = **6,619**: **6,289 from the user's own Pair-Library assignments** (avg ~3.3 assigned ASINs/UPC — e.g. one NIVEA UPC → 6 re-listings of the same 6.8oz creme), only 626 from `keepa_barcodes` (296 overlap). So "all ASINs per UPC" genuinely expands 2,022 UPCs → 6,619 ASINs; it is NOT barcode noise. **Of those 6,619, only ~1,446 have a SellerCloud SKU** (the rest are ASINs matched to the UPC that Ford doesn't carry) — a candidate tighter filter if the user only wants actionable rows.
- **User decisions (2026-09-03):** (1) KEEP both Keepa calls — "I need all the data" (take the asin-data repo approach; 30d AND 90d share columns stay). (2) Eligibility/Approvals **always LIVE, never cached**; only **storage** is cached (60-day) — this is already the behavior: `enrich_asins` runs `getListingsRestrictions` fresh for every ASIN when `live=True` (the `elig_cache`/health-check value is only a fallback when a live call returns nothing); storage seeds from `storage_fee_cache`.
- **Verification export delivered:** `build_template_xlsx(upcs=None, focus_vendor="Quality King Distributors", has_asin=True, only_available=True, live=False)` → **QK_no_keepa_verify.xlsx**, 6,626 rows, all NON-Keepa data filled (SKU 1,446 / OR33 1,435 / FBA total 684 / last cost 321 / available qty on every row), Keepa columns blank. Built in ~6 min — that time is the 4 Azure `LTRIM(RTRIM())` view scans (sku_data/FBA inv/SellerSnap/purchases_view), NOT Keepa. **Honest full-LIVE estimate for all 6,619:** ~6 min Azure + ~2.5-2.8 hr Keepa (token-bucket-limited, both calls) + ~2.5 hr live eligibility (429-throttled, 5 workers) ≈ **~5 hr**; Cancel button + per-phase ETA are wired for it. Repeat runs save the getCatalogItem half via the storage cache but Keepa + eligibility still run fresh.
- **Backend restarted** (PID 7920) so the running server serves the fix; `/api/vendor-offers/status` healthy (4 vendors, 18,818 offers). NOTE: `_bb_window` return signature changed to a 3-tuple — both `_flatten_product` unpackings updated. `_call_sellers` still resolves BB seller names.

**Changes this session (2026-09-01c) — Offer Analytics export: ONE ASIN per UPC (not full expansion) + cancel-on-second-click:**
- The UPC×ASIN union from 2026-09-01b exploded row/enrich counts (1,760 QK items → 5,626 ASINs → ~5h live ETA). User: "only need the cheapest exported." Reverted to **ONE row per UPC**: `build_template_xlsx` now computes candidate ASINs (keepa ∪ assigned), pulls Keepa for all candidates (fast Azure query), then picks the **dominant ASIN per UPC** (`best_asin`: prefers one with Keepa data, then highest `monthly_sold_quantity`), and **only enriches those chosen ASINs**. Verified: full QK build = **1,735 rows** (1,735 distinct UPCs), so live enrichment does ~1,735 SP-API pairs, not 5,626.

New route **`POST /api/vendor-offers/analytics-export/jobs/{id}/cancel`** sets `job['cancel']=True`; the job catches `ExportCancelled` → status `"cancelled"`. Frontend `btnExport` is now a **toggle**: while a job runs it shows `Cancel · NN% (done/total)`, and a second click cancels (POST cancel + stops polling). `exportJob` closure var tracks the active job.
- Duplicate conditional formatting on A/B still applies (now mostly flags a shared ASIN across two UPCs, since UPCs are unique per row). **Backend restart** needed.

**Changes this session (2026-09-01b) — Offer Analytics export: match Atlantic formulas exactly + UPC×ASIN expansion, hyperlink, sort, dedup CF:**
- Re-inspected the manual Atlantic file's actual FORMULAS and matched them in `services/vendor_offers_template_export.py` (cell refs remapped to our column layout):
  - **Net Profit** `=M{r}-L{r}-S{r}-T{r}-U{r}-V{r}` (BB − ProductCost×pack − FBA − Referral − Storage − Prep) — was `=M-(S+T+U+V)-L` (same value, wrong shape).
  - **Net Margin** `=IFERROR(P/M,0)`, **ROI** `=IFERROR(P/L,0)` (Atlantic uses `=Y/V`,`=Y/U`; IFERROR avoids #DIV/0! on zero-BB rows). **L** `=K*J`.
  - **Referral Fee** now TIERED: `=IF(M{r}>10,M{r}*0.15,M{r}*0.08)` (matches Atlantic `=IF(V>10,V*0.15,V*0.08)`).
  - **Link** is a real hyperlink: `=HYPERLINK("https://www.amazon.com/dp/"&B{r})`.
- **Prep & Out fee → currency** format (was General).
- **UPC × ASIN expansion fixed:** ASIN source is now **Keepa barcodes ∪ Pair-Library assignments** (`_assigned_asins_by_upc()` from `vo_asin_by_upc`). keepa_barcodes is 1:1, but UPCs legitimately have many ASINs in our assignments (dist: 1→5752, 2→1603, … up to 19), so a UPC now expands to ALL its ASINs (duplicate rows), fixing "only one ASIN per UPC" and covering the 3,519 UPCs whose assigned ASIN wasn't in keepa_barcodes.
- **Has-ASIN filter honored:** `has_asin` param (frontend sends `state.asin`) → rows with no ASIN are skipped.
- **Duplicate conditional formatting** on UPC (A) + ASIN (B) columns (Excel-standard red `FFC7CE`/`9C0006`) via `Rule(type='duplicateValues')`.
- **Sorted by Estimated Monthly Units DESC** — records collected, sorted, THEN written (so the row-referencing formulas get the correct final row numbers).
- Verified: QK/has_asin/400-UPC build → 1,776 rows, all formulas/hyperlink/prep-$/sort(80000→…)/0-no-ASIN-rows/CF on A2:A,B2:B correct. **Backend restart** needed. PERF: live enrichment is per unique ASIN, and the ASIN union inflates counts (~4.4/UPC here) — a big filtered set can be a long live run; filter tightly.

**Changes this session (2026-08-26) — Weekly catalog health-check JOB (Eligibility + DOG) + Price-Desk imports now feed the Pair Library:**
- **User insight, confirmed and used:** the DOG check and the storage-fee check were BOTH `getCatalogItem` calls done separately. One call can return everything — `getCatalogItem(includedData=productTypes,summaries,attributes,salesRanks)` yields DOG (404), Amazon brand + manufacturer, AND package dimensions (→ FBA storage fee) at once. The new job uses exactly **2 SP-API calls per ASIN**: one catalog GET (DOG + brand/mfr + dims/storage) + one `getListingsRestrictions` (eligibility). Verified live: `B00006EPHL`→CAN_SELL, dims/fee 0.0075/0.0229, brand Mitchum; `B00009V2QV`→RESTRICTED, fee 0.014/0.0432, DURACELL / Procter & Gamble — all from the single catalog call.
- **NEW `services/health_check.py`**: `run_health_check(asins=None, trigger)` runs Eligibility + DOG (+dims/storage) over the Pair-Library ASINs (5 workers), writes a summary row to **`health_check_runs`** + per-ASIN rows to **`health_check_results`** (dog, eligibility, reasons, brand, manufacturer, storage_fee, storage_fee_peak, detail), and **backfills accurate brand/manufacturer into `pair_library`** from Amazon summaries. In-process **scheduler** (`start_scheduler`/`stop_scheduler`, daemon thread) fires **Friday 19:00 `America/New_York`** (DST-aware → 7pm ET year-round); `next_run_time()` verified → next Fri = `2026-08-28T19:00-04:00`. Guarded so only one run at a time (`_running` event). "Inside the running app" per user's choice — so it only fires while the server is up (missed if the app is closed at Fri 7pm).
- **NEW `routers/health_check.py`** (registered in `main.py`, `/api/health-check/*`): `GET /status` (scheduler state + next run + latest summary), `POST /run` (trigger now, background thread; 409 if already running), `GET /runs` (recent summaries), `GET /runs/{id}` (per-ASIN rows), `GET /runs/{id}/export` (CSV). Scheduler is started/stopped from the FastAPI lifespan in `main.py`.
- **Price-Desk imports now mirror into the Pair Library** (`services/vendor_offers.py`): `assign_asin` and `assign_bulk` call new `_mirror_pairs_to_library()` → `database.upsert_library_pair(asin, ids={'upc':[upc]}, brand=<offer-desc leading token>, amz_pack=<pack>)`. This grows the curated ASIN set AND gives the UPC↔ASIN sync real barcodes to match (the library was MPN-only with 0 UPCs). Brand at import is a best-effort leading-token guess from the vendor offer's description (offers carry no structured brand/manufacturer); the weekly health-check overwrites it with Amazon's real brand/manufacturer. Mirror runs OUTSIDE the vo write-lock (upsert_library_pair takes `database._LOCK` itself) and is best-effort (never breaks the assignment). Verified: importing `307667845600→B0TESTLIB1` wrote pair_library `('B0TESTLIB1','NICORETTE','','3')` + a `upc` identifier, and the UPC sync then attached it.
- **Deploy: needs a backend RESTART** — the scheduler only arms on app startup, and the job/mirror/sync are all Python. The Price-Desk page HTML changes still show on hard-refresh, but the job won't be scheduled until the server restarts. (TODO if wanted: a small "Catalog Health" UI panel over `/api/health-check/*`; and — separately — the WAL/stale-connection quirk means a restart also reconciles the desk's item/vendor counts.)

**Changes this session (2026-08-18n) — Price Desk: Pair-Library ASIN sync (UPC-only) wired up + short-UPC paste fix:**
- User asked to "wire up the Pair Library so Cencora items get ASINs, but only join if the UPC matches or the item id." Investigated first — **this Pair Library is entirely MPN-keyed**: `pair_library_ids` holds 511 rows, ALL `id_type='mpn'` (251 distinct MPNs, 86 mapping to >1 ASIN), **zero UPC/EAN identifiers**. That's why the original `sync_asins_from_pair_library` (which read only `upc`/`ean`) attached nothing.
- **Tried item-id↔MPN matching and REJECTED it** (kept the evidence): matching Cencora's `vendor_item_id` to library MPNs produced **only coincidental numeric collisions** — Cencora item `3062` (Windex) ↔ a Smiths Medical MPN `3062`, `2084` (Reese's cups) ↔ a 3M MPN, `4925` (Feria hair color) ↔ a Smith & Nephew MPN — every offer description unrelated to the matched brand. Cencora's item numbers are **ABC warehouse codes, not manufacturer part numbers**. A per-vendor "main id (upc|mpn)" toggle was prototyped then removed; **user chose UPC-match-only for now** ("lets just make it a upc match first").
- **`services/vendor_offers.py`**: `sync_asins_from_pair_library()` rewritten to **UPC-only** — links an offer to a Pair-Library ASIN iff the offer's normalized UPC matches a library UPC/EAN id. A UPC mapping to several ASINs attaches all (desk shows "N ASINs"). **Idempotent** (rebuilds `source='pair_library'` rows each run via `DELETE … WHERE source='pair_library'` then `INSERT OR IGNORE`), and **manual assignments always win** (PK is asin; INSERT OR IGNORE skips manual rows). Returns `{asins, offers_matched_by_upc, library_upcs}`. Since the library has 0 UPCs today, it attaches **0** until UPCs are added — verified live (0/0/0), and cleaned up 14 spurious rows an earlier MPN test had written.
- **Short-UPC fix**: `parse_pasted_pairs()` (the paste-UPC↔ASIN importer) no longer requires ≥8 digits — the **UPC is now the non-ASIN token with the MOST digits** (tie → earliest), so short internal UPCs (<8 chars) are accepted and still aren't confused with a 1–3 digit pack (the pack has fewer digits). Verified: `12345`→`000000012345`, `999 … 12`→upc 999/pack 12, order-independent.
- **UI (`static/price_desk.html`)**: added a **"Sync from Pair Library"** button in Import → *UPC → ASIN matches* (`btnSyncPL` → `POST /api/vendor-offers/sync-asins`), with a result line that reports matches and, on 0, says the library has no UPCs yet. Verified live: boot completes, button opens/works, honest "0 … add UPCs" message, no console errors.
- **Deploy**: the HTML (`/page`) is read from disk per request so UI changes show on **hard-refresh**; the Python changes (UPC-only sync, short-UPC parse) need a **backend restart** to take effect on the running process.
- **Data note found while testing**: the DB actually holds **15,944 offers across 4 vendors** (BILO, Cencora, Diamond Wholesale, Quality King) — the running server was showing a stale **9,310 / Cencora-only** subset (a long-lived WAL reader connection not seeing later commits). A restart makes the desk reflect all four vendors.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18m) — Price Desk: fixed "buttons don't click" / empty desk (null-DATA crash); page is now self-healing:**
- User: on the live Price Desk the Import button (and all others) did nothing and the table was empty — but the page was styled. Diagnosed from the screenshot: the export button showed its **raw HTML label "Export to Excel"** instead of the JS-set **"Excel export (coming soon)"**, proving the page's `<script>` **never ran**. Root cause: `static/price_desk.html` did `const DATA = /*__DATA__*/null;` then immediately `const V = DATA.vendors` — when the ledger JSON was **not inlined** (page served as a static file, or the running backend still had an older `/page` route that returns the file without the inline `.replace`), `DATA` was literally `null`, so the first `DATA.vendors` threw an uncaught TypeError that aborted the whole script → **no event handlers attached** (dead buttons) and nothing rendered (CSS still applied, hence the styled-but-inert shell). The retheme was NOT the cause (CSS-only).
- **Fix (`static/price_desk.html`, JS only):** wrapped the entire script body in **`function boot(DATA){…}`** and replaced the crashing head with a guard — `const __INLINED = /*__DATA__*/null; if (__INLINED) boot(__INLINED); else fetch("/api/vendor-offers/data").then(r=>r.ok?r.json():reject).then(boot).catch(showMsg)`. So: (1) normal fast path unchanged (data inlined by `/page` → `boot` runs); (2) **if the data wasn't inlined, the page fetches the ledger itself** from the JSON endpoint and boots — self-heals even against a stale `/page`; (3) total failure shows a styled **"Couldn't load the price data … restart the server / check /api/vendor-offers/data"** message in `#scroller` instead of a silent dead page. Buttons now always attach on any success path. Confirmed no inline `on*=` handlers and a single `<script>` block, so the function-wrap changes no behavior.
- **Verified** in-browser: inlined-DATA path renders fully (stats/cadence/chips/table all populate); null-DATA-with-failed-fetch path shows the graceful message. The `boot()` wrapper is a resilience win, but live diagnosis (below) showed it was NOT the actual cause of the user's dead buttons — the running server WAS inlining data.

  **REAL root causes of "Import still doesn't click" (found by querying the live server on :8000 — process `Desktop\vetter\.venv\python main.py`, PID 12184):**
  1. **Data-shape mismatch (the true crash).** `/api/vendor-offers/page` was serving the current file WITH data inlined (9,310 Cencora items), so `__INLINED` was truthy and `boot()` ran — but threw **`TypeError: offers is not iterable`** at the item map. `build_ledger()` emits **7-field** items `[upc, title, asin, pack, status, #asins, offers[]]` (offers at index **6**), while the page destructured **6** — `const [upc,name,asin,pack,status,offers]=r` — reading the **#asins NUMBER at [5]** as `offers`, then `for (const o of offers)` blew up. (My earlier standalone preview masked this: the synthetic ledger I injected used the old 6-field shape, so it rendered.) **Fix:** destructure `[upc,name,asin,pack,status,nAsins,offers]` and change the two `r[5].length` reads (stats tile + footer) to `r[6].length`. This is the fix that makes Import actually work.
  2. **Vestigial `hasServer` (a second, quieter throw).** `openImport()` had `if (!hasServer) { …disable buttons… }` left over from the standalone build (which could open as a static file with no backend); the variable no longer exists, so every Import click threw **`ReferenceError: hasServer is not defined`** *after* opening the modal. **Fix:** removed the dead branch — this page is always backend-served, so importing is always available.
- **Verified against the LIVE server** (not just a preview): after both fixes, a fresh `/page` load has `boot()` complete (export button relabels to "Excel export (coming soon)"), the 5 stat tiles populate, **clicking Import opens the modal with no thrown error** (`aria-hidden` true→false, vendor dropdown = BILO/Cencora/Diamond Wholesale/Quality King Distributors), and the import ACTION works end-to-end (`POST /api/vendor-offers/assign-bulk` dry-run parsed a UPC→ASIN pair, 200). **Deploy: a hard-refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R) is enough** — `/page` reads the HTML from disk each request, so no Python restart is needed for these JS fixes.
- **DATA NOTE for the user:** the live desk has 9,310 Cencora items but the table showed "Nothing matches / 0 items" because the **"Has ASIN" filter is ON by default** and none of those items have an ASIN attached yet. To see them, click **"Needs ASIN"**, or attach ASINs via Import → *UPC → ASIN matches* (paste) and/or the `sync-asins` pull from the Pair Library.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18l) — Price Desk (Vendor Offers) restyled to match the catalog-verifier app design:**
