**Changes this session (2026-08-18k) — Tools: eligibility/storage/generic flakiness fixed (SP-API HTTP layer now retries transient failures):**
- User: on a 3,789-ASIN Tools run, ~19 ASINs had an EMPTY storage fee (+20 eligibility ERROR, +6 generic Error); re-running found the dims. Diagnosed NOT missing data — re-fetched the "empty" ASINs (B0053GYCGE/B00EZWTY42/B00HRQQNCO) 3× each, all returned dims consistently. Root cause: **the SP-API HTTP layer only retried HTTP 429**; a `requests` network error (timeout / connection reset) or a 5xx propagated immediately → the per-ASIN worker caught it as empty/ERROR. Over a long bulk run those are near-certain; a low-load re-run succeeds. Fix: hardened **`services/spapi/catalog.py` `_get`** AND **`services/restrictions.py` `_get`** to (a) wrap `requests.get` in try/except `requests.RequestException` → retry with backoff, (b) retry **500/502/503/504** with backoff (was `raise_for_status`), (c) cap backoff at 30s and raise the request timeout 15→25s. Also hardened **`services/generic_check.py` `_validation_preview`** PUT loop (wrap in try/except + retry 429/5xx, 6 attempts). This covers all three Tools checks (storage + generic use catalog `_get`; eligibility uses restrictions `_get`). Verified the happy path still works for all three on B0053GYCGE. **Backend restart to apply.** NOTE: a genuinely dimensionless ASIN still (correctly) shows an empty fee; the fix only stops transient failures from masquerading as one.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18i) — Tools: "Generic Check" (DOG / Generic / listable), validation-only, no listing created:**
- User wanted a Tools checkbox (like Eligibility/Storage) that says whether each ASIN is a **Generic ASIN** or a **DOG** (delisted) ASIN. Discovery: neither Catalog Items nor Listings Restrictions exposes the "generic" (SB85) status — a generic ASIN like B004YNP10I returns brand="MarbleMed" and `CAN_SELL`. The definitive signal is the **Listings Items API `putListingsItem` with `mode=VALIDATION_PREVIEW`** (validates WITHOUT persisting — no listing created): Amazon returns **issue code `5885`** (Generics Policy / SB85) for a generic ASIN. Verified generic-specific: generic ASINs → 5885; real branded ASINs (incl. a brand-GATED one) → VALID/no 5885; DOG → `getCatalogItem` 404. **NEW `services/generic_check.py`** `classify_generic(asin)` → `DOG` (catalog 404) | `GENERIC` (5885) | `NOT_GENERIC` | `ERROR`, building a minimal offer-only VALIDATION_PREVIEW body (productType pulled via `get_by_asin(included_data='productTypes')`; 429 backoff; needs the SP-API **Listings role** + `AMZ_SELLER_ID`). **NEW `routers/generic_check.py`** mirrors `eligibility.py` exactly — `/generic-check/status` + `/check` (5-worker bg job) + `/jobs/{id}` (poll) + `/jobs/{id}/export` (CSV asin,generic_status,detail); registered in `main.py`. **UI** (`static/index.html`, `app.js?v=47`): third "Generic Check" checkbox (default OFF — opt-in, ~2 SP-API calls/ASIN so slower than eligibility) + purple progress row (generic/not-generic/DOG/error stats) + a **`generic_status`** column in the combined Tools CSV. Verified: classify_generic correct on real ASINs (B004YNP10I→GENERIC, Medline→NOT_GENERIC, OakRidge-gated→NOT_GENERIC, dead→DOG); routes register; running server serves v=47 with the checkbox (backend route 404s until **restart**). **Backend restart + hard-refresh.**

**Changes this session (2026-08-18h) — Create SKUs: prefix now favors the DOMINANT catalog code, not a brand-lookalike minority:**
- User: Clorox resolved to **CLO** (real dominant code is **CLX 49/60 = 82%**) and Tresemme to **TRE** (real dominant is **ULV 33/41 = 80%**, their SKUs are `ULV000299`…). Root cause: `_choose_brand_prefix`'s alignment-override (`_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE`) was **0.15** — so a brand-name-lookalike code (CLO⊂CLOROX 18%, TRE⊂TRESEMME 24%) overrode the true mode even when the mode was the actual dominant SKU code. That rule exists for Colgate (COL 70/144 = 49% beats parent J&J), but 0.15 was far too low. **Fix: raised `_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE` 0.15 → 0.40** — the aligned code now only wins when it is NEARLY CO-DOMINANT with the mode. Verified on snapshot: Clorox→**CLX**, Tresemme→**ULV**, Colgate→COL (49%✓), Tide→TID (57%✓), Cardinal→CAH, Medline→MDL, Band-Aid→BAI, Neutrogena→NEU, 3M→MMM, Gillette→P&G (unchanged, GIL 14%). **SIDE EFFECT (flagged to user):** brands with an aligned-code share in [15%,40%) now take the dominant code instead — notably **Curad CUR→MDL** (MDL 41 vs CUR 11 = 79% dominant; an earlier session had preferred CUR). This matches the user's "most popular wins" principle; if a specific brand (e.g. Curad→CUR) should differ, pin it via `sc_reference._MANUAL_PREFIX` (like Dove→DOV) or edit the per-brand card. **Restart** to rebuild `sc_reference` (the resolve_brands 3-char guard + this both apply). Prefix is learned from whichever SKUs are in the company scope, so the exact winner depends on the live Azure Ford-Medical data; on the shown ULV-dominant Tresemme / CLX-dominant Clorox it now picks ULV / CLX.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18g) — Brand Analytics: filter by estimated units sold/mo (Keepa `monthlySold`, free):**
- Added a **units-sold-per-month window** to the Configure step, alongside BSR. Verified live it filters IN Keepa's finder for **0 extra tokens**: `brand=medline` + `monthlySold_gte=100` → 302 (11 tok), `+gte=500` → 112, combined with rank `1..50000 & sold>=100` → 209. **`keepa.find_asins` gained `min_sold`/`max_sold`** → `monthlySold_gte`/`monthlySold_lte` in the finder `base`. **DB:** `brand_analytics_runs.min_sold`/`max_sold` (additive migration, default 0). **Router** `CreateRunBody` + INSERT store them; **`_keepa_brand_pipeline`** reads them from the run row (same SELECT as `search_type`) and passes to `find_asins`. **UI** (`app.js?v=46`, index.html): two new Configure fields "Min units / mo" + "Max units / mo" (wiz state `minSold`/`maxSold`, read on Next, sent as `min_sold`/`max_sold`, shown on Confirm). **CAVEAT:** `monthlySold` is Amazon's "bought in past month" figure, only published above a threshold — so a Min-units filter is STRICTER than a rank filter (medline 302 vs 403) and excludes products with no published figure. Verified: `find_asins(brand='medline', min_sold=100)`→302. **Backend restart + hard-refresh.**

**Changes this session (2026-08-18f) — Brand Analytics: BSR filtered IN Keepa (free); "Pages/brand" removed from Configure:**
- Since discovery is Keepa, the Configure step's **"Pages / brand"** (old SP-API keyword paging) is meaningless → **removed** from the wizard (`static/index.html`, subtitle + Confirm line updated; `app.js?v=45`). The stale `#ba-wiz-pages` reads are null-guarded (default 10, ignored by the Keepa path; the SP-API fallback still uses `pages_per_brand`).
- **BSR now filters directly in Keepa's Product Finder — for ZERO extra tokens.** Verified live: `brand=medline` baseline 31,375 ASINs (11 tok); `+current_SALES_lte=50000` → **403** (11 tok); `current_SALES_gte=1` (ranked only) → 7,556 (11 tok). Same cost regardless of the filter (finder cost is per-page-of-results). **`keepa.find_asins` gained `min_rank`/`max_rank`** → adds `current_SALES_gte`/`current_SALES_lte` to the finder `base` selection (applies to every probe/retrieve/partition). **`_keepa_brand_pipeline` now passes the run's `min_rank`/`max_rank`** into `find_asins`. **IMPORTANT: the Keepa path previously applied NO BSR filter at all** (the min/max code lived only in the old SP-API `_brand_pipeline`), so this is what actually makes the wizard's Min/Max BSR *work* for Keepa runs — and it trims the ASIN list BEFORE SP-API enrichment (Medline max-50k: 31,375 → 403), so the whole run is far faster/cheaper. Verified: `find_asins(brand='medline', max_rank=50000)` → 403. **CAVEAT:** a Keepa rank filter requires a rank, so it EXCLUDES unranked products — matches the app's existing Min-BSR rule; for Max-BSR it's a slight change (the old SP-API path kept unranked). Usually desired (max rank = "must be selling"). The wizard already sent `min_rank`/`max_rank`, so no frontend submit change. **Backend restart + hard-refresh.**

**Changes this session (2026-08-18e) — Brand Analytics: sub-brand discovery is now DATA-DRIVEN from Keepa (finds Flowflex etc.):**
- User: "Discover sub-brands" for the **Acon** manufacturer missed real lines like **Flowflex**. Root cause: `/discover` only used the AI (`discover_brands`) which guesses from world-knowledge — it lists Acon Diagnostics/Biotech/Labs/Mission but can't know Flowflex. Diagnosed via Keepa: Flowflex IS there (212 products, brand=`FLOWFLEX`) and is linked to Acon through the **manufacturer field** (`ACON Laboratories`/`ACON Labs`). `discover_entities` (existing) seeds on `brand=acon` so it never sees Flowflex-branded products. **Fix — new `keepa.discover_sub_brands(seed, extra_terms)`** (`services/keepa.py`): searches products whose **MANUFACTURER** matches the seed (its variants + only the seed-token-anchored longer forms from the AI list, e.g. `ACON Laboratories`; a generic AI guess like `Mission` is NOT searched as a mfr so it can't drag in Mission Foods/Darts/Bauer), samples them, and collects the distinct **BRAND** field values, then gets an exact finder count per brand. Junk brands filtered (`_JUNK_BRANDS`: generic/unknown/…). A guarded round-2 expands to SEEN manufacturer strings that mention the seed token (catches the rest of the family). **Wired into `POST /brand-analytics/discover`** (`routers/brand_analytics.py`): after the AI/cache base, it always runs the Keepa pass (via `asyncio.to_thread`), merges **verified brands first (by product count desc), then AI-only guesses** (`_merge_dedup`), returns `result.keepa.counts`, and saves the merged list to `brand_library`. Resilient: AI failure no longer blocks the Keepa pass. **UI** (`static/app.js` `app.js?v=44`): the sub-brand checklist shows each Keepa-verified brand with its "· N on Amazon" count so the user can prioritise; status line says "N verified from Keepa catalog data". **Verified end-to-end: Acon → FLOWFLEX(212), On Call(22), ON/GO(13), On Call Express(5), KETOTRAK(4), HEMOTOUCH(50), OPTILANCE(2)… for ~380 tokens**, AI-only extras (Acon Diagnostics/Biotech/Labs, Wondfo) listed after. **Backend restart + hard-refresh.** NOTE: generic sub-brand names still show their GLOBAL Amazon count (e.g. `Mission` 2968 includes non-ACON Missions) — inherent ambiguity of a bare brand search; the count is informational. Supersedes the earlier "wire discover_entities into /discover" TODO.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18d) — Create SKUs: brand prefix is ALWAYS 3 chars, never 2 (hardcoded rule):**
- User: TRESemmé's card showed a **2-char prefix `RH`**. Cause: under the live Azure Ford-Medical scope TRESemmé has only ~7 mains whose SKU code leads with just two letters (`RH1234` → `_lead_prefix`=`RH`), so the mode fed to `_choose_brand_prefix` was 2-char and it returned `RH`. (The all-company snapshot has ULV/TRE, both 3-char — so this only surfaces on the company-scoped live data.) Fix — **hardcoded 3-char minimum**: new **`_ensure_min3(prefix, counter, sig)`** in `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`, applied to `_choose_brand_prefix`'s return, upgrades any <3-char pick by preferring (1) a brand-name-ALIGNED 3-char code the brand actually uses (`TRE`), else (2) the most-used ≥3-char code (`ULV`), else (3) the brand-name signature (`TRESEMME`→`TRE`), else pad. This covers BOTH the live index (`prefix_for_brand`) and the `sc_reference` table build (both call `_choose_brand_prefix`). Belt-and-suspenders: `create_po.resolve_brands` now also does `if len(prefix) < 3: prefix = _auto_prefix(brand)` after resolution, so a **stale 2-char value already saved in `sc_brand_prefix`** is upgraded on the card/export even before a table rebuild. `_auto_prefix` already returned 3. Verified: `{RH:7}`→`TRE`, `{RH,TRE}`→`TRE`, `{RH,ULV}`→`ULV`; Colgate→COL, Cardinal→CAH, 3M→MMM, Dove(raw)→ULV all unchanged; every output ≥3 chars. **Restart** to apply (startup rebuilds `sc_reference`; the resolve_brands guard also fixes stale saved values live). NOTE: manual prefix overrides still win and are not force-lengthened.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18c) — Create SKUs: corporate `-C` SKUs no longer masquerade as the main:**
- User: some Gojo mains resolved to a **`-C` (corporate) SKU** (e.g. `GOJ7753-02-C` for MPN `7753-02`) instead of the real un-suffixed main `GOJ7753-02`. Root cause is the SAME class as the old `_QY4-FBA` bug: corporate `-C` rows carry an **empty `ShadowOf`**, so `_mains()` treated them as mains and `main_by_upc`/`main_by_mpn` returned them. Fix: **`_SHADOW_SKU_RE`** (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`) now also matches a **trailing `-C`** (`r"_QY\d+|-FB[AM]|-C$"`, IGNORECASE) so `_is_shadow_sku('GOJ7753-02-C')` is True → excluded from `_mains()`. Only a **trailing `-C`** is excluded — dashes inside MPNs/item IDs are preserved (`GOJ9026-1M`, `GOJ7753-02` stay mains). Verified against the snapshot: `main_by_mpn('7753-02')`→`GOJ7753-02` (was the `-C`), `main_by_mpn('5009-04')`→`[]` (that MPN had ONLY `GOJ5009-04-C`, so Create SKUs now mints the proper `GOJ5009-04` main). Index-build logic only → **restart** to apply (no snapshot rebuild). NOTE: `-CMR` (e.g. `GOJ3901-72-CMR`) is NOT excluded (only exact `-C`); flag if that's also corporate. Also observed but NOT changed: a `.PPE-PARENT` variant can share an MPN with the main (returned alongside it) — separate from `-C`.

**Changes this session (2026-08-18b) — Create SKUs: "Check on SellerCloud" button on the single-brand card:**
- The Step-3 **One brand** card relied on Tab/blur/Enter to trigger the catalog lookup (`loadBrands`), which was easy to miss. Added an explicit **🔍 Check on SellerCloud** button next to the BRAND input (`static/create_po.js` `brandSingle()`), wired via `data-act="checksc"` → `loadBrands(true)`. **`loadBrands(force=false)`** now takes a `force` flag: the button (force=true) **overwrites** the four auto-filled fields (manufacturer/prefix/purchaser/sourcer) with the fresh catalog result — so re-checking after changing the brand actually refreshes — while the gentle Tab/blur path (force=false) still only fills empty fields. Button disabled/dimmed when the brand is blank or a lookup is in flight (shows "Checking…"). Hint text reworded to lead with the button. `create_po.js?v=27` (index.html bumped). Verified live: the running server serves v=27 with the button/case/force-param present and no console errors (full wizard-to-step-3 click-through not driven — needs a file upload — but the edit is self-contained and mirrors the existing `bmode`/`loadBrands` patterns). **Frontend-only → hard-refresh.**

**Changes this session (2026-08-18) — bulk-update the PO "Other" money field via the SellerCloud REST API:**
- **Mapped the PO surface in the SellerCloud swagger** (`https://<tenant>.api.sellercloud.com/rest/swagger/docs/v1` — the raw JSON; the `/swagger/ui/` page is just an SPA shell). 210 paths, **19 under `/api/PurchaseOrders`**. The PO **"Other"** amount is **`OtherTotal`** — read at `GET /api/PurchaseOrders/{id}` → **`TotalInfo.OtherTotal`** (`PoTotalInfoContent`), written at **`PUT /api/PurchaseOrders/{id}/Total`** (`UpdatePoTotalDto`). **There is NO bulk/batch endpoint for totals** — a bulk change is necessarily a loop of one PUT per PO. Other PO writes seen but NOT relevant: `PUT /api/PurchaseOrders/{id}` (`UpdatePoRequest` = company/vendor/dates/description/instructions only — no money), `PUT /{id}/CustomColumns`, `PUT /{id}/Invoice`, `PUT /{id}/Items`, `POST /Import/ImportPurchaseOrders` (file→queued job; its metadata DTO is company/plugin only, so it is a create/replace path, not a targeted total edit).
- **CRITICAL API TRAP:** `UpdatePoTotalDto` carries **FOUR non-nullable doubles** — `TaxTotal`, `OtherTotal`, `ShippingTotal`, `ShippingTotalThirdParty`. It is a **full replace, not a patch**: sending only `OtherTotal` writes **0** to the other three and silently wipes tax/shipping (and thus GrandTotal). Every write MUST be a read-modify-write that echoes the PO's current values back.
- **Client gained PO support** (`services/sellercloud/client.py`): `get_purchase_order(po_id)`, `search_purchase_orders(page_number, page_size, **filters)` / `iter_purchase_orders(...)` (filters are the `model.*` query keys passed WITHOUT the prefix — `companyIDList`, `pOStatuses`, `createDateFrom/To`, `keyword`, …), `update_po_totals(po_id, *, other_total, tax_total, shipping_total, shipping_total_third_party)` (raw 4-field PUT), and **`set_po_other_total(po_id, amount)`** — the safe read-modify-write that changes only Other and returns `{'before':…, 'after':…}`. Also **`_request` gained `ok: tuple[int,...] = (200,)`** because it previously treated ONLY 200 as success — a 204/201 from a write fell through to the retry loop and died with "Max retries exceeded" after 5 attempts; writes pass `ok=(200,201,204)`.
- **The bulk tool now lives OUTSIDE this project, at the vetter root: `C:\Users\RaianNair\Desktop\vetter\bulk_update_po_other.py`** (moved there at the user's request; `catalog-verifier/scripts/` was deleted). It is **fully standalone** — it inlines its own token manager + PO calls and imports NOTHING from catalog-verifier, so the two do not share code. A copy of `.env` sits beside it at the vetter root (root also gained a `.gitignore` covering `.env`/`__pycache__`/`*_dryrun.csv`/`*_applied.csv`; the vetter root is not itself a git repo, only catalog-verifier and AmazonAsinResearch1 are). **Drift warning:** the `client.py` PO methods above and the root script's inlined client are now two independent implementations of the same 4-field PUT — fix bugs in both. Reads a CSV/XLSX of PO ids + amounts (headers matched loosely: POID/PO ID/PurchaseOrderID/ID and OtherTotal/Other/Other Cost/Other Value/Amount), or `--value X` to set one flat amount for every listed PO. **Dry run is the default — nothing is written without `--apply`.** Validates/parses the ENTIRE file before the first API call (a bad row 400 can't surface after 399 live writes); strips `$`/commas; skips POs already at the target value; per-PO try/except so one failure can't kill the batch; writes a `*_dryrun.csv` / `*_applied.csv` log with before/after Other + the preserved Tax/Shipping/3rd-party + GrandTotal-before + status; `--sleep` (default 0.2s, SellerCloud serialises per account anyway), `--limit`, `--out`. Exit 1 if any PO errored.
- **`ReadTimeout` on the PUT does NOT mean the write failed.** SellerCloud recalculates the PO grand total inside `PUT /Total` and can exceed a 30s client timeout; the write still lands, only the response is lost. First live run (83 POs, 2026-08-18) reported "1 error" for PO 49675 on exactly this — a read-back showed `OtherTotal` already at the target, and all 83 POs verified correct. The script now uses a **120s write timeout** and, on `ReadTimeout`, **sleeps 5s and re-reads the PO**: if the value matches, it logs `updated` + "write confirmed by re-read" rather than an error, and only re-raises on a genuine mismatch. Deliberately does NOT blind-retry a timed-out PUT (a succeeded write would get a second redundant PUT). Any future timeout/ambiguity should be settled the same way — re-read, don't assume.
- **Verified:** mocked end-to-end (dry run → apply) confirming the PUT bodies carry the PO's **existing** tax/shipping alongside the new Other, skip/error/validation paths all behave; and a **read-only live probe against the fml tenant** (41,058 POs listed; `GET /api/PurchaseOrders/49772` → `TotalInfo` with `OtherTotal`). **No live write has been performed** — the first `--apply` is the user's call. NOTE: the swagger URL the user supplied is SellerCloud's `tt` demo tenant; FordMed's live host is **`fml.api.sellercloud.com/rest`** (already the `config.py` default) — the API surface is identical.

**Changes this session (2026-08-17) — AI Fill no longer strands at 'running'; how MPNs are found:**
- **Diagnosed why a Brand-Analytics AI Fill "stopped and won't go forward"** (run 21 "Medline Brand Review Final", stuck 873/898). Root cause: `_ai_fill_pipeline` (`services/analytics/brand_runner.py`) is a **single-threaded daemon loop with NO try/except around it**, and the per-item DB write block wasn't lock-resilient — a transient SQLite "database is locked" (the run's process was simultaneously busy) raised out of the loop, killed the daemon thread, and left `ai_fill_status='running'` forever (progress frozen, UI spinner never completes). Compounding it, `reset_orphaned_running_states()` (`services/database.py`) reset `ai_check_status` and brand-run `status` but **not `ai_fill_status`**, so even a restart wouldn't clear it. NOTE: this fill targeted a **single column** (MPN, the column-selectable AI Fill) — `ai_fill_total=898` = items missing MPN, not all 33,318. Of 873 processed only 104 yielded an MPN (see below); the field-based selection `(mpn IS NULL AND ai_mpn IS NULL)` re-selects the 769 the AI found nothing for on any future MPN fill (they never "stick").
- **FIXES:** (1) `_ai_fill_pipeline` is now wrapped in try/except — any crash flips the run to `ai_fill_status='error: …'` instead of stranding on 'running'; and all writes go through a local `_write()` helper that runs the item-update + progress-counter in one locked connection and **retries up to 4× on a transient lock** (0.4s backoff) before giving up on just that item. (2) `reset_orphaned_running_states()` now also does `UPDATE brand_analytics_runs SET ai_fill_status='stopped' WHERE ai_fill_status='running'`. (3) Run 21's stranded status was reset to 'stopped' directly in the DB so the UI un-freezes now. **Backend → restart** to load the fix; then hard-refresh + re-click AI Fill (it resumes on the still-unfilled items).
- **HOW AI FILL FINDS MPNs (important):** `_ai_fill_one` sends each product's OWN `title` + `description[:800]` + `bullet_points[:800]` (text already stored from Keepa/SP-API) to **claude-haiku-4-5** with `_ai_fill_system(fields)`, which instructs it to extract an identifier ONLY if **explicitly present in that text** ("Never invent or guess"). So it is **pure text extraction from the listing — it does NOT search the web**, and it does NOT use the structured `partNumber`/`model` attributes (verified: a 400-item sample of run 21's missing-MPN items had `attributes` dicts but ZERO `part_number`/`model` populated — SP-API simply didn't carry an MPN for them). That's why only 104/873 got an MPN — the rest don't state it in their listing text. **Correction on the source chain (user asked "is MPN filled from Keepa first?"):** NO. Keepa only *discovers the ASIN list*; **SP-API** fills `mpn`/`upc`/`ean`/`gtin` at run time from catalog attributes. For run 21's missing-MPN items BOTH structured sources are empty — verified: a 20-ASIN sample had 0 SP-API part-numbers AND **0/20 Keepa `/product` partNumbers** (old B000-era Medline private-label listings never had an MPN registered).
**Web-lookup fallback — VALIDATED, agreed to build (tiered SP-API → Keepa → Web):** Claude's server-side web-search tool (`{"type":"web_search_20250305"}`) works on the existing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (tested live: 3 searches, clean JSON out) and — importantly — **verifies rather than guesses** (for a FitRight liner it found a related older Medline # `AHBCP07Z` but returned `mpn:null` because it couldn't confirm it mapped to that exact SKU). Honest ceiling: many old private-label SKUs have no confirmable MPN anywhere, so web recovers SOME not all. **Ran as a one-off backfill on run 21 (NOT yet wired into the app):** `scratchpad/web_mpn_test.py` — Claude Sonnet + `web_search_20250305`, brand/title-verified, accepts only high/medium-confidence non-null, writes to `ai_mpn`. Result on run 21's 794 text-unfindable MPNs: **web recovered 208** (so `ai_mpn` = 104 text + 208 web = 312), **586 remain with NO confirmable MPN anywhere** → MPN coverage 97.6% → **98.24%**. Hit rate was ~60% on a hand-picked durable-medical sample but ~23% across the full set; a retry pass (3 attempts + backoff, 4 workers) on the leftover 592 recovered only **+6**, proving the floor is GENUINE (old private-label SKUs simply have no public MPN), not a throttling artifact. **KEY UX gotcha the user hit:** text-based AI Fill writes `ai_mpn=NULL` when it finds nothing, and the estimate/selection is `(mpn IS NULL AND ai_mpn IS NULL)` — so "found nothing" items are re-counted as "missing" and re-selected on every run; the number never drops below the true no-MPN floor no matter how many times AI Fill runs. **STILL TODO (in-app):** wire the tiered SP-API→Keepa→web fallback into the AI Fill button as an opt-in step (MPN only) + record the source per fill; optionally a "checked, none found" marker so re-runs skip them. Scope confirm (MPN-only vs all IDs) still open.


**Changes this session (2026-08-12f) — Brand Analytics AI Fill is now column-selectable:**
- **AI Fill lets you choose WHICH id column(s) to fill** (MPN / UPC / EAN / GTIN) instead of always doing all four (`services/analytics/brand_runner.py`, `routers/brand_analytics.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`). Backend: `_AI_FILL_SYSTEM` replaced with **`_ai_fill_system(fields)`** (dynamic prompt naming only the selected identifiers) + **`_clean_ai_fill_fields()`** (validates the subset, defaults to all four); `_ai_fill_one(item, client, fields)`, `_ai_fill_pipeline(run_id, client, fields)`, `start_ai_fill(run_id, client, fields)` all thread `fields`. Item selection is now **field-based** — `(col IS NULL AND ai_col IS NULL)` OR'd over the SELECTED fields (not the item-level `ai_fill_status` flag) so a later run for a DIFFERENT column re-processes the same items; the UPDATE only writes the selected `ai_*` columns. Endpoint `POST /ai_fill` reads `body.fields`; `GET /ai_fill/estimate?fields=` counts only items needing those fields. UI: the AI-Fill modal gained **4 checkboxes** (`.ba-aifill-field`, all checked by default); `_refreshAiFillEstimate()` re-fetches the count/cost live as you toggle them, and confirm posts `{fields}`. `app.js?v=43`. Verified: imports clean, `_clean_ai_fill_fields` drops junk / defaults to all, prompts target the chosen fields. **Backend → restart; frontend → hard-refresh.** (Couldn't run the live UI click-test — the preview env resolved to a different project's server and this shell's Python lacks uvicorn — but the JS/HTML edits are self-contained.)

**Changes this session (2026-08-12c) — Brand Analytics recall via Keepa (IN PROGRESS):**
- **Brand Analytics now discovers the FULL brand/manufacturer catalog from Keepa (beats SP-API's recall ceiling), then enriches via SP-API** (`services/keepa.py` [new], `services/analytics/brand_runner.py`, `services/database.py`). Root problem: SP-API `searchCatalogItems` is keyword+depth-capped, so a Medline manufacturer run returned only **6,280** ASINs vs Keepa's **~31.5k**. Fix: **`services/keepa.py`** — Keepa Product Finder client. `find_asins(brand|manufacturer)` calls `POST https://api.keepa.com/query` (needs `KEEPA_API` in `.env`). CRITICAL API facts (verified live): the finder caps at **10,000 ASINs per selection** and **`page` pagination does NOT go deeper** (page 1 returns 0), so it **recursively partitions on `trackingSince`** (a timestamp EVERY tracked product has — sales rank can't be used: only 7,558 of Medline's 31,502 are ranked) until each partition ≤10k, then merges+dedupes. Token cost = 10 + 1/100 ASINs; `find_asins` returns `tokens_spent` + `tokens_left`. **Verified live: brand='medline' → 31,501 distinct ASINs, 542 tokens, ~4s.** `brand_runner._brand_pipeline` now dispatches to new **`_keepa_brand_pipeline`** whenever `keepa.is_configured()` (SP-API keyword search stays as the fallback): Keepa discovery (per search term, brand vs manufacturer from the run's `search_type`) → SP-API `search_by_identifiers(batch20, id_type='ASIN', includedData=summaries,identifiers,attributes,salesRanks,images)` → `normalize_amazon_item` → `_upsert_item`, keeping the existing category filter + pause/stop control. **Storage fee** (off-peak + Q4 peak) is computed in `_upsert_item` from the SP-API dims (`services.storage_fees`) — free, no extra call. Keepa token spend/balance stored on the run. **DB (additive):** `brand_analytics_items.storage_fee/storage_fee_peak/buybox_price/eligibility_status`; `brand_analytics_runs.source/keepa_tokens_spent/keepa_tokens_left/elig_check_status/elig_check_done/elig_check_total`. **Verified:** migration+imports clean; SP-API `identifiersType=ASIN` returns BSR/brand/title + storage computes on real Medline ASINs. **FIX (2026-08-12d) — batch enrichment dropped ~50% of ASINs**: a Medline Keepa run stored only **16,572** of Keepa's ~33k. Root cause: SP-API **`searchCatalogItems` with `identifiersType=ASIN` silently returns only ~half** the ASINs in a batch (it collapses/caps variation siblings — verified: batch 20→10, and single `getCatalogItem` recovered 6/6 of the dropped ones, all real Medline products). `_keepa_brand_pipeline` now does **HYBRID enrichment**: the fast batch call, then a single `getCatalogItem` for every ASIN the batch dropped — verified 40/40 recovered (was 20/40). Cost: the recovery adds ~1 single call per dropped ASIN (roughly doubles SP-API time for a big brand), but the run now matches Keepa's full count. **Re-run the brand to apply.** NOTE: buy box = SP-API `getItemOffersBatch` `Summary.BuyBoxPrices` (New); most niche/medical ASINs currently have a SUPPRESSED buy box (no featured offer) so fall back to `LowestPrices` (lowest New landed offer) — verified live on 100 ASINs (0 had a live buy box, 83 had a lowest offer).
- **ENHANCEMENTS (2026-08-12e) — term-variants + Keepa entity discovery** (`services/keepa.py`): a Medline run hit 26,376 not 33,198 — diagnosed NOT a bug: discovery+enrichment were 100% for the terms given, but one run term was `medline industries, lp` (Keepa exact-match → **238**) vs Keepa's real `medline industries, lp.` (**6,934**); the exact 3-value OR = **33,198**. Two fixes: (a) **`_term_variants()`** expands each brand/manufacturer term into punctuation/suffix variants (toggle trailing period/comma; lp/inc/llc/ltd/co/corp ±period) passed as a Keepa OR list, so `find_asins(manufacturer='medline industries, lp')` now returns **7,172** (was 238) — a missing period never loses products. Wired into `find_asins` (Brand Analytics Keepa path benefits automatically). (b) **`discover_entities(seed)`** — for a seed like 'Medline', light-samples products' `brand`/`manufacturer` fields via Keepa `/product` (history/stats=0, ~1 token/ASIN) then gets an EXACT finder count per distinct value; returns the real strings + counts (verified: brand 'Medline' 31,527; mfr 'Medline' 19,736; 'Medline Industries, LP.' 6,934; 'Medline Industries Healthcare' 6,528; 'Patterson Medical Holdings Inc.' 2,209; …) for ~398 tokens — so the "Discover sub-brands" step can show the ACTUAL Keepa brand/manufacturer spellings to select instead of AI-guessed ones. **STILL TODO:** wire `discover_entities` into the `/discover` endpoint + Brand Analytics wizard UI (show entities w/ counts + checkboxes → search terms).
- **STILL TODO (next):** (a) **buy box** via a NEW SP-API Product Pricing client (getItemOffersBatch/getCompetitivePricing, ~1.6k batched calls); (b) **approval status** by reusing the 5-worker eligibility (`services/restrictions.py` `get_restrictions_api`) over the brand's ASINs (on-demand, since 31.5k = ~2 hrs); (c) **UI** — Keepa token display + Storage/Buybox/Approval columns. **Backend → restart.** NOTE: a full Medline run enriches 31.5k ASINs via SP-API (~1.6k calls, ~15 min).

**Changes this session (2026-08-12):**
- **Analytics: Approval status (CAN_SELL / NEEDS_APPROVAL / RESTRICTED) + FBA storage fee for Approved & Review ASINs** (`services/analytics/eligibility_check.py` [new], `services/database.py`, `routers/analytics.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/storage_fees.py`+`services/restrictions.py` reused). User asked to surface, for Approved/Review candidates, the selling eligibility + storage fee. **Key efficiency:** storage fee is computed from the Amazon dimensions ALREADY stored on each candidate (`data_json.amazon._raw.attributes` via `services.storage_fees.extract_dimensions`+`calc_storage_fee`) — **no extra API call** (verified 296/300 run-92 candidates computable); only **eligibility** needs a live `getListingsRestrictions` call per UNIQUE ASIN (deduped across rows). Scoped to **Approved+Review only** (run 92 = 7,744 distinct ASINs) — checking everything up front would waste calls on Not-approved, so it's the fast path. **On-demand** via a per-run button (like the AI-check button), because at ~5 calls/s a big run is ~25 min. **New module** `eligibility_check.py`: `start_eligibility_check(run_id)` (bg daemon thread), `stop_eligibility_check`, `is_running`, `approved_review_asins` (count); pipeline groups Approved/Review candidates by ASIN, computes storage once per ASIN, calls restrictions per ASIN (5 workers), writes `eligibility_status`/`storage_fee`/`storage_fee_peak` to EVERY candidate row sharing that ASIN; progress on `analytics_runs.elig_check_status/done/total`; degrades to storage-only (status "UNAVAILABLE") if `AMZ_SELLER_ID`/SP-API missing. **DB:** additive columns `analytics_candidates.eligibility_status/storage_fee/storage_fee_peak` + `analytics_runs.elig_check_status/done/total`. **Endpoints:** `GET /runs/{id}/eligibility/estimate` (distinct ASIN count + running), `POST /runs/{id}/eligibility` (start), `POST /runs/{id}/eligibility/stop`. Run-detail `SELECT *` carries the run-level progress; candidate `select_cols` gained the 3 columns. **Export:** Approved/Review sheets gained "Approval Status", "Storage Fee/unit/mo", "Storage Fee/unit/mo (Q4 peak)". **UI:** "Eligibility & Storage" button + Stop + a cyan progress card (mirrors AI check, polls while running); two new table columns "Approval" (coloured `_eligBadge`) + "Storage $/mo" after BSR; a confirm dialog shows the ASIN count + rough time before spending calls. Verified: migration + all imports clean; storage-write path on real run-92 data; ONE live eligibility call works from here (`B002OTLNVA → NEEDS_APPROVAL`, seller_id set — SP-API IS reachable, unlike Azure SQL); dev server starts with no backend errors; page loads with no console errors; button/stop/card + 15 aligned headers present in the DOM. `app.js?v=42`. **Backend → restart; frontend → hard-refresh.** NOTE: the full check is not auto-triggered (button only); eligibility depends on the SP-API Listings role + `AMZ_SELLER_ID`.

**Changes this session (2026-08-11b):**
- **Create SKUs follow-ups: reuse malformed FBM shadows via the channel field; keep climbing only for genuine name collisions; add "Existing" + "All" sheets to the Bulk file** (`services/create_po.py`, `routers/create_po.py`). (1) **Malformed-shadow reuse** — `_existing_shadow(index, asin, suffix, channel, main)` now also matches a row whose `ShadowOf` equals the channel label ("FBA"/"FBM") — the local snapshot stores the channel there, so a badly-named FBM like `ULV50200.2` is recognised as that ASIN's FBM and reused instead of minting a duplicate `-FBM`. Excludes the main itself (so `P&G13875` with ShadowOf="FBM" isn't mistaken for its own FBM). Safe on live Azure (there `ShadowOf` is the parent SKU, never literally "FBM"). (2) **Climb still correct for distinct ASINs** — confirmed/kept: two batch rows sharing a main+pack but DIFFERENT ASINs still climb (`…_QY6-FBA` / `…_QY6-FBA2`) because an FBA/FBM SKU must be unique; we only REUSE when the SAME ASIN already owns that channel's shadow. (3) **Two new bulk sheets** — `build_files(..., index=)` now writes, besides "Bulk" (SKUs to CREATE): an **"Existing"** sheet (every on-SC main/FBA/FBM with its catalog data — ProductID, ProductName, FulfilledBy, ManufacturerSKU, UPC, ASIN, BrandName, ManufacturerID, Qty/CostPerCase) and an **"All"** sheet (Main SKU / FBA / FBM / ASIN / Status = All exist·New·Partial for every product). Export route passes `index=sess.get("index")`. Verified end-to-end on the snapshot index. **Backend → restart.**
- **ProductName is now pulled on BOTH catalog paths** (`services/azure_sql.py`, `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`). The column IS named `ProductName` — confirmed in `SC data.xlsx` ("Sku Data extended"). Azure: `_OPTIONAL_COLS=["ProductName"]` is selected with a graceful retry (if the view lacks it, the pull silently drops it instead of erroring). Snapshot: `snapshot_rows_from_xlsx` now captures `ProductName` (snapshot rebuilt → 102,383 rows). In the Existing sheet a shadow with no name of its own **borrows its main's ProductName** (`_strip_to_main` → main row). Verified: `SOC615559` + its `-FBA`/`-FBM` all show "Soft Sheen Carson Lets Jam …". NOTE (relevant to the earlier FBA-export ask): `SC data.xlsx` "Sku Data extended" ALSO has **`AverageCost`, `AggregateQty`, `OnOrder`, `LastCost`** — the inventory/cost fields previously thought unavailable — so a fuller FBA export (or an inventory column on the Existing/All sheets) is now feasible from the snapshot; not yet wired.
- **Create SKUs: shadow SKUs are REUSED from the ASIN instead of climbing to -FBA2/-FBM2; the main is recovered from an existing shadow when UPC/MPN miss; and the title count isn't duplicated in the UOM** (`services/create_po.py`). User's Bulk export (`Bulk_399164`) created `SOC615559-FBA2`/`-FBM2`, `P&G13875-FBA3`, `ULV50200-FBA3` etc. for ASINs that ALREADY had FBA/FBM shadows on SellerCloud, plus random new mains. Root cause: `derive_rows` minted the child name from the main and `_claim`-climbed on ANY collision with the live catalog — so `SOC615559-FBA` (existing, same ASIN) forced a climb to `-FBA2`; and `_main_exists` only looked up the main by UPC/MPN, minting a brand-new main when those missed even though the ASIN's existing shadow proves the main already exists. **Fixes:** (1) **`_existing_shadow(index, asin, suffix)`** — returns the ASIN's existing FBA/FBM shadow (plain or `_QYn` kit, prefers the non-climber canonical name; company-scoped via the `-FBA`/`-FBATRB` suffix), and `derive_rows` now does `fba = ex_fba or _claim(...)` — reuse if the ASIN already has that channel's shadow, mint+climb only when it doesn't. `fba_on_sc`/`fbm_on_sc` are True when reused so `build_files` (bulk + kits + amz) skips re-creating them. (2) **`_main_from_asin(index, asin)` + `_strip_to_main`** — when `_main_exists` (UPC/MPN) finds nothing, recover the main by STRIPPING the shadow/kit suffix off the ASIN's existing child SKU (`SOC615559-FBA` → `SOC615559`, `DOV266545_QY6-FBA2` → `DOV266545`) and using it if it exists in the catalog; only mint a new `base_main` when the ASIN is genuinely unknown. NOTE: in this data `ShadowOf` stores the CHANNEL ("FBA"/"FBM"), not the parent — so we strip, not read ShadowOf. (3) **Title count de-dup** — `_title_pack` now returns the match span and `product_name` strips the in-title phrase before appending the UOM: "Bandages 10 count" → **"… Bandages (10 count)"** (was "… 10 count (10 count)"); "Glide Floss Picks 60 count" → "… (60 count)"; "pack of 2" → "(2 pack)"; no count → "(Each)". (4) kits.xlsx emission now guarded by `not r.fba_on_sc`/`not r.fbm_on_sc` (amz_shadows.xlsx already was). Verified offline against the snapshot index: `B000GCT6FW`+bogus-UPC → main `SOC615559` (on_sc), fba `SOC615559-FBA` (on_sc), fbm `SOC615559-FBM` (on_sc) — nothing recreated; `B00169ESVG` reuses the existing climber `ULV50200-FBA2` instead of `-FBA3`; genuinely-new ASIN+UPC still mints main + creates `-FBA`/`-FBM`; a pack=6 kit still yields `…_QY6-FBA`. **Backend → restart.** KNOWN edge cases (separate from this bug, not fixed): a malformed catalog FBM whose ProductID isn't `…-FBM` (e.g. `ULV50200.2`) won't be detected as the ASIN's FBM shadow → a fresh `-FBM` is minted; and two batch rows sharing a main+pack but DIFFERENT ASINs still climb (`DOV266545_QY6-FBA`/`-FBA2`) since an ASIN maps to one shadow.

**Changes this session (2026-08-06):**
- **Create SKUs: brand prefixes + manufacturers are now saved to a local "SellerCloud memory" (new `services/sc_reference.py` + tables `sc_brand_prefix` / `sc_manufacturer`) — read instantly instead of re-parsing the 179k-row Azure view; fixes Medline `MDLMDS`, Dove `ULV`, and blank/mis-spelled manufacturers on new brands** (`services/sc_reference.py` [new], `services/create_po.py`, `routers/create_po.py`, `main.py`). Three user-reported bugs, one root design change. **(a) Prefix >3 chars (Medline→`MDLMDS`)**: `_lead_prefix` greedily takes ALL leading letters, and Medline SKUs are `MDLMDS…`/`MDLDYND…`/`MDLNON…` (the item code itself starts with letters) → mode `MDLMDS` (235). Fix: the saved prefix is the mode of **`_lead_prefix(pid)[:3]`** (capped at 3) over **company-scoped MAINS** → MDL wins (1,869). **(b) Dove→ULV**: under Ford Medical, LLC Dove's mains are ULV=109 (Unilever's umbrella code, shared by 25 brands: Axe/Degree/Nexxus/…) vs DOV=11 (10% — under the old `_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE=0.15` guard). No count heuristic can separate this from Cardinal (whose umbrella CAH=19-brands is the *correct* prefix, CAR=11-brands is wrong), so umbrella detection was abandoned. Fix: **manual exceptions** — `_MANUAL_PREFIX{("DOVE","Ford Medical"):"DOV"}` seeded as `source='manual'`, never clobbered by a rebuild. **(c) Manufacturer blank / mis-spelled on new brands**: purchaser/sourcer were looked up **by brand** only (a NEW brand isn't in the map → blank), and the AI's "Nestle" was never reconciled to SellerCloud's "Nestlé S.A." (968 rows, purchaser MasonB, sourcer AviS). Fix: `sc_manufacturer` table (manuf→canonical name + mode purchaser/sourcer) + `lookup_manufacturer(name, company)` that matches **case/spacing/ACCENT-insensitively** (`_mkey` NFKD-folds é→e, so "Nestlé S.A."→key `nestlesa`) and ranks STRONG matches (exact / close-length substring, len-ratio ≥0.67) by SKU count over WEAK fuzzy — so "Nestle"/"nestle s.a."/"NESTLE" all resolve to **Nestlé S.A.** + its purchaser/sourcer, not a 3-row stray "Nestle". **Build:** `build_reference()` scans `azure_sql.fetch_rows()` per company ("Ford Medical"/"Turba"), upserts brands (preserving `source='manual'`) and rebuilds manufacturers clean; **2,149 brands + 968 manufacturers in ~15s**, warmed on startup after the Azure pull (`main.py` lifespan). **Wired into `resolve_brands(..., company=)`**: prefix now prefers `sc_reference.prefix_for(lookup|brand, company)` over the live index; after overrides, the manufacturer is reconciled via `lookup_manufacturer` (canonical name + fills purchaser/sourcer when empty — the new-brand case); a saved NEW brand's manufacturer is reused (skips the AI guess) via `brand_manufacturer()`. **New brands are remembered on EXPORT** (`routers/create_po.export` → `cp.save_brand_reference` → `set_manual_prefix`, `source='manual'`) so next run they resolve instantly (prefix + manufacturer + purchaser/sourcer) instead of blank. Verified end-to-end: Dove→DOV, Medline→MDL, 3M→MMM, OXO→OXO, Colgate→COL, Cardinal Health→CAH; new brand + AI "Nestle" → "Nestlé S.A." + MasonB/AviS; save→recall round-trip recovers prefix+mfr without calling AI. All safe-wrapped (`_sc_prefix`/`_sc_manufacturer`/`_sc_brand_manufacturer`) so Create SKUs never breaks if the tables/Azure are unavailable. **Backend → restart** (startup rebuilds the tables; `build_reference(force=True)` to refresh from a fresh Azure pull). NOTE: exceptions like Dove are seeded in `_MANUAL_PREFIX` for now — a UI "edit prefix" save can be added later; the manufacturer table isn't company-manual-preserving (no manual rows there yet).

**Changes this session (2026-07-17):**
- **Create SKUs: MPN existence check is brand-scoped, and the prefix heuristic no longer picks a rare letter-aligned outlier** (`services/create_po.py` `_main_exists`, `services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py` `_choose_brand_prefix`). User (creating Cardinal Health items) saw them flagged "already on SellerCloud" reusing `EMO4284`/`DYN4282`/`SMM4289-C` — other brands' SKUs that merely share the numeric MPN. (a) `_main_exists(..., brand=)`: UPC stays a global match, but **MPN hits are now filtered to the same brand** (`_brands_match`) — an MPN like "4284" isn't unique across manufacturers, so a Cardinal item no longer matches Eminence's `EMO4284`. `derive_rows` passes `bi.brand`. (b) **Prefix regression fix**: my earlier brand-letter-alignment picked the *rare* aligned "CAR" (6 SKUs) over Cardinal's real "CAH" (248). `_choose_brand_prefix` now defaults to the MODE and only overrides with an aligned prefix when the mode is NOT itself aligned (a parent/cross code) AND the aligned alternative is ≥ `_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE`(0.15) of the mode — keeps Colgate→COL (70/144), Dove→DOV (67/390), excludes Cardinal's CAR (6/248 → CAH), 3M→MMM. Verified live: `Cardinal Health, MPN 4284` → prefix **CAH**, main **CAH4284** (Cardinal's own SKU, branded "TED", exists → correctly "already on SC"), never `EMO4284`. **Backend → restart; prefix affects NEW runs + rescore.**
- **Create SKUs mapping: BOTH UPC and Item ID are now mappable regardless of "Create by"** (`static/create_po.js` `previewMap`). User (Medical, create-by Part No.): the Map step showed only the ★ Main identifier (Item ID) — no UPC card — so a file that also had UPCs couldn't populate the UPC column. "Create by" only chooses HOW the SKU is built; both identifiers should still be mappable. Fix: `previewMap` now always renders the ★ Main identifier (the create-by one, required) AND the OTHER identifier as an optional card (`otherId = idField==='upc' ? 'mpn' : 'upc'`). Backend already supported it (`normalize_rows` reads both `upc`+`mpn`; `build_files` writes UPC on every SKU). Verified in-browser (Medical/Part No. → Item ID ★ Main + optional UPC card, both auto-mapped) and end-to-end: create-by MPN with a mapped UPC → bulk row `ProductID=BSN4335, ManufacturerSKU=4335, UPC=012345678905`. `create_po.js?v=26` (hard-refresh).
- **Analytics pack parsing: prefer the explicit "…of N" form over a bare "Nct", and ignore SKU-sized counts** (`services/analytics/matcher.py` `_pack_count`, `services/analytics/runner.py` rescore). User: ASIN B00RAQ59XK "LYSOL Brand **85020CT** … (**Case of 12** Bottles)" got amz_pack **85020** because `\b(\d+)ct\b` matched the SKU code "85020CT" and `_pack_count` returned the FIRST match (case-of wasn't even in `_PACK_RE`). Fix: (1) added `case of N` to `_PACK_RE`; (2) new `_PACK_OF_RE` (`(?:pack|box|set|count|case) of N`) is checked FIRST in `_pack_count` — the authoritative full form wins over a bare token; (3) bare `Nct`/`N count`/… above `_MAX_BARE_PACK`(1000) are ignored as SKU/model numbers. Verified: LYSOL → **12**; "85020CT" alone → 1; "36 count"→36, "1000 count"→1000, "case of 50"→50, "2 rolls"→2 all preserved; `_effective_pack` → 12. Also: the **rescore pipeline now updates the `amz_pack` column** (its UPDATE previously set only confidence/verdict/data_json/sales_rank), so this fix reaches existing runs on rescore — computed from the recomputed `effective_pack`, else the SP-API package-quantity attribute. **Matcher change → NEW runs + RESCORE for existing.**
- **Analytics matcher: apparel-size check now reads Amazon's STRUCTURED size attribute; UPC-trust loosened; analytics export gains AMZ Pack** (`services/analytics/matcher.py`, `routers/analytics.py`). (a) **Apparel size** — user showed the JOBST vendor titles DO carry the size ("…Beige Small 15-20 mmHg"); the gap was that ASIN B0012XYFQE's TITLE has no size but its `attributes.size` = "X-Large". `_apparel_size_mismatch(src, amz_title, amz_size_attr="")` now unions garment sizes from the Amazon title AND its size attribute, so vendor "Small"/"Medium" vs Amazon "X-Large" → disjoint → hard reject (29 → Not Approved). Verified: B0012XYFQE now keeps ONLY row 430 (X-Large, UPC match) at 100; the Small/Medium/Large rows → Not Approved; same-XL-but-different-item-ID rows (Closed Toe, Opaque) → Review via `mpn_field_conflict`. (b) **UPC trust (user request)** — `upc_match AND brand_confirmed` → 100 already existed; added a tier `elif upc_match and best_title_ratio >= 60: total = max(total, VERIFIED_FLOOR)` so a UPC hit with a decent title agreement approves ("most UPC matches are correct"); the ≥60 title guard keeps a cross-attached barcode on an unrelated product in Review, and the hard-reject caps (size/apparel/gender/colour/count) still override. Conservative in practice (run-97 recompute: only 4 new approvals). (c) **Analytics export AMZ Pack** — `export_analytics_run` cand SELECT now includes `amz_pack`, and "AMZ Pack" is a new column (between Amazon Brand and BSR) on Approved/Review/Not-Approved sheets, defaulting to **1** when no pack was detected (per user — no blanks). Verified run 95 export shows pack values (2/3/60/16/…) and 1 for singles, zero blanks. **Matcher changes → NEW runs + RESCORE for existing; export change is immediate.**
- **Analytics matcher: item-ID/MPN CONFLICT now blocks the brand+title auto-verify (fixes one ASIN approving for many different-size SKUs)** (`services/analytics/matcher.py`, `static/app.js`). User: ASIN B0012XYFQE (JOBST Relief Compression Stockings, UPC 035664148218 / MPN 114821) was verified for ~10 vendor rows when only one is correct. Cause: those rows are different sizes of the same line — their vendor TITLES are identical ("JOBST Relief Thigh High Dot Band Stockings, Open Toe Beige") because the SIZE lives in the item ID/UPC, not the title. Only row 430 (UPC+item-ID 114821 exact) is right; the rest have different item IDs (114818/114819/…) and UPCs but verified at 90 via the brand+title VERIFIED_FLOOR — the matcher had a positive `mpn_field_exact` signal but NO negative one. Fix: new `mpn_field_conflict` = vendor part number and Amazon structured MPN are BOTH specific (≥4 alnum incl. a digit) and DIFFER (neither a substring/variant of the other); when set AND there's no `upc_match`, soft-cap to **80 → Review** (added after the floors, before the hard-reject caps). Added to the scores dict + `_verdictReason`/export reasons ("Item ID / MPN mismatch"). Verified on run 97: B0012XYFQE now keeps ONLY row 430 verified (100) and sends the 9 wrong sizes to Review (80); run-wide, 1,146 of 1,580 "verified" were this same JOBST size-cross-match false-approval and now go to Review. Deliberately **Review not Not-Approved** (some vendors use distributor codes that legitimately differ from the manufacturer MPN — a human confirms); only fires when Amazon actually has a structured MPN. `app.js?v=41`. **Matcher change → NEW runs + RESCORE for existing.**
- **Analytics wizard: Vendor Title auto-mapped to the SUPPLIER column ("Vendor Name") instead of the product description** (`static/app.js` `_bestGuessMapping`). User: "why is the vendor title just the brand?" — run 95 (RBC) showed vendor title "Scholls"/"Nestle". Root cause: the title auto-guess regex `/(title|desc|product|name)/` — the bare **`name`** matched **"Vendor Name"** (the supplier company: "Scholls", "Nestle"), and first-match won because that column precedes "Product Description"/"Summarized Title" in the file. So the real title ("Dr. Scholl's Callus Removers…", "Vital Proteins Collagen…") was ignored. Fix: title guess now (a) EXCLUDES supplier/company/number columns (`/(vendor|supplier|company|brand)name|number/`) and (b) uses ranked preference `[/description|title/, /productname|itemname/, /product|name/]`, taking the first non-excluded match. Verified on real header sets: RBC → "Product Description" (was "Vendor Name"); others → "Vendor Title", "Original Item Description". `app.js?v=40` (hard-refresh). NOTE: this fixes the auto-guess for NEW runs; already-created runs like 95 stay mis-mapped — **re-run** (title now auto-picks the description column) or **rescore** selecting the correct title column. The wizard always lets you override the auto-pick on the Map step.
- **Analytics matcher: two size/ounces fixes — (A) parse truncated units, (B) flag Amazon title-vs-attribute size contradictions** (`services/analytics/matcher.py`, `static/app.js`). User: "why do different-ounce products match?" Diagnosed two causes on the Palmers/E.T. BROWNE run 94. **(A) Malformed vendor units** — "7.25Z" (dropped 'o' from oz), "1Z" → `_extract_volume_ml` returned None, so `_size_mismatch` couldn't fire and they matched on brand+title. Added `_TRUNC_OZ_RE` (`(?:^|[\s(\[/])(\d+(?:\.\d+)?)\s*z\b`) as a fallback in `_extract_volume_ml` → treats a standalone `Nz` as ounces; tight boundary so product codes ("ABC5Z", "X4Z") don't false-match ("OIL8.5" with NO unit is still unreadable — accepted). Effect: run 94 recompute → 50 candidates now correctly Not Approved on real size mismatch. **(B) Amazon's own title vs structured size disagree** — the real reason "2.1oz matched 3.3oz": ASIN's title said "3.3 Fl Oz" but `attributes.size` said "2.1 Fl Oz"; the matcher trusts the attribute (2.1) which matched the vendor 2.1oz, so it wasn't a mismatch. New `_amz_size_ambiguous(src, amz_title, amz_attr)` — **targeted**: fires only when vendor≈attr AND vendor≠title AND title≠attr AND the title has exactly ONE volume token (so kits/bundles like "Cream 8.8 Oz + Oil 6.6 Fl Oz" whose components differ from the total attr are NOT false-flagged). Returned as `size_conflict`; soft-caps confidence to **80 → Review** (not a hard reject) so a human decides. Surgical: only 6 run-94 candidates hit it (an earlier broad version demoted ~400 — rejected). UI reason "Amazon size conflict (title vs listing)" added to `_verdictReason` + the export reasons tooltip. `app.js?v=39`. **Matcher change → applies to NEW runs and to existing runs after a RESCORE.** (The 2.1oz Batana case was already Review 76 via brand+title; the fix now labels WHY, and demotes the ones that would've hit 90.)
- **Analytics: opening a second run showed the FIRST run's candidate table (stale cross-run data)** (`static/app.js` `openAnalyticsRunDetail`). User opened run B and saw run A's rows — proof: E.T. BROWNE (run 94, 108 catalog rows) displayed candidate rows 115–270, impossible for a 108-row catalog. Root cause: `openAnalyticsRunDetail` reset tab/search/sort/page but NOT `state.analyticsRun.data` or `state.analyticsRun.tabPageData` (the per-tab candidate cache); `fetchAnalyticsRunDetail` only clears `tabPageData` on a **status CHANGE**, so two runs both "Complete" reused the first run's cached candidate pages, and the auto-fetch guard (`if !_curEntry || aiVerdictCounts==null`) saw a populated stale entry and skipped re-fetching. Fix: `openAnalyticsRunDetail` now also sets `state.analyticsRun.data = null; tabPageData = {}; _lastStatus = null` before fetching, forcing a clean load of the opened run. Verified in-browser: open run 94 → Palmers rows (36–53, within 108); open run 90 → 3M/Bauerfeind/Teleflex (its own data) — tables no longer bleed across runs. This also explained the user's "why do completely different products match?" — those pairings (e.g. vendor 'BSN Medical Cutimed' next to 'Beutlich Hurricane') were STALE table rows from another run, not real matches; run 90's real row 115 is 'BD Insulin Syringe' → 'BD Ultra-Fine Syringes' (sensible, all not_approved). `app.js?v=38`. (Frontend-only — hard-refresh.) NOTE: genuine over-matches can still occur via short/generic Item IDs matched by keyword (e.g. item id '10011' → 'Marinco 10011 …') — separate recall concern, not this bug.
- **Create SKUs ProductName UOM suffix: MAIN uses the count/pack from the TITLE; FBA/FBM use the amz-pack multiplier** (`services/create_po.py` `build_files.product_name` + new `_title_pack`). User rule: a MAIN whose vendor title says e.g. "3 Count" / "3-pack" gets suffix **`(3 count)` / `(3 pack)`** (not `(Each)`); an FBA/FBM keeps `(Each)` for amz-pack 1 and `(Pack of N)` for a multipack — the title's count does NOT change the shadow suffix. New module-level `_title_pack(title)` → `(n, 'count'|'pack')` for n≥2 (`pack of N`, `N-pack`, `N count/ct/cnt`; ignores PLY/BX/size/`1 count`). `product_name(r, name_qty, is_main)` branches: main → `_title_pack` on the (cleaned-or-raw) title else `Each`; shadows → existing `uom(name_qty)`; `emit` passes `is_main`. Verified end-to-end both with AI cleaning OFF (raw "…3 Count") and ON (AI expands "3 CT"→"3 count" via the Library, then it's detected): MAIN `(3 count)`, single FBA/FBM `(Each)`, kit FBA/FBM `(Pack of 2)`. NOTE: kit suffix stays `Pack of N` (capitalised, as in prior exports) — user wrote "pack of 2" lowercase; trivial to switch if desired. Backend-only. (Restart to apply.)
- **Create SKUs AI title cleaning is now wired to the shared Abbreviation Library — reads known abbr→full for consistent expansion AND saves new AI expansions back** (`routers/create_po.py` `_apply_ai_titles`). User asked to connect "our attributes table" so abbreviations/packs/counts in titles are expanded from what's already known, and AI un-abbreviations are stored. The app already had `abbreviation_library` (abbr/full, categories, `added_by` system|user|**ai**) with the same read+learn pattern used in verify/scans (`database.flat_library()` + `database.add_library_entry(..., added_by="ai")`). `_apply_ai_titles` now: (1) loads `flat_library()` and passes the known abbr=full pairs into the Haiku prompt (capped ~4000 chars) so expansions stay consistent; (2) prompt returns per-line objects `{"name","abbr":[{"a","f"}]}` where `abbr` = expansions NOT already known; (3) saves each new one via `add_library_entry("Product Attributes", a, f, added_by="ai")` (deduped vs known + within-run; tolerates the old string-array format). Added `from services import database`; `max_tokens` 2000→3000. Verified end-to-end (real Haiku): "SAFETEC UNSC … 8 OZ BTL" → "…Unscented … 8 fluid ounces", "DUKAL GAUZE SPNG 4X4 … NON-STER 200/BX" → "…Sponge 4 inch by 4 inch … Non-Sterile 200 count", and 4 new abbreviations learned (STER→Sterile, SPNG→Sponge, BTL→bottle, UNSC→Unscented; test entries then removed). New abbreviations land in the **"Product Attributes"** category (review/recategorize in the Library UI). Only runs when the "✨ Improve titles with AI" checkbox is on (export `clean_titles`). Backend-only. (Restart to apply.)
- **Create SKUs catalog matching is now scoped to the batch's OWN company (Ford Medical → 'Ford Medical, LLC'); SKUs under any other company are ignored** (`services/azure_sql.py`, `routers/create_po.py`, `static/create_po.js`). User: "only take info from company 'Ford Medical LLC'; if a brand is only under another company (or none), it's new." The view's company column is **`CompanyName`**; distinct values incl. `'Ford Medical, LLC'` (108,642 rows), `'BRDJ'` (44,602), `None` (8,732), Turba (1,221), etc. — e.g. Mane 'n Tail (`MAN*.FDS`, ".FDS" = Ford Distribution Services) is `CompanyName=None`, 0 rows under Ford Medical LLC. Fix: `azure_sql` adds `CompanyName` to `_COLS`/the row dict; `_COMPANY_NAME = {"Ford Medical":"Ford Medical, LLC","Turba":"Turba"}` + `_rows_for_company(rows, company)` (punctuation/space/case-insensitive match via `_co_key`); `catalog_index(company)`, `brand_map(company)`, `catalog_source(company)` all filter the (still globally-cached, all-company) `fetch_rows()` per call. `routers/create_po._catalog_source(company)` threads it; `/brands` reads `body.company`, `/generate` uses `config.company`, `/check-sku` uses `sess.config.company`; frontend `loadBrands` now sends `company: S.company`. Verified via `/brands`: Mane 'n Tail → **new**; NIVEA MEN / NIVEA → in_catalog. Side benefit: scoping to Ford Medical, LLC also corrected the Nivea prefix to **BSF** (Beiersdorf, the real Ford-Medical convention — matches the SellerCloud UI) instead of `NIV` picked up from other companies' rows. LIMITATION: the offline snapshot fallback (`ci.local_index()`) is NOT company-filtered (only the live Azure path is) — acceptable since Azure is primary. `create_po.js?v=25`. (Restart + hard-refresh.)
- **Create SKUs now does SMART (normalized/fuzzy) brand→catalog matching, not exact-only** (`services/create_po.py` `resolve_brands` + new `_match_catalog_brand`). User: "why does Nivea show as new when there's a lot on SellerCloud?" — because their file's brand was **"NIVEA MEN"** but the catalog stores `BrandName="NIVEA"` (50 SKUs, mfr Beiersdorf Inc.; "for men" is in the product NAME, not the brand field), and `resolve_brands` matched brands by **exact upper-name** only, so "NIVEA MEN" ≠ "NIVEA" → flagged new. **Confirmed with the user** to add smart matching. Fix: `resolve_brands` builds a `norm_map` ({normalized catalog brand → canonical name}) once, and `_match_catalog_brand(brand, ...)` resolves the input to a catalog brand via: exact upper → normalized-equal → sub-brand **prefix containment (≥5 chars)** → fuzzy **≥88** — reusing the Analytics engine's `_normalize_brand` (imported from `services.analytics.matcher`, `rapidfuzz.fuzz`). A matched brand is treated `in_catalog` and inherits the MATCHED catalog brand's manufacturer / prefix / purchaser / sourcer / canonical BrandName (`lookup = matched or brand` drives `index.prefix_for_brand`, `index.manufacturer_for_brand`, `mapping.get(lookup.lower())`; exported BrandName = the catalog canonical, e.g. "NIVEA"). AI manufacturer suggestion now only fires for *genuinely* new brands. Verified via `/brands`: NIVEA MEN & NIVEA FOR MEN → in_catalog, prefix NIV, mfr Beiersdorf Inc., export "NIVEA"; a never-seen brand stays new (no false match); "Parker Labs" resolves to the Parker catalog entry. Tradeoff: conservative but not perfect — over-merges are possible; every field stays editable per card and the "New brand / In catalog" badge shows the decision. Backend-only (card reflects the server's resolution). (Restart to apply.)
- **Create SKUs: auto-generated new-brand prefix is now always 3 chars (was appending a uniqueness digit → 4 chars)** (`services/create_po.py` `_auto_prefix`). User saw NEW brands get `NIV2` / `TRE2`. Cause: `_auto_prefix` took the first 3 alnum (`NIV`), and if that base was already a prefix of another brand it appended a digit (`NIV2`) to force uniqueness. But **prefix uniqueness is not a real constraint** — verified on the live catalog that **363 of 1315 brand prefixes are shared by ≥2 brands** (`PRL`→64 brands, `P&G`→38, `MMM`/3M→32, `LRL`/L'Oréal→35; `BAA` = Bad Air + Banana Boat) because a main SKU is unique on **prefix + identifier**, not prefix alone. And `NIV`=Nivea / `TRE`=Tresemme, so NIVEA MEN / TRESemmé sharing them is correct (same family). Fix: `_auto_prefix` now just returns the 3-char base (`re.sub(...).upper()[:3] or "SKU"`) — no digit suffix; the `taken` arg is kept for call-site compatibility but ignored. Verified via `/brands`: NIVEA MEN → `NIV` (len 3), a never-seen brand → `SOM` (len 3). Backend-only (the card shows the server's prefix; manual edits still allow up to 8 chars). (Restart to apply.)
- **Create SKUs: multiple input rows of the SAME product (shared main) no longer produce duplicate mains, colliding shadow SKUs, or a single+kit pair for one ASIN** (`services/create_po.py`, `static/create_po.js`). User's export was "messed up": Item ID `5134-01` on 2 rows (2 ASINs) + `6534-01` on 2 rows made `build_files` build a full family PER ROW → the main `GOJ5134-01` emitted twice, two ASINs both named `GOJ5134-01-FBA` (collision — `next_free_shadow` only checked the static catalog, not sibling batch rows), and a pack-2 row emitted BOTH a single shadow and the `_QY2` kit shadow pointing at the same ASIN. **Confirmed with the user** the intended model: one main per product + one child per ASIN (unique climbing names), and a multipack ASIN → kit shadow ONLY. Fixes: (1) `derive_rows` is now **batch-aware** — a `used_skus` set seeded from `index.all_skus`, and a `_claim(base, sfx)` helper that climbs `-FBA`/`-FBA2`/`-FBA3` reserving each name so sibling rows can't collide; the main is reserved too. (2) Each row yields **exactly one child per channel**: `pack>1` → the `_QY{n}` KIT shadow (`fba`/`fbm` ARE the kit shadows now, qty n, `is_kit=True`); else a single shadow (qty 1). Never both. (3) `build_files` **dedups mains** (`emitted_mains` set → one bulk row per unique main), emits the one child with the right qty, puts kit shadows only in `Kits.xlsx` (→ main, qty n) and single shadows only in `AMZ.xlsx` (kit rows skipped via `is_kit`). New `RowResult.is_kit`/`pack`; `build_groups` returns them; `counts` counts DISTINCT mains for "New mains". (4) Review board `childCell` shows the single child SKU (editable) + a "KIT · Pack of n" tag when `is_kit`. Verified end-to-end (4-row repro: 2×`5134-01`, 2×`6534-01`, one pack-2): before → duplicate mains + `GOJ*-FBA` collisions + single+kit for B0DC7JQS5H; after → **no duplicate ProductIDs**, `GOJ5134-01`/`GOJ6534-01` once, B0DC7JQS5H gets only `_QY2-FBA` (qty2 "Pack of 2"), the two `6534-01` singles climb to `-FBA`/`-FBA2`; Kits/AMZ files consistent; board renders one child per ASIN with the KIT tag; no console errors. `create_po.js?v=24`. (Backend restart + hard-refresh.)

**Changes this session (2026-07-16):**
- **Create SKUs review board: "Child SKU" columns replace the separate Shadow + Kit columns** (`static/create_po.js`). The board went from `… MAIN SKU | SHADOW -FBA | SHADOW -FBM | KIT | NOTES` (8 cols) to `… MAIN SKU | CHILD SKU -FBA | CHILD SKU -FBM | NOTES` (7 cols). Each **Child SKU** column now stacks the editable channel shadow on top and, when the item is a multipack (`kit_value` set), the read-only **kit shadow** beneath it with a small "KIT" tag — i.e. `-FBA` column shows `<main>-FBA` + `<main>_QY{n}-FBA`; `-FBM` likewise. The standalone KIT column (which showed the kit parent `_QY{n}` + both shadows) is removed. Refactor: `editableCell` split into `editCellInner` (the input/display/loading/dash control, no padding) + a thin wrapper; new `childCell(row, field, sfx)` composes `editCellInner` + the kit line. Display-only — the export (bulk main/shadow rows + separate kit file) is unchanged. Verified in-browser via a driven upload→generate: header reads `CHILD SKU -FBA / -FBM`, a pack-of-2 row shows the shadow + `KIT …_QY2-FBA`/`-FBM`, a pack-of-1 row shows just the shadow, no console errors. `create_po.js?v=23` (hard-refresh).
- **Pair Library: you can now DELETE incorrect entries** (`services/database.py`, `routers/pairs.py`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`). Previously the library was import-only (import overwrites, never removes) — no way to purge a wrong ASIN↔identifier pair. New `database.delete_library_pair(asin, id_type='', identifier='')`: drops a whole ASIN (all its `pair_library_ids`) or one identifier; **reverses the `verified_items` mirror** (removes any `'Pair Library Import'` verified row for that ASIN whose barcode form is no longer backed by a remaining upc/ean id) so runs stop auto-approving the bad pair; removes the `pair_library` row when the ASIN has no identifiers left; blacklist untouched (a shared UPC may still be valid for other ASINs). New endpoint `POST /pairs/library/delete` `{asin, id_type?, identifier?}` (404 if nothing matched). UI: each **Pair Library search result row now has a ✕ Remove button** (`.plib-del-btn`) → `removeLibraryPair(r)` → `showConfirm` (danger) → delete → toast + refresh stats + re-run the search. Verified end-to-end (API + real browser click-through): MPN-only pair removed, search empties; UPC pair removed with **`verified_cleared: 3`** (all barcode-form mirrors gone); missing ASIN → 404; the ✕ renders and the confirm→remove flow updates the toast/stats/table. **How to use:** Pair Manager → search the ASIN or identifier (e.g. `B00ARJFASQ` or `1530-2`) → click ✕ on the row → Confirm. `app.js?v=37`, `styles.css?v=11`. (Backend restart + hard-refresh.)
- **Create SKUs: the brand NAME is now editable on the Step-3 brand cards** (`services/create_po.py`, `routers/create_po.py`, `static/create_po.js`, `static/create_po.css`). Previously Manufacturer/Prefix/Purchaser/Sourcer were editable but the brand title was a static `<span>`. Now it's an inline editable input (`data-in="bname"`, `.cpo-bname` — looks like a heading until hover/focus). It sets `brand_overrides[<brand>].brand_name`; `resolve_brands` honors `ov.get("brand_name")` → `BrandInfo.brand_name` → the export's **BrandName column** and the review-board display (`build_groups` now returns `brand_name`; the Review board header + per-row brand cell + search use `g.brand_name || g.brand`). Scope: this changes the **exported/displayed** name only — grouping and manufacturer/prefix/purchaser/sourcer lookups stay keyed on the original detected brand string (edit those fields separately if a rename should also change them). `/brands` response now includes `brand_name`. Verified end-to-end: override "Colgate"→"Colgate-Palmolive USA" flows through `/brands`, `/generate` (group brand_name), and the exported Bulk BrandName column; grouping key stays "Colgate". (FYI on the card buttons: the top-right button is `bconfirm` — for a **new** brand it's "Confirm"/"✓ Confirmed" (acknowledge the AI-suggested manufacturer/prefix; card is amber until confirmed); for an **in-catalog** brand it shows "✎ Edit" but is the same confirmed-toggle — the fields are always editable regardless.)
- **Create SKUs: a hand-edited main SKU was dropped from the bulk export** (`services/create_po.py` `derive_rows`). User renamed a reused main (J&J78331 → COL78331); the bulk got only `COL78331-FBA`/`-FBM` and no `COL78331` main. Root cause: `main_on_sc` was `bool(existing)` — computed from the row's **UPC/MPN** lookup, not the final main string — so after the edit it was still `True` and `build_files` skipped the main (`if … and not r.main_on_sc: emit`). Fix: `main_on_sc = bool(main) and index is not None and index.sku_exists(main)` — it now reflects the FINAL (possibly edited) main SKU, matching the `check-sku` frontend re-check. So renaming a reused SKU to a new one marks it as a main to CREATE; an unchanged reused SKU stays on-SC and is (correctly) not recreated; a genuinely-new product is emitted as before. Verified end-to-end: editing J&J78331→COL78331 now yields bulk rows **`COL78331` + `COL78331-FBA` + `COL78331-FBM`** (was missing the main). The reported spurious **`_QY4` for an each was the same kit-shadow-as-main bug below** (the "main" was literally `..._QY4-FBA`); confirmed a clean each (no amz-pack column mapped) now produces `kit=None`. NOTE: a source column literally named `Pack`/`Pack Qty`/`amz pack` still auto-maps to the amz-pack field (`guessField`, `create_po.js`) → a kit; set that field to "— none —" if it isn't the Amazon multipack.
- **Create SKUs: kit-shadow SKUs no longer masquerade as MAINs (a main showed as `..._QY4-FBA`)** (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`). User saw a Main SKU rendered as `J&J78331_QY4-FBA`. Root cause: in the Azure `sku_data_extended_view`, **kit-shadow rows carry an EMPTY `ShadowOf`** (verified live for UPC 035000783318: `J&J78331_QY4-FBA` and `J&J78331_QY2-FBA` both have `ShadowOf=''`, while the plain shadow `J&J78331-FBA` has `ShadowOf='J&J78331'`). `CatalogIndex._mains()` filtered on `ShadowOf` alone, so those kit shadows passed as "mains"; `main_by_upc()` returned all three and `_main_exists()[0]` happened to be the `_QY4-FBA` one → used as the main. Fix: new module-level `_is_shadow_sku(pid)` (regex `_SHADOW_SKU_RE = _QY\d+|-FB[AM]`) and `_mains()` now excludes any row whose ProductID carries a shadow/kit marker in addition to the ShadowOf check. Verified end-to-end through the running server: the Colgate row now resolves `MAIN='J&J78331'` (the real main), `main_on_sc=True`, note "Already on SellerCloud — will not recreate". This ALSO prevents a duplicate-main bug (before, if the reuse had returned empty it would have minted a brand-new `COL78331` main duplicating the existing `J&J78331`). `main_by_mpn()` gets the same protection (both call `_mains`).
- **Create SKUs: editing a SKU on the review board re-checks SellerCloud + reloads the note** (`routers/create_po.py`, `static/create_po.js`, `static/create_po.css`, `static/index.html`). New endpoint `POST /create-po/check-sku` `{token, sku, field}` → `{on_sc, note}` — looks the **SKU string itself** up in the session's `CatalogIndex` (`index.sku_exists(sku)`), distinct from the row's UPC/MPN-based `main_on_sc`. Frontend: hand-editing a `main`/`fba`/`fbm` cell and clicking out (blur) or pressing Enter now runs `commitCell(key)` → shows a quick spinner on that cell (`.cpo-spin` + `.cpo-checking`, low-opacity `checking…` state; ~340ms min so it doesn't just flash) → calls check-sku → updates that cell's `✓ on SC` badge and, for the main, the row note ("Already on SellerCloud — will not recreate" when found, else reverts to the row's `_baseNote`: new-brand / `amz pack N → kit` / blank). Escape cancels without a lookup. Helpers: `findRow`, `baseNote`; state `skuChecking{}`; `_baseNote` captured per row at generate. Verified: check-sku returns on_sc True for `J&J78331`/`J&J78331_QY4-FBA`, False for made-up `COL78331`; Create SKUs view mounts with no console errors (`create_po.js?v=21`, `create_po.css?v=9`). **Backend change → restart; frontend → hard-refresh.**
- **Create SKUs brand prefix is now brand-letter-aligned, not the raw mode** (`services/sellercloud/catalog_index.py`). The per-brand SKU prefix was already the *most-used* leading-letter run among the brand's main SKUs (`_mode` = `Counter.most_common(1)`), NOT the first one used — but the outright mode is often a **parent-company code**, not the brand's own code, because many SKUs are prefixed by owner. Verified on LIVE Azure (176,583 rows): Colgate mains were 55% **J&J** (parent) vs 27% COL; Dove mains were 82% **ULV** (Unilever) vs 14% DOV. So the Step-2 card showed Colgate→J&J, Dove→ULV. New `_choose_brand_prefix(brand_key, counter)` (replaces the `_mode` call for `brand_prefix` only; `manuf_prefix` still uses `_mode`): computes the brand's own signature via `_brand_sig` (leading letters+`&` of the BrandName, upper — "COLGATE"→"COLGATE", "PARKER LABORATORIES"→"PARKER", "3M"→"" since it starts with a digit), then picks the **most-used prefix that lines up with that signature** (`len>=2` and `sig.startswith(p) or p.startswith(sig)`; ties → shortest, i.e. closest to the base code). Falls back to the overall most-used prefix when nothing lines up. Verified against live data: **Colgate J&J→COL, Dove ULV→DOV** (fixed); **3M→MMM, Parker→PRK, Clorox→CLO, Kyocera→KYO** all unchanged (correct — brand name shares no leading letters with the prefix, so the most-used fallback stands). Single point of change — both the Azure path (`azure_sql.catalog_index()`) and the snapshot path (`catalog_index.local_index()`) build through `build_from_rows`, so both are fixed. `brand_prefix_conf` (now a lower share for a re-picked brand, e.g. Colgate 27%) is informational only — used solely by the CLI debug print, nothing gates on it, and `resolve_brands` uses `index.prefix_for_brand()` directly with no confidence threshold. **Requires a server restart** (Python module reload) for the running app to pick it up; the index is rebuilt from cached rows on restart. Prefix is still editable per-brand on the Step-2 card.

**Changes this session (2026-07-15b):**
- **Pair Library Lookup & Export is now read-only — not-in-DB rows go to Not Found** (`routers/pairs.py`, `static/app.js`). Previously a lookup row that carried an ASIN but wasn't in the library was silently **auto-saved** and reported as "Added" (never in Not Found). Now `library_lookup_export` does a pure lookup: any row whose identifier isn't matched in the library → **Not Found** sheet (reason notes if an ASIN was supplied), nothing is written to the library. Use the Import feature to add pairs. `X-Plib-Added` stays (always 0); the UI toast/result text dropped the "added" wording. Verified: a not-in-DB UPC + fake ASIN → matched 0 / added 0 / not-found 1, library unchanged.
- **Pair Library import gained an `amz pack` column** (`services/database.py`, `routers/pairs.py`, `static/index.html`). New `pair_library.amz_pack TEXT` column (+ additive `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` migration); `upsert_library_pair(..., amz_pack="")` stores it (per ASIN); `pair_library_rows` returns it. Import: `_HEADER_MAP` recognises `amz pack`/`amzpack`/`pack`/`pack qty`/`amazon pack` → `amz_pack`; `_IMPORT_HEADERS` + the download template + Notes + `_EXPORT_HEADERS`/`_library_row_cells` (round-trips) all include it; the import route passes `amz_pack=_get(...)`. UI template-hint text updated. Verified: header maps, value round-trips upsert→rows→export.
- **Create SKUs wizard restructured to Upload → Map → Configure → Review, + sheet/header picker, export history, and a Home button** (`static/create_po.js`, `routers/create_po.py`, `services/sku_exports.py`, `services/file_parser.py` reused). New stepper: **1 Upload / 2 Map columns / 3 Configure / 4 Review & Export**; "Generate" is no longer its own step — it's a **loading overlay** (`S.generating`) between Configure and Review. Stage split: `stepUpload()` (step 1) = dropzone OR uploaded-file card + **sheet dropdown + header-row `<input>`** (reparse via `POST /create-po/reparse`) + a first-3-rows preview + **Export History** card (`historyCard()` from `GET /create-po/history`); `stepMap()` (step 2) = file preview + `previewMap()` field mapping + Back/next; `stepConfigure()` (step 3, was step 2) = batch settings + brand roster. Nav actions: `go2` (Upload→Map, no gate), `go3` (Map→Configure, gated by `requiredMissing()`), `back` = `stage-1`, `home` = `goHome()` (full reset to a fresh step 1 + `loadHistory()`). **Backend:** `/preview` now stores the raw bytes + returns `sheets`/`active_sheet`/`header_row`; `_parse_with_header(filename,data,sheet,header_row)` (via `parse_raw_rows`) treats the 1-based header row as the header; `/reparse` re-reads with a chosen sheet/header; `/history` → `sku_exports.list_recent()`. History is warmed on `mount()` and refreshed after each export. Verified end-to-end (header-row 2 reparse, all 4 steps, generate→Review, Home returns to upload with the history list showing the prior export). Note: bump `create_po.js` cache version on any change (now `?v=20`).

**Changes this session (2026-07-15):**
- **Azure SQL live catalog is CONNECTED and wired into Create SKUs as the read-only reference source** (`services/azure_sql.py` [new], `routers/create_po.py`, `main.py`, `requirements.txt`). After the user pasted a valid secret for the **granted** app `194d38fb-1186-4491-a149-4dd0dce60b7c`, the pure-Python pytds + AAD recipe works end-to-end: token ~1.9s, connect ~1.5s, `[analytics].[sku_data_extended_view]` → **176,509 rows** (live SellerCloud mirror). New `services/azure_sql.py`: `_connect()` (sets `urllib3 HAS_IPV6=False`, `ClientSecretCredential`, `pytds.connect(..., access_token_callable, cafile=certifi.where(), validate_host=False)`), `fetch_rows()` (SELECTs the 13 needed cols, **30-min in-memory cache**, ~32s first pull), `catalog_index()` (→ `build_from_rows`, ~1s), `brand_map()` (brand→{manufacturer,purchaser,sourcer}, mode per brand), `catalog_source()`, `is_configured()`. `routers/create_po.py` new `_catalog_source()` prefers Azure and **falls back to the local snapshot (`ci.local_index()`) + `brand_map.get_map()`** on any failure; `/brands` + `/generate` use it (index+mapping cached in the session). `main.py` lifespan warms the cache in a daemon thread on startup so the first run isn't blocked by the pull. **Scope (user): PULL/REFERENCE ONLY — we do NOT create SKUs on SellerCloud; export stays files-only.** Verified live in the app: Smith & Nephew roster showed purchaser **Robinm** (the Azure value; the static snapshot gave ArnurT) → confirms the live source is active. This supersedes the static `sc_snapshot.json` + `brand_mappings` table as the primary source (they remain the offline fallback). Deps added: python-tds, pyopenssl, certifi, azure-identity. NEVER echo `AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET`.
